So P2 and P3 also run once per device, with P5's entry drops, outside any page: not inside `Store::open` (P3 rewrites speaker names, and only the runtime runs `names_before` and `names_changed_tx`, so a summary that used the name is marked, 7A.9), but in the runtime (`earshot_runtime::sync::removal_repair`), called:
    - *in the app* on the `earshot-sync-start` thread, unconditionally, after `copy_ignores_without_vault` and before `start_if_on` (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs:304-310`), whether sync is on or off;
    - *in the worker* after `open_services` and before `Worker::open(services, config)` (`crates/earshot-worker/src/main.rs:139-141`), which takes `services` (review 9 A4).
    One transaction through `services.guarded_write(|w| removal_repair_in(w))` (a one-line wrapper over a guarded root, 16.3; the services store lock is held throughout) re-reads `removal-repair` and does nothing if it is there, reads `names_before`, runs P2 then P3 with P5's entry drops, runs `names_changed_tx`, and writes the device record `removal-repair` (`DeviceRecord::RemovalRepair`, `version u32 LE | JSON` counts only); its presence means done. Racing a tick is safe: both take the store lock, so one runs after the other, and P2 and P3 are idempotent (the second finds nothing left, 7A.8). Best effort, as the sweep: an error rolls back, the app starts, the next start tries again; a damaged row never holds it, since its reads are section 16's (D1) and a row that does not read is skipped and counted. When it erased anything (`Applied::erased`-style count: names cleared, speaker names rewritten, entries dropped) it runs `Store::flush_erasures` after its commit (7A.4). It reports what it changed as sync does: the repair returns the meeting and person ids it wrote, and the app sends them as one `SyncEvent { meetings, people }` through the same emitter the sync listener uses (`sync_settings.rs`); a window already listening re-reads them, and a window that mounts after the commit reads fresh state. A window that read before the commit but started listening after the event shows the old names until its next read of that meeting or the People page (a start-up window only; accepted, and phase 2 checks the window's subscribe-then-read order before relying on more). As built (T14.49f) the window subscribes, then reads: its first list read waits for the subscription, so this case does not arise for the meeting list (7A.12b). The worker shows nothing and drops them. Its writes carry this device's stamp and wait in the outbox like any edit, so with sync off they go when sync is turned on, and with sync on the first page's repairs then find nothing. Its reads and writes follow D1 (section 16). The CLI does not run it (it starts no sync and shows no Speakers panel; the app's next start does).
  - Why not stamp the tombstone before the rows in `remove_person` instead: P1 and C1 stamp tombstones again, after the rows, whenever they defend them, so the relay's order can never be relied on; and section 15's rule (a write that removes what a meeting shows is stamped after the rows that replace it) stays as it is.
- **F1. A folder tombstone is final, both ways**, exactly as P1 (content: no name; re-stamps counted in `tombstones_restamped`). On the deleting computer the folder's meetings were already moved out or deleted (`folders::delete_with_meetings`).
- **F2. Nothing else changes for folders.** A meeting whose `folder_id` names a tombstoned folder still reads as in no folder (`LiveFolders::resolve`) and its row is not rewritten. Two live folders with one name both stay (rule 3).
- **C1. A tombstone stamped again leaves in the same tick** (client only, `engine.rs` `tick_into`; A3). A tick pushes before it pulls (6.2), so today a tombstone that a pull stamps again waits in the outbox for the next tick.
  - *The count.* A new `Applied::tombstones_restamped` counts exactly the tombstones a page stamped again and kept queued: the meeting, voiceprint and voice sample arms of `apply_remote_with` (the three places that increment `deletions_kept` after a `touch` today), and P1 and F1 in both cases. It is not `deletions_kept`, which also counts a live child of a deleted meeting that is skipped (no stamp, nothing to send). With it, `Applied::stamped_here` lists the ids of every row the page wrote with this device's stamp: those tombstones plus what P2, P3 and rule 3a and the voice rules wrote on the same page.
  - *The trigger.* The tick adds the counts and ids of every page it applied: the start-of-tick `retry_held` page, the pull's pages and the `parent` retry. When the count is above 0, after the `parent` retry and whatever the pull's result, the tick pushes once more.
  - *What it sends* (N1). There is no second send path. C1 is `engine.rs` `push` itself, given an optional id set (`stamped_here`), which it passes to the one outbox read: `Store::outbox_page` gains an optional restriction to those ids, applied in the same query after the same filters. So the extra push inherits every filter a push has: opt-in kinds (`SyncKinds::syncs`: with voiceprint sync off, the default, no voiceprint or voice sample row goes out, whatever P1, rule 3a or `forget_identity_biometrics` wrote), meetings that are recording (left out when the page is read and again before sealing), unreadable rows (`unsendable`), the `ahead` filter and sealing refusals (`refused`). A row of the set that is no longer queued (already sent) is simply not found; one written again since is sent as it now is. Pages of `PUSH_PAGE`, at most `MAX_PUSH_PAGES`, as any push, so a long outbox does not hold these rows back; the rest of the queue waits for the next tick. Each answer is checked like any push (6.2), and sent rows leave the outbox as any push.
  - *The id set* (advisory, fifth review). `stamped_here` can be large (P3 over an identity with many speaker rows), past SQLite's bound-parameter limit for an `IN (...)` list. `outbox_page` takes the set through a temporary table (`temp.push_only(id BLOB PRIMARY KEY)`, filled and joined in the read's own transaction, dropped after it), never a bound list, so the page keeps the one query and its oldest-stamp-first order.
  - *The tick's counts* (advisory, fifth review). Today `push` assigns `report.unsendable`, `report.refused` and `report.clock_ahead` at its end (`engine.rs`, the three assignments after the page loop), so a second push in one tick would overwrite the first push's counts with the extra push's smaller ones and the Sync page would understate what is stuck. Phase 2 makes `push` fold into the report instead: `unsendable` the larger of the two (it is already a maximum over pages), `refused` and `clock_ahead` the size of the union of the two pushes' id sets (the report keeps the sets for the tick). Tested by a tick whose first push has refused and too-far-ahead rows and whose extra push has none.
  - *Parents* (advisory 3). Because the extra push sends only its set, a speaker row P3 rewrote can reach the relay before its meeting's row when that meeting row is still queued here (created here and not yet sent, or held back by a filter). A receiver then holds the speaker row `parent` and applies it when the meeting row arrives (the `parent` retry, 5.3), as for any row that arrives before its parent today; nothing is lost.
  - *Bounds.* At most one extra push per tick, never repeated from inside it: the extra push pulls nothing, so it cannot cause a new re-stamp, and anything stamped later waits for the next tick. No extra push when the tick's first push was refused for a far stamp (`clock_refused`: it would be refused again), when the tick ended early on a vault reset, or when the client is stopping; rows the `ahead` filter holds back are not sent by it either. A tick with no tombstone stamped again pushes once, as today. P4's re-stamped live `is_me` copy and rule 4a's re-stamped edit are not tombstones and keep waiting for the next tick. No wire field, no relay change, no format change.
- **D1. Damaged rows: section 16 applies** (rewritten after review 10, once the owner moved T14.57 ahead of this task, 2026-11-27; review 11's advisories fixed). Section 16 (T14.57, spec-reviewed and passed) is built first, and 7A relies on it instead of special cases of its own:
  - *Its paths are 16's paths* (16.2 R1). Everything 7A adds inside `apply_remote` (P1, P2, P3, P4, F1, P5's write in the page loop), the widened `names_before` (7A.9), the one-off sweep in `Store::open` and the one-off removal repair are guarded roots under R1 to R4: free functions taking a `GuardedTx` or `GuardedRead` (store side) or `&mut ServicesWrite` (the repair, as a one-line `services.guarded_write(..)` wrapper), reading only through `split_rows`, lenient `Col` readers and named lenient aggregates, writing a damaged row only through 16.4's named writers under the written-columns rule. Their names join 16.8's root list when built, and each new SQL statement is a named constant in `guarded.rs` with its owner in `SQL_OWNERS`. A row that does not read is skipped and counted (`Applied::damaged_skipped`, 16.7), never fails the page, the open or the repair.
  - *What 7A's readers read.* The sweep (review 11, advisories 2 and 3): a root `sweep_identities(g: &mut GuardedTx)` opened by section 16's guarded IMMEDIATE constructor (`Store::guarded_immediate`, 16.3), with its own SQL constant in `guarded.rs`, `SWEEP_SPEAKERS_SQL` (per meeting: `rowid`, `id`, `deleted`, each a `Col`), owned by `sweep_identities` in `SQL_OWNERS`; not `speaker_links`, which reads more than the sweep may. An unreadable `deleted` drops the entry, as P5 says. An entry that matches no readable row, in a meeting that holds a row whose `id` does not read, counts as missing and is dropped (the owner's 7A.12 point 7: the sweep drops what it cannot show to be a live row's entry; a row whose `id` does not read is not shown). This is the sweep's rule; `forget_identity`'s rule for the same kind of entry is section 16.5 item 20 (dropped in a user-started erasure, kept under a pulled tombstone's side effect when it could be the user's). P2's inputs: `person_links` (`id`, `deleted`, `merged_into`, `is_me`), then `chain_end`. A live person merged into a removed person whose own `is_me` does not read is skipped by P2 (not tombstoned, its biometrics not forgotten) and its rowid counted in `damaged_skipped`, as Remove refuses with `PersonUnreadable` for such a member (16.5 item 18) (review 11, advisory 7). The widened `names_before` set: `speaker_links` joined to `person_links`, then 16's per-row skips (16.5 items 12 and 14), so a damaged speaker or summary row costs only its own mark; the per-meeting skip and `TickReport.names_skipped` of the ninth review are replaced by these. The removal repair: the same reads as P2 and P3. `forget_identity`'s `linked_before` test reads only entries.
  - *P3's rewrite* is 16's `unlink_speakers_tx` (16.5 item 21): a readable row rewritten as P3 says; a damaged row whose `person_id` reads as a removed person rewritten by `unlink_speaker_by_rowid` (`person_id = NULL`, `ignored = 0`, the first free "Speaker N" only when `display_name` and `deleted` both read and `deleted` is `No`, `''` when `deleted` reads `Yes`, this device's stamp); a damaged row whose `person_id` or `meeting_id` does not read is not written (only its entry is dropped, P5, `from` unreadable). Names that do not read count as taken by no row. *Whether a rewritten damaged row stays unsendable* (review 10 advisory 4): it stays unreadable, so unsendable (T12.77b7), whenever any column the writer does not set is damaged; it becomes readable and sendable only when its only damage was `ignored`, or the name of a tombstoned row, which the written-columns rule sets to the constant the readable path writes, so what is sent is the row the readable path would send.
  - *P3 and `ignored`* (review 10 advisory 3): P3 sets `ignored = 0` on every row it unlinks, readable or damaged, whatever `ignored` read as, because the readable path (`Store::remove_person`, T12.93) clears it on every unlink; the D1 text and test 7A.11 ("a damaged speaker row under a pulled tombstone") both assert `ignored = 0` after the rewrite.
  - *One chain walk, extended* (review 9 A1). `relinked`'s `removed(p)`, rule 3a and P2 use 16's `chain_end` (16.3: `Removed | Live | Unknown` from each step's `deleted` and `merged_into`, today's `ends_deleted` answer on readable rows). 7A extends it with P4's outcome: at a tombstoned step it also reads `is_me`; `true` is `MeTombstone`, never a removal (P4); `false` is `Removed`; unreadable is `Unknown`, so a tombstoned person who might be the user is never erased by guess (7A.12 point 3), which changes rule 3a's answer only for `is_me` tombstones, as P4 intends. `Unknown` changes nothing anywhere: no erasure, no unlink, no P2 tombstone; the entry keeps `p` in `linked_before` (`relinked` case 3), so it still goes when a readable tombstone of `p` arrives.
  - *Counted `Unknown`* (review 10 advisory 1, narrowed by review 11 advisory 4): only an `Unknown` caused by an unreadable step counts, and it puts that step's `(person, rowid)` into `damaged_skipped` (16.7), once per page; `Applied::chains_unknown` is the number of such steps. A loop or a person not known here (not yet pulled) is `Unknown` too but counts nothing, because nothing is damaged. *Cost of `relinked` case 3 when `to` is not yet known*: the entry keeps `from` in `linked_before`, so if `from` is removed before the row is next written, the entry is dropped although the voice had been said to be `to`'s (errs towards erasing; it affects one centroid, never the row or its link).
  - *`row_relinked_tx` has two entry points* (review 11, advisory 6) over the one pure `relinked` and one record read and write (`speaker-identities/<meeting>`, a device-local record, no rows): `row_relinked_guarded(g: &mut GuardedTx, ..)` for the page loop and P3 (guarded roots), and `row_relinked_tx(tx: &mut StoreTx, ..)` for `edit::set_row_person`, a user edit that is not on section 16's paths (16.2 R1) and keeps the ordinary traits (16.3). Chosen over relaxing 16.3: the rule stays one function, and the user edit, which already read the row whole and refuses a damaged one as edits do, gains no guarded constructor it does not need.
  - *No special cases of 7A's own.* Every damaged-row arm that earlier drafts gave 7A (P3's by-rowid write, the sweep's three-way read, the per-meeting skip, `chain_end` on direct reads, the "Known, left to T14.57" list of existing readers) is now section 16's rule or reader; 7A adds no damaged-row rule beyond `MeTombstone`'s `is_me` read above.

Each of P2 and P3 writes content that depends only on synced rows, so every device writes the same row, bar the "Speaker N" a device picks while it holds only part of a meeting's rows: whole-row last writer wins then keeps one copy, and two rows of one meeting may show the same "Speaker N" until one is renamed (accepted, as in rule 4c). Run by kind (T14.50): P2 for a page that wrote a person row (a re-stamp counts); P3 for one that wrote a person or speaker row, or after P2 or 4c wrote; all of them on a client's first page (`apply_remote_every_repair`). A client's first tick that applies no page at all (the pull is empty: today `apply` returns at once for an empty page, `engine.rs`) still runs every repair once, with no rows (advisory 5), so rows an earlier build left (a speaker row linked to a person tombstoned here, a live person merged into one) are mended at the first start of this build even when nothing new arrives. P5 is not a repair: it runs at each write it names, and its one-off sweep runs once per device inside `Store::open`.

### 7A.3 Writers that refuse a removed person

Read in the code today; phase 2 checks each again and pins the set with a scan test.

- **Links and saved voices.** `edit::link_in` (behind `link_speaker` and `save_voice_with`) takes an existing person only through `live_person`, which uses core `resolve_person` (`crates/earshot-core/src/voiceprint/mod.rs`): a chain that ends at a deleted or missing person, or loops, is `None`, so `EditError::PersonNotFound`. `save_voice_with` resolves the row's earlier person the same way.
- **Merges and renames.** Core `merge_persons` refuses unless both are live; `edit::merge_into_me` and `edit::rename_person` refuse a deleted or merged-away person (`PersonNotFound`).
- **`set_row_person`** writes whatever person its caller passes; every caller has resolved it first. Phase 2 makes it resolve the person inside its transaction itself and refuse a removed one, so no later caller can link to one, and it calls P5's helper.
- **The final pass.** Naming by voiceprint matches only live prints (`live_prints`, `admissible`) inside the write transaction, and after any page rule 3a leaves no live print of a tombstoned person, so naming cannot pick one. A carried edited row keeps its stored `person_id` (`post/mod.rs`, the `user_edited` branch) and an identity entry carries `given_person`: phase 2 resolves both inside the write transaction, writes a row whose person does not resolve as P3 would ("Speaker N", unlinked, with P5's `relinked` applied to its in-memory entry, never the helper) and treats an unresolvable `given_person` as none. An `is_me` tombstone resolves as the user for these checks (P4).
- **Sync rules.** Rule 1 writes live "Me" rows only; rule 4c revives a speaker row with its stored person, and P3 runs after it; a pulled speaker row is P3's case above.

### 7A.4 Erasing the cleared bytes

Today the client checkpoints after a page only when `Applied::biometrics_forgotten > 0` (`crates/earshot-runtime/src/sync/engine.rs`, the `if applied.biometrics_forgotten > 0` before `flush_erasures`), which counts voiceprints and voice samples only, and `forget_identity_biometrics` drops the count of speaker identity entries `forget_identity` returns. So a removal of a person with no saved voice (a name, speaker names and centroids) leaves the old bytes in the WAL until some later checkpoint. Phase 2 adds `Applied::erased`: person and folder names cleared (a tombstone written over a named live copy by P1 or F1, P2), speaker names P3 rewrote, identity entries dropped (the count `forget_identity` returns, carried out of `forget_identity_biometrics`, and P5's drops), plus `biometrics_forgotten`. The client's gate changes from `biometrics_forgotten > 0` to `erased > 0` (A6), and runs `Store::flush_erasures` then; a `Busy` means the erasure is committed and leaves at the next checkpoint, as today. `Store::remove_person` already checkpoints always. Outside `apply_remote`: a user edit whose `row_relinked_tx` dropped an entry checkpoints after its commit (P5, "Writers that change one row"), and P5's sweep checkpoints with retries inside `Store::open` when it dropped anything (P5, "The one-off sweep"), and the one-off removal repair runs `Store::flush_erasures` after its commit whenever it erased anything (P5, "The one-off removal repair").

### 7A.5 Names after a removal

Once a device has applied the page that brings or keeps a removal, the fields these rules own hold no trace of the removed identity:

- Person rows: tombstones with no name (P1, P2). Folder rows: no name (F1).
- Speaker rows once linked to the identity: "Speaker N", or no name on a tombstoned row, and no link (P3), on every device.
- Speaker identities (`speaker-identities/<meeting>`, centroid and `given_name`): every entry of the identity dropped, whatever order the rows came in (P5), except an orphan left on a live unlinked row while this device ran 0.9.0 (P5's accepted limit).
- The relay: one body per row key: the tombstone, once it reached the relay after the last live write to P or a holder that can send has synced since (7A.6); B's live copy in the limit's case (7A.1).

Not rewritten, as on the removing computer, and so outside the pin (A4): summary text and the bodies of summary tombstones (section 15 and the summary history), meeting titles filled from a summary, segment text (a word choice or a typed correction can hold the name), `word-choices/<meeting>` Changed tokens, Dictionary rows (`dictionary_word`) and the stored list, live summary records (`live-summaries/<meeting>`), `summary-names/<meeting>` (it records the name against a summary only when that summary's text already uses it, `summary_names::record_tx`, `uses_name`), exported notes, and sealed bodies in `sync_pending` (opaque until applied, and applied through these same rules). The words lists read live persons only (`word_choices::named_persons`), so the name leaves the word choices offered and the summary's people at once.

The pin (7A.11) checks exactly the fields this section owns, on both computers after the removal syncs: every person row's and folder row's name, every speaker row's name and `person_id`, and every `speaker-identities` entry (`given_name`, `given_person`, `linked_before`, and that no entry of the identity's rows is left). None may hold the removed name or the identity's ids, with the positive control that the same check finds them before. Nothing else is scanned, so the pin is not read as a promise about the places listed above.

### 7A.6 Walks

A removes person P (identity: P and those merged into P as A knows them; their voices erased; A's speaker rows of that identity back to "Speaker N"). The relay (`Db::push`) stores a pushed row only when its stamp is strictly later than the one it holds for the key, overwriting the held body; otherwise the row is answered `superseded`, and the client (`engine.rs` `send`) marks every row sent, superseded ones included, so a superseded row leaves the outbox. A tick pushes before it pulls (6.2); a tombstone a pull stamps again goes out in the same tick through C1's extra push.

**The limit's case** (7A.1). A pushes the tombstone and is never used again; no other device has pulled it. B, offline, renamed P (stamped after the removal) and saved a new voice for P. B's push: B's copy of P is later, so it is stored over A's tombstone; B's new voiceprint and sample are new keys and are stored. B's pull: A's voiceprint and sample tombstones (final, so A's old voices are gone on B too) and A's rewritten speaker rows (kept where they are the later write; each one that unlinks a row from P adds P to that row's `linked_before`, P5), but no row for P. B ends with P live, B's name and B's new voice. A third computer C that joins now ends the same. Nothing changes this until a holder that can send syncs again.

**When a holder syncs again.** A (or a device C that pulled the tombstone before B's push) pulls B's copy of P: P1's first case keeps the tombstone, stamps it again above B's copy and keeps it queued (`tombstones_restamped`). The same pull brings B's new print and sample: rule 3a erases them (their person is tombstoned here) and P3 unlinks B's speaker rows linked to P, dropping their entries here (P5). The extra push of the same tick (C1) sends exactly these rows (each stamp is strictly later, so all are stored), so one tick of the holder is enough. B's next pull: P1's second case writes the tombstone as pulled (it is the later copy); `forget_identity_biometrics` drops every entry of P's identity on B, both those whose rows are still linked to P and those A's rewritten rows unlinked earlier (`linked_before`, P5); the erased print and sample replace B's live ones, and P3 unlinks what is left. Every device that pulls from then on ends removed.

**Speaker identities across pages and ticks** (B1). P has more speaker rows than one pull page, and on B one of them, S in meeting M, was linked to P by B's user (B's entry for S has `given_person` `None` and B's centroid of P's voice). A's removal stamped the rewritten rows, S's among them, before the tombstone, so the relay numbers them first.
- *S's row in an earlier page or tick than the tombstone.* Page 1 writes S unlinked: P is live on B, so P5 adds P to S's entry's `linked_before`. The tick may end here, and the tombstone may come days later or be held `clock`: the record is on disk. When the tombstone applies, `forget_identity` finds S's entry through `linked_before` and drops it.
- *The tombstone in the same page as S's row, or an earlier one.* If S's row comes first in the page, as above; if the tombstone comes first, `forget_identity` finds S's entry through its current link, and S's row then writes over a row with no entry.
- *S's row after the tombstone* (the tombstone was stamped again by a holder after A's rows, so the relay now numbers it later): the tombstone drops S's entry through the current link; S's unlinked row then changes nothing.
- *On the removing computer.* B links a row R to P after the removal and before it hears of it. A pulls R linked to P (P tombstoned on A): the write adds nothing (it links to P, not away), then P3 unlinks R, and P5 drops A's entry for R at that write, because P is tombstoned on A. On B the entry for R goes when the tombstone arrives, through the current link or, if A's unlinked R comes first, through `linked_before`.
- *A re-transcription in between* (N2). P's print tombstones reach B first while P is still live there; B re-transcribes M; a voice the earlier pass named P by voiceprint no longer matches and the pass writes it "Speaker N". The pass builds that voice's new entry with `relinked(old entry, P, None)`, so it holds P in `linked_before`, and writes the record whole with it. When P's tombstone arrives, `forget_identity` drops the entry. Had P been tombstoned before the pass, `relinked` would have returned `None` and the pass would not have written the entry.
In every order the entry is dropped once the device has both the unlink and the tombstone, and P5 keeps the information across page and tick boundaries.

**Both arrival orders.** For any write B made to P before it heard of the removal:

| Who reaches the relay first | What happens |
|---|---|
| A's tombstone | B's copy stamped before the removal: answered `superseded`, leaves B's outbox; B pulls the tombstone and P1 writes it. B's copy stamped after: stored over the tombstone; then as "When a holder syncs again", or the limit if no holder that can send does. |
| B's copy | A's tombstone stamped after B's copy: stored; B pulls it and P1 writes it. Stamped before: answered `superseded` and leaves A's outbox; A's pull in the same tick brings B's copy, P1 keeps the tombstone stamped above it and queued, and the extra push of the same tick (C1) stores it; B then pulls it. What remains of the window: the tick ends after the pull's page committed and before the extra push's answer (the app quits, the computer loses power or the network, the client is stopping, the first push was refused for a far stamp, or the extra push fails), and A is then never used again. The re-stamped tombstone then stays in A's outbox and no other device holds it: the removal has not synced, like any change on a computer that is switched off. |

**Where finality holds.** From the moment a device has applied the tombstone, that device never shows P live again by its own doing (P1 both ways, and its writers refuse, 7A.3 and 7A.8), and every centroid of P's identity it holds is gone (P5). Every device that pulls the tombstone before a later live copy has reached the relay ends removed and stays removed, and is itself a holder from then on. A later live copy that reaches the relay first is undone by the first completed tick of any holder that can send (its pull and C1's extra push); until then, and for good if no such holder ever syncs again, devices that never pulled the tombstone show P. That last case is the limit (7A.1).

| B does, before it hears of the removal | Every device ends with (once a holder has synced, where the order needs it) |
|---|---|
| Renames P | P a tombstone, no name. B's voices of P go (5.4) and B's speaker rows linked to P are unlinked, by A's rewritten rows where A had them and by P3 where A did not or B's copy was later; their entries go either way (P5). |
| Links a new voice to P (and saves it) | The speaker row reaches A naming a tombstoned person: P3 unlinks it there and P5 drops A's entry for it, and on B once the tombstone applies. The saved sample is erased by rule 3a on both. |
| "Not P" on a voice | That row is unlinked on both by either write (B's keeps it an ignored "Speaker N" if later, A's a plain one). The entry keeps P in `linked_before` and is dropped on B when the tombstone applies (P5's cost: errs towards erasing). |
| Relinks a voice from P to Q (review 7 advisory 6) | *A's unlink is the later write* (B relinked before A removed P): last writer wins keeps A's unlinked "Speaker N" on every device, so B's link to Q is lost with no notice (7A.12 point 2). On B, the pulled row adds Q to the entry's `linked_before` (`relinked` case 3; case 2 had cleared P when B linked to Q); P's tombstone drops the entry only if the pass had named the voice P (`given_person`), and a voice B saved for Q stays Q's (its sample is Q's). *B's link is the later write*: the row stays linked to Q everywhere; Q is not part of P's identity, so P3 leaves it and B keeps its entry. On A the entry went with the removal, so Save of that voice on A answers `NoStoredVoice` until a re-transcription there gives it a new one. |
| Merges another person X into P | X arrives live with `merged_into = P`: P2 tombstones X, its voices go (3a, 5.4) and P3 unlinks X's speaker rows. The removal takes X, as it would have on A had A known. |
| Merges P into another person Q | B's copy of P (live, `merged_into = Q`) loses to the tombstone on every device. Q is not part of P's identity and stays as it is, with its own name and voices; the voices that were P's are erased and P's speaker rows are unlinked by P3. The same when Q is the user (`merge_into_me`): Q is live, so P4 does not come into it. |
| Removes P too | Two tombstones: plain last writer wins, identical content; the speaker rows both rewrote settle by last writer wins. |

Folders: A deletes folder F (its meetings moved out, or deleted with it). Both arrival orders go as for P, with the same limit.

| B does, before it hears of the deletion | Every device ends with |
|---|---|
| Renames F | F a tombstone (F1); the rename is gone. |
| Moves meeting M into F | M live with a `folder_id` naming a tombstone: in no folder (F2). Also when A chose "Delete folder and its meetings": M was not in F on A, so it is not deleted. |
| Moves M out of F while A deletes F and its meetings | M is deleted everywhere (a meeting tombstone is final on every device that holds it, 5.3 step 6; the limit of 7A.1 applies to it as to P). |
| Makes a folder with F's name | A new id, a live folder; nothing to settle. |
| Deletes F too | Two tombstones, last writer wins. |

**Held tombstones** (A7). A person tombstone held on B has moved B's cursor, so B's own later write to P may already have replaced the tombstone at the relay. B holds it in `sync_pending`, so B is a holder, but a held row defends nothing until it applies:
- *Held `clock`* (stamped more than `MAX_CLOCK_AHEAD_MS`, 24 h, past B's clock): it is retried on every tick (`retry_held`) and applies at the first tick at which B's clock has come within 24 h of its stamp. So B defends from then, at most (the stamp's lead over B's clock) minus 24 h after it was pulled. Meanwhile P5 has already recorded every unlink the page brought, so the centroids still go when it applies.
- *Held `format`* (written by a newer build): it applies only once B is updated.
When it applies, P1's second case writes it, stamped again above B's copy, and C1's extra push of that tick sends it. A `busy` hold never applies to a person or folder row (neither belongs to a meeting).

**A changed vault** (T14.45). A relay restored from a copy older than the tombstone has lost it; every device then sends everything again, the holders of the tombstone among them, so it is lost only if no holder that can send is used again (the same as any other change on that computer).

### 7A.7 Versions

No `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` change, no migration, no synced row shape change, no wire field and no relay change. `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` stays 4 (`crates/earshot-sync/src/seal.rs`). Its doc and the AGENTS rule name two reasons to raise it: an existing synced entity's serialised shape changes, or older builds must not hold the new rows. Neither applies: P1 to P4, F1 and F2 write person, folder, speaker, voiceprint and voice sample rows with the fields they have today (a cleared name, `person_id` cleared, `ignored` false, "Speaker N", tombstones), add no entity, and 0.9.0 reads and applies every such row. The first revision raised the format only to keep 0.9.0 pushes away from a relay that enforced R1; with no R1 there is nothing to keep out. Rows of formats 1 to 4 open as today, and 0.4.2 computers are refused already (section 3). P5's `linked_before` is a field of a device-local record that is never synced; it joins record version 2 with a default, so `IDENTITY_RECORD_VERSION` stays 2 (the AGENTS rule for a field older readers can ignore).

What a 0.9.0 computer (format 4, without these rules) does beside updated ones, part of the accepted limit:

- It orders person and folder rows by stamp alone. Its own writers never make a tombstone it holds live again (they re-read the row live), but it takes a later live copy pulled over its tombstone and never stamps a tombstone again. So it does not defend a removal: for 7A.1 it counts as silent even when it holds the tombstone, and a vault of 0.9.0 computers alone behaves as today.
- With an updated holder that keeps syncing, it ends removed: the holder's re-stamped tombstone is later than any live copy, so 0.9.0 writes it and forgets the identity's voices as it does today (5.4). Until then it may show P again after a later live copy, even if it had applied the tombstone before. It has no P5, so a centroid whose row an earlier page unlinked stays on it (the gap of B1, as today), and after it is updated too while that row is live (P5's accepted limit; the sweep drops only entries of rows that are gone). A 0.9.0 build run on the same store after this one (a downgrade) rewrites records without `linked_before`, and the sweep, already done on that device, does not run again (P5, "A downgrade").
- It runs no P2 or P3, so a speaker row it links to P, or a person it merges into P, reaches updated computers, which unlink the row or remove the person and send the result with a later stamp; 0.9.0 then takes those rows. Until they arrive its own speaker rows keep the removed name.
- A removal made on a 0.9.0 computer sends an ordinary tombstone, which updated computers then defend.

Release note line, word for word: "Update every computer that syncs; a 0.9.0 computer does not defend a removal."

Nothing here loses a recording or a meeting. The cost is that while 0.9.0 computers remain, a removal holds only as long as an updated holder that can send keeps syncing; updating every computer is recommended and nothing enforces it (7A.12).

### 7A.8 Stamps, loops and what the relay holds

- A device stamps its tombstone again at most once per pulled live copy that is later, in either P1 case, and never for a tombstone or an earlier copy. With N devices each holder may stamp once for the same losing copy; those tombstones hold identical content and settle by last writer wins. Nothing stamps in answer to a tombstone, so there is no loop. The same holds for the live copy P4 keeps.
- Applying a page twice writes nothing more: P1 ignores a copy that is not later, P2 and P3 find nothing left, and P5 adds a person to `linked_before` once and drops an entry once.
- Once a holder's tombstone is stored after the last live write, the relay holds one sealed tombstone for that row key (no name) and no live copy; it keeps one body per key. In the limit's case it holds B's live copy instead (7A.1).
- Once a device holds the tombstone, none of its own writers may make the row live again: `rename_person`, `merge_persons`, `enroll`, `folders::rename` and `move_meeting` re-read the row live inside their transaction, links resolve their person through `live_person` (7A.3), and rule 1 writes only live "Me" rows.
- Known window: until a device has pulled the tombstone it shows the removed name (and on it the name can still be given to a voice). The window ends at that device's next completed pull after the relay's copy of P is the tombstone: at once when the tombstone reached the relay after the last live write, otherwise after the next completed tick of a holder that can send (its pull and C1's extra push, 7A.6). In the limit's case it does not end.

### 7A.9 What the client does around the page

- **Names** (T12.82). P1's second case and P3 rewrite speaker names outside the page's own speaker rows, so before the page the client also reads `names_before` for the live speaker rows that P1's second case or P3 can rewrite: rows linked to a person that is tombstoned or whose chain ends at a tombstone (`chain_end` is `Removed`), either in the page or here, and on a page applied with every repair, every live speaker row already linked to such a person. Only those: "a person the page holds a row for" in earlier drafts was wider than needed. The set is picked by guarded reads (`speaker_links` joined to `person_links`, then `chain_end`; D1).
  - *Widening without a new failure* (review 9 B1, rewritten after review 10). The widened set reaches meetings the page did not write, which 0.9.0 never read, so a damaged speaker or summary row there must not stop the first page at every start. Under section 16 `names_before` and `names_changed_tx` are guarded roots that skip a damaged row and count it (16.5 items 12 and 14): a damaged speaker row loses only its own `summary-names` mark, a meeting whose current summary does not read loses its marks, P3 still rewrites its rows. The ninth review's per-meeting calls and `TickReport.names_skipped` are no longer needed; the page's own meetings are read the same way.
  - `names_changed_tx` then runs in `rules.after`, after P3 (7A.2's order), and marks a summary that used the name, as on the removing computer. The removal repair does the same per meeting.
- **Window.** `Applied::people` is set by P1 (either case), P2, P3, P4 and P5, and `Applied::meetings` names the meetings whose speaker rows P3 rewrote or whose identity record P5 changed. Counts only: `Applied::persons_removed_with` (P2), `Applied::speakers_unlinked` (P3), both in `TickReport.repaired`, `Applied::erased` (7A.4) and `Applied::tombstones_restamped` (C1), with `TickReport.extra_push` saying whether C1 pushed.
- **Erasure.** `Store::flush_erasures` after a page with `Applied::erased > 0`, in place of today's `biometrics_forgotten > 0` gate (7A.4).
- **C1.** The tick keeps `tombstones_restamped` and `stamped_here` across the `retry_held` page, the pull's pages and the `parent` retry, then makes C1's extra push (7A.2).

### 7A.10 Shape

No synced row changes shape, no migration, no new entity, no wire field, no relay change and no new problem word. `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` stays 4 (7A.7). One device-local field: `SpeakerIdentity::linked_before` (record version 2 unchanged). Two device records, each meaning done by its presence, each with a `DeviceRecord` variant in the runtime's registry pinned to its key: `identity-sweep` (P5's one-off sweep; key in earshot-store, written inside `Store::open`) and `removal-repair` (`DeviceRecord::RemovalRepair`, the one-off removal repair, written by the runtime). New code is in `apply_remote` (P1 to P5, F1, `stays_final` with P4's exception, the order of 7A.2, rule 4c's name for a nameless row), `identities` (`relinked`, `row_relinked_tx`, `forget_identity`'s new `linked_before` test, the sweep as a guarded root with its own SQL constant, D1), `Store::open` (the sweep before the open's checkpoint, in a guarded IMMEDIATE transaction), P3 through section 16's `unlink_speakers_tx` (D1), `earshot_runtime::sync::removal_repair` (a one-line `services.guarded_write` wrapper) and its two callers (the app's `earshot-sync-start` thread, the worker before `Worker::open`) with its `SyncEvent`, section 16's `chain_end` with 7A's `MeTombstone` outcome and `Applied::chains_unknown`, the widened `names_before` set (section 16's guarded `names_before`, per-row skips), `outbox_page` (the temporary id table), `push`'s folded counts, the writers of 7A.3, the client's `names_before`, erasure gate and C1's extra push in `tick_into`, and the counts of 7A.9.

### 7A.11 Tests phase 2 must have

Every convergence case runs both through `LocalRelay` (the real relay code, which orders by `updated_at` alone) and as straight `apply_remote` pages, in both arrival orders, with the offline write stamped both before and after the removal.

- **The limit, pinned** (`sync_known_limit_a_removal_whose_holders_never_sync_again_is_undone_by_a_later_offline_rename`): A removes P, pushes and is dropped; B's offline rename and newly saved voice, stamped after the removal, then B syncs; then a third device C joins and syncs from the start. B and C show P live with B's name and B's saved voice, A's erased voices stay erased, and the relay holds B's copy. Its name and comment say it pins the documented limit of 7A.1, so a later fix changes it on purpose, never by accident.
- **A holder closes it**: the same, then A runs one tick: P a tombstone with no name, no live print or sample of P, no speaker row linked to P, no identity entry of P's rows, on A, B and C, and the relay holds the tombstone. The same with C pulling the tombstone before B's push and C, not A, syncing again. The same with B's copy stamped before the removal (answered `superseded`, nothing to close).
- **Holders that cannot send** (7A.1): a holder whose tombstone row is damaged, one whose clock is more than 24 h ahead, one whose clock is more than 24 h behind the live copy's stamp (the copy is held `clock` and P1 never sees it), and one refused `clock_refused`: none closes the limit in that tick, and each says so through the existing counts (`unsendable`, rows held back, held rows, `clock_refused`); the one behind closes it at the first tick after its clock comes within 24 h of the stamp.
- **A superseded tombstone**: B's later copy reaches the relay first, A's earlier tombstone is answered `superseded` and leaves A's outbox, A's pull stamps it again and the same tick's extra push stores it, B ends removed.
- **C1, the extra push**: after one tick of a holder whose pull stamped a tombstone again, the relay holds the re-stamped tombstone and the holder's outbox has no entry for it; the same for a folder, a meeting, a voiceprint and a voice sample tombstone, for P1's second case (a pulled tombstone written with this device's stamp), and for a tombstone stamped again by the start-of-tick `retry_held` page. A tick whose pages stamped nothing again makes exactly one push request (counted at a `LocalRelay` wrapper), and a tick that stamped several tombstones makes exactly two. A page that only skipped a live child of a deleted meeting (`deletions_kept` above 0, `tombstones_restamped` 0) makes no extra push. A holder with more than `MAX_PUSH_PAGES` x `PUSH_PAGE` rows queued still has the re-stamped tombstone on the relay after one tick. No extra push after a `clock_refused` first push, a vault reset or a stop. A failing extra push leaves the tombstone queued and the next tick sends it.
- **Every row of both tables in 7A.6**, and the arrival-order table, for persons and folders.
- **Split pages and ticks** (B1): an identity with more speaker rows than a pull page, one of them S linked to P by B's user (entry `given_person` `None`). (1) S's rewritten row in page 1 and the tombstone in page 2 of one tick; (2) the same with the tick stopped after page 1 and the tombstone applied in a later tick; (3) the tombstone held `clock` while page 1 applies, then released; (4) the tombstone first and S's row after (a holder stamped the tombstone again later). In all four B ends with no entry for S in `speaker-identities/M`, read back from disk, and the on-disk check that S's centroid bytes are gone with a second connection kept open. The negative control removes `linked_before` from `forget_identity`'s test and must fail (1) to (3). Also the removing computer's form: B links R to P after the removal; on A, P3 unlinks R and A's entry for R is gone; on B, R's entry is gone in both orders. And a page boundary between P2's merged person and its tombstoned target.
- **P5 itself**: `relinked` as a pure function over every case and the order of its cases (`from` removed with `to` live and with `to` none: `None`; `from` live and `to` live: `linked_before` cleared, `from` not added; `from` live and `to` none: added; neither: unchanged); `row_relinked_tx` from each of its callers (a user link, Not, a pulled row, P3) and a damaged local speaker row (`from` read from its column; neither readable: entry dropped); a user edit whose helper dropped an entry checkpoints and the on-disk check finds the centroid gone (second connection kept open); `linked_before` added once, cleared by a link to a live person; a record written with `linked_before` read by the earlier build's decoder (version 2, field ignored). A scan test that the final pass never calls `row_relinked_tx`.
- **Forgetting a voice keeps the transcript** (the owner's invariant, P5): a meeting with a speaker row linked to person P and an identity entry for it; `Store::forget_voiceprints(P)`, and the same through a pulled voiceprint tombstone: P's prints, samples and centroids are gone (on-disk check), and every speaker row's name, `person_id` and `ignored`, every person row, and every segment's speaker are byte-equal to before. With a positive control that the same comparison sees a change after `Store::remove_person(P)`. Then the sweep at the next open leaves that meeting's other entries of rows linked to live persons in place.
- **The final pass and P5** (N2, advisory 6), read back from disk after the pass's own rewrite of the record: on B, P's print tombstones arrive first while P is still live; B re-transcribes M; a voice the earlier pass named P by voiceprint no longer matches (no live print) and is written as "Speaker N"; the record read back holds that voice's entry with P in `linked_before`; then P's tombstone arrives and no centroid of P remains in `speaker-identities/M` (on-disk check with a second connection kept open). The same with the old person already tombstoned when the pass runs: the new entry is not written. A continued voice keeps its old `linked_before`.
- **The one-off sweep** (P5): a store with, in one meeting's record, entries whose rows are tombstoned with name "" (fifth review B-2), missing, and with a `deleted` column that does not read; and entries whose rows are live and unlinked (automatic name, ignored), live and linked to a live person, to a person not known here, to an `is_me` tombstone and to a non-`is_me` tombstone; plus an undecodable record in another meeting. After `Store::open`: the first three kinds and the undecodable record are gone, every live row's entry is byte-equal to before (the last is then dropped by P3 on the first tick); `identity-sweep` exists; the drop is off the disk (second connection kept open). Ordering: entries written by an edit, a pulled page and a final pass before the open change nothing about what the sweep drops (B-1). A second open changes nothing and reads no record. The sweep reads no column but the speaker row's `id` and `deleted` (a scan test over its function).
- **A damaged row does not fail the open** (advisory 4, review 6): a meeting with one speaker row whose other columns do not decode (`tx.speakers` would fail) and whose `deleted` reads 0, and another whose `deleted` does not read: `Store::open` succeeds, the first row's entry is kept, the second's dropped.
- **Save after the sweep** (review 6): an older meeting with an unnamed voice and its entry; after the open's sweep the user names and saves it: `remember_in` enrols it (not `NoStoredVoice`), and a re-transcription keeps the name. Positive control: the same with the entry removed by hand gives `NoStoredVoice` and the name is not carried.
- **No wait for the ignore copy** (advisory 2, review 6): with `ignore-copied` absent the open still sweeps; a live row's entry with a legacy ignore is kept and the copy then flags the row; the accepted case (a tombstoned row with a legacy ignore, revived by rule 4c after the sweep and before the copy) comes back not ignored, pinned under a name that says it is the documented limit.
- **The tick's counts and the id set** (C1, advisory 5): a tick whose first push refuses rows r1 and r2 and holds a1 too far ahead, with `unsendable` 2, and whose extra push refuses r2 and r3 with `unsendable` 1, reports exactly `refused == 3`, `clock_ahead == 1` and `unsendable == 2`; a tick whose extra push has none reports the first push's counts exactly; a `stamped_here` of 40,000 ids (above SQLite's bound-parameter limit) is read through the temporary table and sent in stamp order.
- **Rule 4c, nameless row**: a tombstoned speaker row with name "" named by a live edited final line comes back live as the first free "Speaker N", never "".
- **First start repairs** (advisory 5): a store with a speaker row linked to a person tombstoned here and a live person merged into one, and an empty pull: after the first tick both are mended (P3, P2); a second tick with an empty pull writes nothing.
- **The one-off removal repair** (review 7 advisory 5): the same store with sync off: at the next app start both are mended, a summary that used the removed name is marked (`summary-names`), `removal-repair` exists, the rewritten rows wait in the outbox and go when sync is turned on; a second start writes nothing; an injected store error leaves the app started, nothing written and no record, and the next start repairs.
- **Damaged rows under section 16** (D1, rewritten after review 10). End to end, through `apply_remote` and the removal repair (possible now that section 16 is built first): P3 over a meeting with a speaker row whose `person_id` reads a person tombstoned here and whose `ignored` does not read: unlinked by `unlink_speaker_by_rowid` with `person_id` NULL, `ignored` 0 (the constant the readable path writes; review 10 advisory 3), a "Speaker N" when its name and `deleted` read, and this device's stamp; it becomes readable and sendable, since `ignored` was its only damage (review 10 advisory 4); with a second damaged column (`role`) it stays unreadable and unsendable; its entry dropped; readable rows rewritten as usual. A row damaged only in `person_id` (review 9 B2): not rewritten (byte-equal after P3), its entry dropped; through `LocalRelay`, the same row linked to a live Q on another device stays linked to Q there and this copy is never sent. `chain_end` over a chain with an unreadable step is `Unknown` and counts in `Applied::chains_unknown` (review 10 advisory 1); `relinked` then keeps `p` in `linked_before`; rule 3a and P2 over the same chain change nothing. A tombstoned step whose `is_me` reads `true` is `MeTombstone` and one whose `is_me` does not read is `Unknown`: neither erases (P4). `relinked` with `to` linked to a person whose chain is `Unknown` or `Removed` keeps `from` in `linked_before` (case 3, review 10 advisory 2); with `to` live it clears it (case 2). A damaged person row elsewhere and a damaged speaker row in a meeting with a summary, under a pulled person tombstone and under the removal repair: the page and the repair commit, counted. The sweep over a damaged speaker row (covered under the sweep's tests). 7A's new roots (P1 to P5's page functions, the sweep, P2's inputs, the widened `names_before`, the removal repair) are in section 16's scan test (16.8), so a decoding read, a call through `.store()` or `.parts()`, or SQL outside a named constant in them fails it; there is no list of known exceptions. Controls (review 11, advisory 8): with the tombstoned step's `is_me` readable `false`, `chain_end` is `Removed` and rule 3a erases and P2 tombstones; with every step readable, `chains_unknown` is 0 and the chain adds nothing to `damaged_skipped`; a loop and a person not yet pulled give `Unknown` with `chains_unknown` 0 (advisory 4). P2 with a live person merged into a removed one whose own `is_me` does not read (advisory 7): it is not tombstoned, its voices stay, its rowid is in `damaged_skipped`; control: with its `is_me` readable `false`, P2 tombstones it and forgets its biometrics. *The deliberate change to section 16* (advisory 5): `MeTombstone` turns a readable tombstoned `is_me` step from `Removed` (section 16's `chain_end`, its test 12 case and 16.6) into `MeTombstone`, so rule 3a no longer erases there; 7A's commit updates section 16's test 12 expected output for that case, and 16.3, 16.6 and test 12 say "until 7A".
- **A widened `names_before` never stops a page** (review 9 B1): a store holding a 0.9.0-era leftover (a speaker row linked to a person tombstoned here) in a meeting the first page does not write, with a damaged speaker row and, separately, a damaged summary row in that meeting: the first page applies, P3 unlinks the leftover, `Applied::damaged_skipped` counts the damaged summary row (section 16.7), the meeting's `summary-names` gets no mark; a positive control with no damaged row marks it. The same through the removal repair. A widened set picked for a person merely held by the page (live, not chaining to a tombstone) is empty.
- **The removal repair's placement, races, erasure and window** (review 8): in the app it runs on `earshot-sync-start` before `start_if_on` with sync on and off; in the worker before `Worker::open`; a repair and a tick started together leave the same rows as either alone (one runs after the other under the store lock); a repair that cleared a name calls `flush_erasures` and the old name is gone from the database file and WAL (second connection kept open); its changed meeting and person ids reach the window as one `SyncEvent`, and a listening Speakers panel re-reads.
- **The sweep, best effort** (review 7 advisory 2): an injected error inside the sweep leaves `Store::open` succeeding, nothing dropped and no `identity-sweep`, and the next open sweeps; two opens racing sweep once (the record re-read inside the transaction); in the batched variant, a run stopped after its first batch keeps that batch's drops and no record, and the next open completes with the same final records as an uninterrupted run.
- **A relink lost to a removal** (7A.6, review 7 advisory 6): B relinks a voice from P to Q, stamped before A's removal: every device ends with the row unlinked, no notice, B's entry holding Q in `linked_before`, and B's sample for Q still Q's; stamped after: the row stays linked to Q everywhere and B's entry is kept.
- **C1 and the filters** (N1): with voiceprint sync off, a pull that makes P1 stamp a person tombstone again and `forget_identity_biometrics` tombstone prints and erase samples: the extra push sends the person tombstone and no voiceprint or voice sample row (counted at a `LocalRelay` wrapper); with a meeting recording, its speaker rows P3 rewrote stay queued and go after the recording ends; a P3 speaker row whose meeting row is still queued here is held `parent` on the receiver and applied when the meeting row arrives.
- **Three devices** where two hold the tombstone and one sends a later live copy (one stamp each, then no further writes), and a page applied twice (nothing written, no entry changed).
- **Tombstone against tombstone** (plain last writer wins).
- **P2** through a chain of two merges, with a chain that ends at an unknown person or loops (nothing written), and with a chain ending at an `is_me` tombstone (nothing written).
- **P3** on a live row, a tombstoned row, a row of a leased meeting, a pulled row naming a tombstoned person, a pulled row naming a person not yet known (left, then unlinked when the tombstone comes), after rule 4c revived a row (unlinked in the same page), and a role-Me row linked to an `is_me` tombstone (left linked).
- **P4** (A1): an `is_me` tombstone pulled over a live copy and a live copy pulled over an `is_me` tombstone, each stamped earlier and later (live everywhere, no voice forgotten); an `is_me` tombstone pulled as the first copy (no local row): no `forget_identity_biometrics`, no entry dropped, no speaker row unlinked, no voice erased by rule 3a, and after the live copy arrives every link, entry and voice is as before; an `is_me` tombstone over a damaged local person row; an `is_me` tombstone as the only copy (one "Me" after rule 1, even when two devices make one); copies that disagree on `is_me` (a pulled live copy claiming `is_me` over a non-`is_me` tombstone here: stays removed; a pulled tombstone not `is_me` over a live `is_me` copy here: stays live; the same with the local row damaged: the pulled copy decides); the scan test that no shipped code tombstones an `is_me` person and none changes `is_me` on an existing row.
- **Held tombstone** (A7): a person tombstone held `clock` on B, a rename on B pushed meanwhile (replacing the tombstone at the relay), B's clock moved forward to within 24 h of the stamp, then the hold applies (tombstone everywhere, and nothing applied before that tick).
- **Order** (A5): a page whose P3 renames a speaker that a summary used: `summary-names` marks it in the same page (P3 before `rules.after`); a page where 4c revives a row linked to a tombstoned person: unlinked in the same page.
- **Writers** (7A.3): each refuses a removed person, through a chain too; `set_row_person` refuses one; a final pass over a meeting with a carried row and an identity entry naming a removed person writes "Speaker N"; the scan test of 7A.8.
- **Erasure** (A6): removing a person with no saved voice on A leads B to call `flush_erasures` for a page whose only erasures are a cleared person name and rewritten speaker names (`erased > 0`, `biometrics_forgotten` 0), and the on-disk check that the old name is gone from B's database file and WAL with a second connection kept open (the AGENTS rule for erasure tests).
- **Names pin** (7A.5, A4): the owned fields only, on both computers, with its positive control; a summary that used the name is marked on the device that did not remove.
- The convergence comparison of T14.50 (`store_sync_repairs_by_the_kinds_a_page_wrote_equal_every_repair_after_every_page`) extended with P1 to P5 and F1.
- `accept m11` unchanged and passing.

### 7A.12 Decided by the owner (2026-11-26)

1. **A removed person cannot be brought back** by a rename, merge or saved voice from another computer: final. Naming a voice with the same name afterwards makes a new person with no voices (as today on the removing computer); the old meetings stay "Speaker N" until named again. *On the other computer* the person leaves the People page, every Speakers panel shows "Speaker N" and asks for a name again, and voices saved there for that person are erased, at its first sync after the relay's copy of the person is the tombstone (7A.6), except in the limit's case (point 5).
2. **An offline change lost to a removal is dropped with no notice, for now**: a rename, a new link or saved voice, and both merges. Merging X into P offline removes X with P. Merging P into Q offline still removes P: Q keeps its own name and voices, loses the voices that were P's, and P's lines go back to "Speaker N" (also when Q is you). The People page shows no message; a notice can be a later task.
3. **Sync never removes you** (P4 kept). Nothing shipped removes the user's own person; if a copy of a removed one ever arrives, the live one wins everywhere, and nothing of yours (links, voices, speaker identities) is forgotten in the meantime. Nothing shows on either computer.
4. **A deleted folder stays deleted** the same way: an offline rename is lost, a meeting moved into it elsewhere stays and shows in no folder, and "Delete folder and its meetings" never deletes a meeting that was moved in on another computer after the folder was read. *On the other computer* the folder leaves the list at its next sync and such a meeting shows under All meetings only.
5. **No relay rule and no format rise; the never-syncs-again case is accepted as a limit** (7A.1). If every computer that holds a removal and can send it stays silent after another computer's later offline change reached the sync server, that change wins there, and computers that never received the removal show the person or folder again. A holder cannot send when its copy is damaged, its clock is more than a day ahead, or the server refuses it. The same limit already applies to deleted meetings, voiceprints and voice samples. Not decided, recommended: update every computer, because a 0.9.0 computer does not defend a removal it holds (7A.7); nothing enforces it.
6. **Facts that do not change** (for the record):
   - A removal made on a computer that is then never used again before it syncs is lost, like any other change made there.
   - Until the other computer has synced, it still shows the name, and its user can still name a voice after that person; that work is undone at the sync.
   - Text already written keeps the name everywhere: summaries, transcripts, word choices, the Dictionary and exported notes are not rewritten (a summary that used the name is marked as using an earlier name).

7. **Decided by the owner (2026-11-27).**
   - *The one-off clean-up drops only entries of rows that are gone*: once per device, at store open, every speaker identity entry whose speaker row is missing, tombstoned or unreadable is dropped (P5, "The one-off sweep"), in place of the selective sweep that reviews 2 to 5 blocked. An entry whose row is live, linked or not, is never touched, because Save, the re-run carry, the ignore and the meeting detail read it (review 6: an earlier draft dropped live unlinked rows' entries, which would have broken Save for every unnamed voice of an older meeting).
   - *Accepted limit*: an orphaned centroid of a person removed on another computer while this device ran 0.9.0 stays on a live unlinked row on this device: device-local, never synced, not shown. From this build on `linked_before` prevents new ones.
   - *Forgetting a voice never takes a person out of a transcript*: it only stops them being recognised in future (P5, the invariant). Nothing in this section rewrites a speaker row, person row or line on a forget.
   - *The gap today's code still has*, moved to its own task, T14.54: a forget drops the person's speaker centroids (`forget_identity_biometrics`), and a re-transcription carries a named row only through those entries (`identities::match_previous`), so re-transcribing one of their meetings after "Forget voice" loses the name there. 7A neither widens nor fixes it.

The second review's fixes (7A.13) change no decision above: P5 erases more, never less; P5's sweep erases only entries of rows that are gone; A1 keeps more of the user's own data, as point 3 already said.

### 7A.12a As built: the one-off sweep (T14.49c, 2026-11-27)

- Cost, measured on a generated store of 2,000 meetings (four speaker rows each, one tombstoned; a record of four entries with 512-float centroids, the CAM++ size; a 54 MB database): the open that sweeps takes about 0.34 s in a release build (three runs: 331, 339 and 341 ms, its checkpoint and close included) and 2.0 s in a debug build; an open after it about 3 ms. Under the 2 s line for shipped builds, so the sweep is one transaction and the batched variant is not built. Reproduce with `cargo test --release -p earshot-store --test sync_sweep cost -- --ignored --nocapture`.
- `SWEEP_SPEAKERS_SQL` is owned in `SQL_OWNERS` by `sweep_speakers`, the sweep's private reader, not by `sweep_identities` itself: section 16.8 forbids SQL calls in a root's body, so the constant cannot be both. The scan holds the intent instead: `.sweep_speakers(` may be called only inside `sweep_identities`, and the constant's columns must be exactly `rowid, id, deleted`.
- The tables are STRICT (16.10a), so a speaker row's `deleted` that does not read cannot be stored; the sweep still reads it three ways, and the test pins that SQLite refuses such a value.
- The best-effort test injects its error with a trigger in the test's store that refuses the record's write, after every drop; shipped code has no seam for it.
- The revived row of the accepted ignore-copy case is made live by a later live copy over the tombstone (the case *Trust boundary* names beside rule 4c).

### 7A.12b As built: the removal repair (T14.49e, 2026-11-27)

- The root is `earshot_runtime::sync::removal_repair_in` (`crates/earshot-runtime/src/sync/removal.rs`) behind the one-line wrapper `removal_repair`; its store half is `earshot_store::removal_repair_tx` (P2 then P3 on the caller's `GuardedTx`, counted as a page's `Applied`). Both are in 16.8's scan.
- `names_before` (through `rows_removal_may_rename` with an empty page and every repair) is read under the services store lock just before the transaction, as `apply_page_in` reads a page's, because those readers take a `GuardedRead`; the lock is held from that read to the commit, and the record is read again inside the transaction.
- The record's JSON holds `persons_removed_with`, `speakers_unlinked`, `erased`, `names_recorded` and `damaged_skipped` at version 1.
- `SyncEvent.people` is a flag, so the repair returns the changed meeting ids and whether a person changed (`RemovalRepaired::changes`), not person ids.
- In the app the `earshot-sync-start` thread runs `SyncHost::start_up` (the ignore copy, the repair, the sync thread, in that order); the order inside it is pinned by a source test, since the thread's first tick comes after the repair either way. A panic in the ignore copy or the repair is caught there (`START_STEP_PANICKED`), so the sync thread still starts (T14.49f). The window's first list read waits for its sync subscription (`AppShell`, T14.49f: subscribe, then read; a subscription that fails still lets the read start), so names the repair changed before that read are in it and a change after it is heard.

### 7A.13 Revisions

- **After the first spec review (2026-11-26).** Blocking: finality held in one direction only, and the relay could drop a tombstone whose writer then went silent. Added R1 (a relay rule) and the second case of P1 and F1 (7A.2), the blocking walk and both arrival orders (7A.6), and a format bump to 5. Advisories: writers that refuse a removed person (7A.3, with `set_row_person` and the final pass tightened); the user under an `is_me` tombstone (P4, replacing the first draft's "applied like any other"); the erasure flush for removals with no voice (7A.4); the names pin (7A.5); the owner's questions with recommendations and what the other computer shows (7A.12); the tests (7A.11).
- **After the owner's decision (2026-11-26).** The owner chose not to build R1 or raise the format, and accepted the case R1 closed as a stated limit, the same limit meeting, voiceprint and voice sample tombstones already have. Removed R1 (`pulled`, `unseen`, `server_update_needed`, the second push after a pull, the old-relay check) and the rise to format 5; 7A.1 now states the limit and when it applies. Kept P1 to P4, F1, F2 and everything else. 7A.6 walks both arrival orders again against the relay as it is (`Db::push` keeps the strictly later stamp, a superseded row leaves the outbox, a tick pushes before it pulls) and states where finality holds; 7A.7 says why no version changes and what a 0.9.0 computer does beside updated ones; the tests pin the limit and the holder that closes it; 7A.12 records the owner's answers. Then, at the coordinator's request: C1 (7A.2), a client-only extra push in the same tick after a pull that stamped a tombstone again, bounded to one per tick, which shrinks the "A stops between pull and push" window of 7A.6 to a tick cut off before the extra push's answer; its tests in 7A.11; and the release note line in 7A.7.
- **After the second spec review (2026-11-27).**
  - Blocking B1: a removed identity's centroid could stay in `speaker-identities` when its row was unlinked by an earlier pulled page or tick than the tombstone (the earlier "pre-scan the page" order fixed only the same page). Replaced it with P5 (`linked_before` on the entry, `row_relinked_tx` at every link change, `forget_identity` reading it, a one-off sweep for entries orphaned before), walked in 7A.6 across pages, ticks, a held tombstone and the removing computer, and tested in all four splits with a negative control. Reordering the stamps in `remove_person` was considered and not taken: re-stamps reorder the relay anyway.
  - A1: an `is_me` tombstone now has no side effects anywhere (biometrics, P2, P3, rule 3a, P5), and `stays_final` does not apply against a pulled `is_me` row.
  - A2: the guarantee is now about a holder that can send, with the cases that cannot listed (damaged tombstone, clock more than 24 h ahead, refused), and C1 sends its rows by id so a long outbox no longer blocks it.
  - A3: C1 counts `tombstones_restamped`, not `deletions_kept`, and includes the `retry_held` page.
  - A4: the names pin covers only the fields this section owns, and the places it does not touch are listed.
  - A5: the order inside `apply_remote` is stated (P2 in `converge`, P3 after `edits_win` and before `rules.after`).
  - A6: the flush gate changes to `erased > 0`, with a test whose page erases only names.
  - A7: a held tombstone's bound is stated.
  - Lesson for AGENTS (to add when phase 2 lands): anything that decides an erasure from a link must survive the link being cleared by an earlier pulled page or tick; record what the link said before it changes, and never rely on the relay's order of rows.
- **After the third spec review (2026-11-27).**
  - N1 (privacy): C1's by-id send skipped `outbox_page`'s filters and would have sent voiceprint rows with voiceprint sync off and rows of a recording meeting. C1 is now `push` with an optional id set passed to `outbox_page`, so it inherits every filter; tested with voiceprint sync off and a recording meeting.
  - N2: the final pass rewrites `speaker-identities` whole from entries it built in memory, so a helper writing the stored record mid-pass would be lost. P5 is now a pure function on an entry (`relinked`), used by the pass on its in-memory entries and by the store helper for one-row writers; the pass never calls the helper. Tested with the reviewer's sequence, read back from disk after the pass's own rewrite.
  - N3: the sweep skipped `user_edited` entries and rows stamped here, which is exactly what an orphan becomes after a re-transcription. Both conditions are gone; the sweep keys on unlinked, not ignored, an automatic "Speaker N" name, and `given_person` `None` or removed, runs in one transaction with its marker before the first tick.
  - Advisories: a record-level `tracks_links` marker replaces the one-off `identity-sweep` record, so a downgrade that rewrote a record is swept again (1); a damaged local speaker row gives `from` from its own column, or its entry is dropped (2); P3 rows sent ahead of a queued meeting row rely on the receiver's `parent` hold (3); a holder more than 24 h behind the live copy's stamp is listed in the limit (4); a first tick with an empty pull still runs every repair once (5); the pass test reads the record back after the pass's rewrite (6).
  - Lessons for AGENTS (to add when phase 2 lands): any send of synced rows outside `outbox_page` applies the same filters, so a new send goes through `push` and `outbox_page`, never beside them; a writer that rewrites a device-local record whole applies per-row rules to its in-memory copy, never through a helper that writes the stored record in the middle of its transaction.
- **After the fifth spec review and the owner's decisions (2026-11-27).** *Superseded by the sixth review's entry below: this entry describes a sweep that dropped live unlinked rows' entries and a cost no longer in 7A.12 point 7; kept as history.* Reviews 2 to 5 each blocked on a removed person's voice data left on a device; the fifth found the rest sound (P1 to P4, F1, C1) and blocked only P5's selective sweep of entries orphaned before this build.
  - Blocking B-1 and B-2 (the selective sweep: other writers set `tracks_links` before it ran; a tombstoned row's name is "", so the "Speaker N" test missed it): replaced, by the owner's decision, with a blunt one-off sweep at `Store::open` that drops every entry whose row is unlinked and not ignored, tombstoned or missing, reads only the row's `deleted`, `person_id`, `role` and `ignored` columns (no name, stamp or entry field), and is marked done by a device record only it writes. An ignored row keeps its entry, so the user's ignore survives a re-transcription (the coordinator's change before review 6; the row's `ignored` is a synced column only the user's ignore sets, so it reopens neither B-1 nor B-2). It waits for the ignore copy, which still reads legacy flags from those entries. `IdentityRecord::tracks_links` is removed; `linked_before` and `relinked` stay, because they cover rows unlinked from now on across pages, ticks and re-transcriptions, which a sweep run once cannot. Its costs, including one the owner had not been told (an old typed name on an unlinked row, lost only at a re-transcription), are in 7A.12 point 7.
  - The owner's invariant, written into P5: forgetting a voice never takes a person out of a transcript. Checked against 7A: nothing here rewrites a row on a forget. The existing re-transcription gap is described in 7A.12 point 7 and moved to T14.54.
  - Advisories, each checked against the code: C1's second push overwrote the tick's `unsendable`, `refused` and `clock_ahead` (`engine.rs` assigns them at the end of `push`): now folded (larger, and union of id sets). `relinked`'s cases now have a stated order (removed first, then a link to a live person). `flush_erasures` now follows the sweep (inside `Store::open`, with retries) and a user edit whose helper dropped an entry. The sweep's cost is stated and to be measured. Rule 4c gives a revived nameless row a "Speaker N". `stamped_here` goes to `outbox_page` through a temporary table, not an `IN` list. P4: the local copy's `is_me` decides when it reads, else the pulled copy's.
  - Lesson for AGENTS (to add when phase 2 lands): a one-off clean-up keys only on facts no other writer sets for it; a marker that other writers also set, or a field that one writer clears, makes the clean-up skip what it was for.
- **After the sixth spec review and the owner's decision (2026-11-27).** Blocking: the sweep's stated cost was wrong. A live row's entry is also what Save enrols (`edit::remember_in`, `edit.rs:2289-2294`, `NoStoredVoice`, while `save_voice_with` keeps the link), so dropping live unlinked rows' entries broke Save for every unnamed voice of an older meeting and lost any name given afterwards at the next re-transcription. The owner narrowed the sweep to entries whose row is missing, tombstoned or unreadable and accepted the limit (an orphan from the 0.9.0 era stays on a live unlinked row, device-local and not shown). Removed with it: the ignored-row exception, the "Rename here" cost and the renumbering cost (no live row's entry is dropped now).
  - Advisories: (2) the wait for `ignore-copied` is dropped, because the copy reads entries of live rows only (`ignore_copy.rs:106-113`); the one case left (a tombstoned row with a legacy ignore revived before the copy) is stated and tested. (3) The one synced value that decides a drop, the row's `deleted`, is stated as a trust-boundary decision. (4) The sweep reads `id` and `deleted` directly, never `tx.speakers`, so one damaged row cannot fail `Store::open`; tested. (5) The C1 fold test asserts exact union sizes. Added the Save-after-sweep test with a positive control.
  - Lesson for AGENTS (to add when phase 2 lands; the reviewer's): a clean-up that drops device-local biometric entries lists every reader of them (Save, the re-run carry, the ignore re-stamp, the detail view) before its cost is stated.
- **After the seventh spec review (2026-11-27).** *Superseded in part by the eighth and ninth reviews' entries below: D1 no longer hardens existing readers (T14.57), and a damaged row whose `person_id` does not read is no longer rewritten; kept as history.*
  - Blocking: `forget_identity`, P3's "Speaker N" choice and `relinked`'s `removed()` read rows through `tx.speakers` / `synced_by_id`, which fail on one damaged row, so a pulled person tombstone could roll back the page and stop sync for good. Added D1 (7A.2): those reads go through `split_rows` or direct column reads, P3 unlinks a damaged row by rowid in SQL, and a damaged person never causes an erasure by guess; tested with a damaged speaker row under a pulled tombstone and a damaged person row under a link edit.
  - Advisories: (2) the sweep is best effort at open (an error leaves the open succeeding and the record unwritten), re-reads `identity-sweep` inside its transaction, and its batched form is tested stopped midway; (3) the fifth-review entry is marked superseded; (4) the sweep's own three-way read of `deleted` is stated, an unreadable flag erring towards erasing; (5) P2 and P3 also run once per device at app start, sync on or off, as the removal repair (in the runtime, not in `Store::open`, so `names_changed_tx` runs); (6) a relink from P to Q lost to a later removal is in 7A.6 and tested, dropped with no notice as 7A.12 point 2 says.
  - Lesson for AGENTS (to add when phase 2 lands; the reviewer's): code inside `apply_remote`, or code that decides an erasure, never reads speaker or person rows through `tx.speakers` or `synced_by_id`; it reads through `split_rows` or the columns it needs, so one damaged row cannot stop a page or an erasure.
- **After the eighth spec review and the owner's decision (2026-11-27).** Blocking: D1 promised more than 7A can deliver; many existing readers on the same paths decode whole rows (`names_before`, `names_changed_tx`, `identity_of` and `all_persons`, `voiceprints_of`, rule 1's `all_persons`, `forget_identity`'s `tx.speakers`, `edit::remove_person` and `Store::remove_person`). The owner split damaged-row hardening into T14.57. D1 now promises only that 7A adds no new decoded multi-row read of speaker or person rows (the sweep, P3, `removed()`, the repair's own reads), lists the existing readers as known and left to T14.57, and states that until then one damaged row can still stop a page or hold the repair, as in 0.9.0. The `forget_identity` and `Store::remove_person` direct-read changes moved to that list (they were existing reads, not 7A's). The damaged-row tests now cover 7A's own functions directly, and the scan test only 7A's new code.
  - Advisories: (A2) the repair's place is named (the app's `earshot-sync-start` thread before `start_if_on`, unconditionally; the worker before `worker.start`) and racing a tick is safe; (A3) the repair runs `flush_erasures` after an erasing commit and joins 7A.4, with the on-disk test; (A4) 7A.10 lists both device records and the new code; (A5) the repair sends its changed ids as one `SyncEvent`, with the start-up staleness case stated.
- **After the ninth spec review (2026-11-27).** Blocking, two places where 7A made a damaged row worse than 0.9.0:
  - B1: 7A.9 widened `names_before` (`summary_names.rs:180-184`, `engine.rs:1246-1253`) on every first page with repairs to meetings the page did not write, so one damaged speaker or summary row there would stop sync at every start.
