D1 now states the rule that 7A never widens what an existing decoding reader reads; the widened meetings are read one at a time and a meeting that does not read is skipped and counted (`TickReport.names_skipped`), losing only its `summary-names` mark; the widened set is narrowed to persons tombstoned or chaining to a tombstone. Tested with a 0.9.0-era leftover and a damaged row in that meeting.
  - B2: P3 rewrote by rowid a row whose `person_id` merely did not read, which made it sendable and could overwrite good copies vault-wide. Now such a row is left alone and only its entry is dropped; a by-rowid rewrite happens only when `person_id` reads as a removed person, and writes only the columns read as wrong. Tested with a row linked to Q on another device.
  - Advisories: (A1) `removed()` and `ends_deleted` are one function, `chain_end`, on direct reads, as 7A's change (it now decides P2 and P4); (A2) D1's list adds P2's input query and the widened `names_before` query, and the scan test covers both; (A3) the seventh review's entry is marked superseded in part; (A4) the worker runs the repair before `Worker::open`, which takes `services`.
  - Lesson for AGENTS (to add when phase 2 lands; the reviewer's): a by-rowid repair writes only what was read as wrong. And: a change that hands an existing decoding reader more rows than before reads them per unit and skips what does not read, or it turns one damaged row into a stopped sync.
- **After the tenth spec review and the owner's decision (2026-11-27).** Review 10 blocked a third time on damaged rows (through `forget_identity`, called in cases 7A adds); the owner moved T14.57 ahead of this task, and section 16 now holds the damaged-row rule (spec-reviewed and passed). *Supersedes the damaged-row parts of the seventh, eighth and ninth entries above.*
  - D1 is rewritten to rely on section 16 (R1 to R4, the guarded types, `chain_end`, the written-columns rule, the scan test): 7A's new reads are guarded roots under 16, not special cases; the "Known, left to T14.57" list, the per-meeting `names_before` skip and `TickReport.names_skipped`, and the scan's list of known exceptions are gone (7A.2 D1, 7A.9, 7A.11). The removal repair is a one-line `services.guarded_write` wrapper and no longer waits on T14.57.
  - Kept: the owner's decisions (7A.12), `chain_end`'s `MeTombstone` extension (now on top of 16's `chain_end`, with an unreadable `is_me` at a tombstoned step `Unknown`), the narrowed sweep, the start-up repair and the relink cases.
  - Advisories: (1) `Unknown` chain ends are counted, `Applied::chains_unknown`, folded into the Sync page's damaged note; (2) `relinked` case 2 needs `chain_end(to) == Live`; (3) D1 and the test agree that P3 sets `ignored = 0` on a damaged row; (4) a rewritten damaged row stays unsendable unless its only damage was a column the written-columns rule sets to a constant (`ignored`, a tombstone's name), and then it sends the readable path's row, stated and tested.
- **After the eleventh spec review (2026-11-27): passed, with eight advisories, all fixed.**
  - (1) Leftovers that contradicted D1: 7A.11 asserts `damaged_skipped`, not the gone `TickReport.names_skipped`; 7A.10's new-code list names the guarded sweep, section 16's `chain_end` with `MeTombstone` and the guarded `names_before`; 7A.9's set is picked by guarded reads; `ends_deleted` is replaced by `chain_end` / `MeTombstone` in P2, P4 and P5.
  - (2) The sweep has its own constant, `SWEEP_SPEAKERS_SQL` (`rowid`, `id`, `deleted`), owned by `sweep_identities`; an entry matching no readable row in a meeting holding a row whose `id` does not read counts as missing and is dropped (7A.12 point 7), and D1 says that section 16.5 item 20 is the rule for `forget_identity`.
  - (3) Section 16.3 and 16.8 name `Store::guarded_immediate` (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, as `migrate` and the export lease do), and the sweep is opened by it.
  - (4) Only an unreadable step counts in `damaged_skipped` (`chains_unknown` is the number of such steps); loops and persons not yet pulled count nothing; `relinked` case 3's cost when `to` is not yet known is stated.
  - (5) 7A.11 names `MeTombstone`'s deliberate change to section 16's test 12 case and 16.6, which now say "until 7A".
  - (6) `row_relinked_tx` has two entry points over the pure `relinked` (`row_relinked_guarded` for the page and P3, the `StoreTx` one for `edit::set_row_person`, a user edit outside section 16's paths); 16.3 is not relaxed.
  - (7) P2 skips a live merged-in person whose `is_me` does not read, counted, as Remove refuses `PersonUnreadable`; tested with a control.
  - (8) The D1 tests have controls (`is_me` readable `false` erases; a readable chain counts 0; loops and unpulled persons count 0).
  - Lessons from the review (the reviewer's):
    - "A count shown as "damaged" counts only rows that do not read, never a temporary absence such as a person not yet pulled or a loop."
    - "A guarded root that must hold the write lock before its first read opens an IMMEDIATE guarded transaction."

## 8. Presence (T11.3c)

- Blob plaintext: `{v: 1, name, app_version, kind: "app", offers_summaries: false, writer_kind: null, recording: bool}`, sealed with `k_presence` like a row (`aad = "earshot-presence-v1" | device_id`).
- Sent every 60 s and once with `gone: true` when sync is turned off, best effort (5 s). Since T11.3ca also on quit (the send starts first and is waited for at most 1 s, never past the 4 s exit limit) and when Windows is about to sleep (`PowerRegisterSuspendResumeNotification`, waited for at most 1.5 s); while away no beat goes out, every presence send holds one lock so a gone never lands before a beat already on the wire, and after a resume the device ticks at once and beats after the first tick that reaches the relay. The decision is `presence::Beats`, clock-free tested. A thread replaced by a new one says nothing, and removal relies on the relay clearing a revoked device's presence.
- The plaintext is padded to 640 bytes before sealing, so the blob's size says nothing of a flag or the name.
- Online is `age_s <= 150` and not `gone` (a judgement: two missed beats). The rule is one pure function with a clock-free test.
- Presence is advisory. A blob's associated data holds no time, and `age_s` is the relay's, so the relay could replay an old blob as fresh. Nothing decides on presence alone: offload (10.1) also needs the target's claim, which is a synced row the relay cannot forge.
- Phase 1 uses the name, `recording` and the age. Phase 2 fills `writer_kind` and acts on `offers_summaries`.
- What a host shows of another device (its name, writer kind, a worker's model place) is read from a blob once, `Peer::heard`. The sync thread reads presence only for its own work (the offload step, while this device waits on a claimed request, 10.1); each such read is also told to the host (`SyncClient::with_presence`, T14.50), at no request of its own. The app keeps the names in memory (`offload::remember_presence`, the one way in, also fed by the pages that list the computers) for "Waiting for <device>" and the notes of a summary run; a device whose name it had not heard, or had heard differently, is answered back, and the meetings waiting for it (`offload::meetings_waiting_on`) are named in that pass's `SyncEvent`, so an open meeting reads its banner again. Before this the names came only from a page that listed the computers or an ask made in the process, so after a restart a waiting meeting said "your other computer" until one of those ran. Limits: a request not yet claimed (90 s at most) is not named by this, and the notes of runs that have ended take a name only once some meeting waits or a page lists the computers.
- What T11.3c ships: `earshot_runtime::sync::presence` (blob, `online`, `clean_name`, `computer_name`); the thread sends presence after its first tick without a problem and every 60 s, and says gone when it is turned off (T11.3ca adds quit and sleep). `settings/device-name` (absent: the computer's name, set by `set_device_name`) and `settings/sync-offer-summaries` (off; `set_offer_summaries`) are stored and sent; the page's switch for the latter comes with T11.3d, where it does something, and no page edits the name yet. The Sync page's computer list shows each other computer's name, Online (and recording), else when it last reached the server; a blob that cannot be opened lists the computer by its short id.

## 9. The Sync page (T11.3a)

A Settings page, "Sync", after People and before Notes and storage. What T11.3a ships:

- **Not set up**: what sync does in two sentences; "Set up the first computer" (address, setup secret, Set up) and "Join with a pairing code" (address, code, Join), each its own form. The secret and code fields are cleared after every attempt.
- **Set up**: "Sync is on" switch; the server address; last sync; rows waiting to send, held for later and that could not be read; the last problem in fixed copy (and that sync is stopped until it is fixed, for a problem that stops it); a line when sync should be running and is not; "Sync now", which starts a thread that should run and does not.
- **Add a computer**: shows a pairing code once asked, until Hide, its expiry, or leaving the page.
- **Computers**: from the server (`GET /v1/vault`): this computer first, others by the first eight hex digits of their id with last seen, until presence brings names (T11.3c). The open page asks again every 10 s, only while the window is watched (visible and focused, `useWindowWatched`; hidden in the tray it asks nothing) and at once when it is watched again (T14.50). No ask makes a sync client of its own (each has its own HTTP client and runtime): `SyncHost::list_devices` asks through the sync thread's client when a thread is in place (`SyncClient::vault_devices`; it takes neither the tick lock, the store lock nor a lock of the thread's, so it runs beside a tick), else through one client kept for the pages, dropped whenever a thread is started or this computer is removed (so it never outlives its address). The Summaries page's list of computers asks the same way.
- **Voiceprints**: "Sync voiceprints" (off by default).
- **Remove this computer**: a confirmation dialog. Removal also turns voiceprint sync off, so joining a vault again asks for it afresh.
- **Remove another computer** (T11.6c): each other computer's row has a Remove (named `Remove <computer>`), confirmed in a dialog that says its synced meetings stay, that removal does not take back the sync key it holds, and to wipe a lost computer too. The list then shows the computers left; focus goes to the list, or back to the Remove on Cancel. A refusal shows in the page's notice bar with the command copy.

Not in T11.3a: revoking another computer (T11.6c), device names, the Dictionary switch (phase 2), an event (the page reads `sync_view` when it opens and after each action).

IPC commands in `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync_settings.rs`: `sync_view`, `sync_devices`, `set_up_sync`, `offer_sync_code`, `join_sync`, `set_sync_on`, `set_sync_voiceprints`, `sync_now`, `remove_sync_device`, `remove_other_sync_device` (T11.6c: by `SyncDeviceView.id`, the hyphenated UUID; returns `SyncRemovedView` with the computers left, or no list when they could not be listed after the revoke, which is still a success and the page asks again; this computer's id or text that is not an id is `invalid_setting` with `remove_other.not_another`). Every command has an `Ipc` method and a mock (`packages/ui/src/ipc/mockSync.ts`). One shared fixture, `fixtures/ui/sync_copy_cases.json`, holds the status copy (`problems`), a button press's copy (`command_problems`: nothing retries a button press, so none says Earshot will try again), the words that stop sync (`stops`), the address rule's cases, and the copy for removing another computer (`remove_other`: the row's button name, the dialog's title, text and confirm, and the refusal); both suites read every key. The key and token live in Credential Manager (`Earshot/sync-key`, `Earshot/sync-token`, `sync_vault.rs`); no response carries them, the setup secret or a used code.

Choices apply at once; Set up and Join submit typed text and are in the save-rule test's list. Screens are judged by screenshot in light and dark.

## 10. Phase 2 interfaces

### 10.1 Summary offload (T11.3d)

Superseded in part by T12.63a (docs/summary-order.md section 4): the chosen helper is now a computer step of the order of summary writers (`settings/summary-order`; `settings/summary-offload-device` is only its mirror for older builds, written by `summary_order::set_summary_order`). A run records itself in `summary-run/<meeting>`, and `ask_for_run` names the request in that record in the request's own transaction. `OffloadHost::write_here` became `continue_run`, decided by the record (`summary_order::run::hand_back`): `Continuation::Order` continues the same run, a named run ends with the computer's outcome, no record is `Continuation::Legacy` (written here as below), an unreadable record or one naming another request is settled with no job; the host answers `Started`, `Refused` or `Settled`. A `Done` request a waiting record names ends the run with that computer as its writer (`end_after_done`). The helper's writer is the first writer step of its order (`summary_order::run::helper_writer`, presence `WriterKind::of_order`), and nothing else runs for a request. The rest of this section is the T11.3da design as built.

New synced table and entity (`syncable_row!(SummaryRequest, "summary_request")`):

```
summary_request(id, META, meeting_id BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES meeting(id),
                target_device BLOB NOT NULL, asking_device BLOB NOT NULL,
                segment_count INTEGER NOT NULL, state TEXT NOT NULL, reason TEXT NOT NULL)
```

States and who may write them:

```
Requested --(target)--> Claimed --(target)--> Done
     |                     |  \--(target)--> Declined(reason)
     \--(asker, 90 s)--\   \--(asker, target stale 5 min)--\
                        `------------------> Withdrawn <----'
```

- Asker, where `host::summary_from_settings` runs: a chosen device that is online, offers summaries and is not recording gets a request; otherwise the device's own writer runs with banner `summary-offload`.
- Target: claims; waits until it holds `segment_count` live segments of the transcript; runs `host::run_summary` under `lease_meeting_alone(Processing)` in its meeting queue; re-reads the request in the summary's write transaction and stores nothing unless it is still `Claimed`.
- A kept-local meeting: the target uses a local model or answers `Declined(kept_local_no_model)`.
- The asker shows "Waiting for <device>" in the meeting status; on `Withdrawn` or `Declined` it runs its own writer.
- Setting: `settings/summary-offload-device` (a device id or empty) on the asker; "Write summaries for my other devices" (`settings/summary-offers`, off) on the helper.

What T11.3da built (`earshot_runtime::offload`), with the detail this section left open:

- **Names.** Store migration 7 adds the table, its index and its outbox triggers. `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` stays 1 (the coordinator, 2026-11-15): no existing entity changed shape, and an older build holds a row of an entity it does not know as `format` (it is applied after an update), whereas a raised format would make 0.3.7 hold every row from an updated device. States are the words `requested`, `claimed`, `done`, `declined`, `withdrawn`; `reason` is empty except for a decline. The helper's switch is the existing `settings/sync-offer-summaries` (T11.3c), not `settings/summary-offers`.
- **Presence.** `writer_kind` is sent only while the device offers summaries: `local`, `anthropic`, `openai_compatible`, `claude_code`, `codex`, or absent for "no summary". A helper with no writer, or a word this build does not know, is not asked.
- **The decision.** `offload::decide` (pure) and `choose_now` (one presence request, no store lock). It runs in `host::after_meeting` when the summary is due (after the final pass, never for a failed pass or a cancelled summary) through `AfterMeeting::offload`, not at meeting start, because presence changes during a meeting. An asked helper gives `SummaryResult::Offloaded`; a chosen helper that is not asked gives `HostOutcome::offload = HereInstead { why }` (sync off, relay unreachable, offline, not offering, recording, no writer, no final transcript, request not written) and this device's writer runs.
- **The count.** `segment_count` is the meeting's live final segments when asked. A meeting without a final transcript is not offloaded. The helper waits until it holds at least as many, and declines `no_transcript` 5 minutes after claiming (a judgement: a re-transcription on the asker can change the count for good, and a claim must not hang).
- **Steps.** `Offload::step` runs after every sync tick (`SyncThread::start_offloading`), whatever the tick's outcome: the 90 s rule needs no relay. The thread's step reads no request while nothing can have changed (T14.50, `Offload::step_after_tick_into`): when the last scan looked at no request and wrote nothing (none open for this device as asker or helper, none closed still to hand back or settle), the next steps skip the scan until the tick before them wrote, deleted or repaired a row here, the request table's mark moves (its row count and latest stamp, two aggregate reads: a request this device writes itself), or this device's clock reads earlier than at that scan. A thread's first step, a step with any request still looked at, and the last step of a stopping thread always scan. No index or migration: an idle device still reads the table's count and latest stamp each tick, not every request. Pinned by `offload_a_step_skipped_after_a_tick_is_one_a_scan_would_have_done_nothing_in` (a varied run of two devices: whenever the step would skip, the scan is run all the same and must change nothing). A tick that stops sync (a problem the user must act on, such as this device having been removed) is followed by one last step, `Offload::step_sync_stopped` (T11.6c), before the thread ends: every open request this device asked is withdrawn here at once (no claim window, no presence) and its meeting handed to `write_here` as withdrawn, because nothing this device writes can reach the helper; requests asked of this device are left alone (a claim could not reach the asker, which withdraws on its own rules once this device's presence goes). While the host refuses a hand-back (a job of the meeting runs, it is in use) the step is repeated every tick until each is started or settled, for at most `STOPPED_HANDBACK_LIMIT` (30 minutes, a judgement); a stop asked meanwhile ends it at once on a stopping problem. Turning sync off runs the same hand-back (its thread is retired on a thread of its own, so the page never waits). A withdrawal is final, so if sync works again later it wins on every device; a helper that already stored its summary meanwhile is rule 7.2's case. Every timer runs on the device clock from this device's own readings (device record `offload-times`: when it asked, first saw the claim, claimed, or first found the helper's presence missing; pruned to open requests), never on HLC wall time, which can run ahead of the clock by up to the apply window. The asker withdraws an unclaimed request 90 s after asking, and a claimed one when the helper's presence is at least 300 s old, or has been missing or unreadable for 300 s since the asker first found it so (presence that could not be asked withdraws nothing), then hands the meeting to its host (`OffloadHost::write_here`); a decline is handed the same way. A host that cannot start the write (a job of the meeting runs, the meeting is in use) says so and the next step hands it again. The helper claims, or declines `not_offered` when its switch is off, and hands the request to its host (`OffloadHost::write_for`) once the transcript is there. Each request is handed once per process while it starts; after a restart, a withdrawn or declined request with no summary created since it was asked, live or replaced by an undo, is handed again. Settled requests (done, or declined or withdrawn with a summary since) are not read again in the process, and a declined or withdrawn request whose write here first started, or that a summary settled, more than 7 days ago on this device's clock (`LOOK_BACK_MS`, readings in `offload-times`) is not read at all; one whose write here never started is never aged out. A step's write of `offload-times` re-reads and merges in one transaction, so an `ask` meanwhile is kept. A meeting has at most one open request this device asked: `ask` answers with it (`ask_for` names that request's own device, which the summary step reports), and re-summarise "On <device>" for another computer is refused (`already_waiting`).
- **The helper's write.** `offload::write_for_request`: a queue ticket, the host's writer for the meeting's privacy, its turn, `lease_meeting_alone(Processing)`, then `SummaryService::summarize_answering`, whose write transaction re-reads the request (still claimed by this device, else `Conflict`, nothing stored) and marks it done with the summary. Any failure declines (`failed`, `busy`, `kept_local_no_model`, `no_writer`); a cancel leaves it claimed. In the app the write runs on thread `earshot-offload`; a panic there, or a thread that cannot start, declines `failed` (`offload::decline`).
- **A withdrawal is final.** In `apply_remote`: a pulled withdrawal replaces an open request whatever the stamps (written as it came), and on the asker a withdrawn request is never replaced by an open one (the withdrawal is stamped again by the asker and goes out). A done or declined answer stays last writer wins: a withdrawal and a stored summary can both happen, and rule 7.2 keeps the later summary.
- **Busy.** A summary request is never held as `busy`: it holds no content and must reach a device whose summary job holds the meeting.
- **Authorship.** A request whose last write came from a device not allowed to write its state (`updated_at.node`) is ignored by every reader.

What T11.3db built in the app (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/offload.rs`): the Sync page's "Write summaries for my other computers" (`set_sync_offer_summaries`) and the computer's name (`set_sync_device_name`, typed, with its own Save name button); the Summaries page's "Another computer" (`summary_helpers`, `set_summary_helper`), listing each computer that offers summaries as "On <device> (<writer kind>)" and the chosen one whatever it offers now; the meeting's `SummaryEvent::Offloaded` and `MeetingDetail.waiting_for` ("Waiting for <device> to write the summary."), and banner `summary-offload` when the chosen computer was not asked, which says this computer wrote the summary only when one was stored (otherwise the table's `not_asked` words, claiming nothing); re-summarise "Here" or "On <device>" (`ask_summary_helper`, refused with the reason when the computer would not be asked). `HostOffload` re-summarises a handed-back meeting with its reason as the job's first warning and writes another computer's request on thread `earshot-offload` in the meeting driver's queue with a work pass (none to be had declines `busy`). Copy comes only from `fixtures/ui/offload_copy_cases.json`, read by both suites.

### 10.2 Dictionary (T11.3e)

`dictionary_word(id, META, word TEXT NOT NULL)`, class `SyncableOptIn`. Rules as `docs/sync.md` section 9. `settings/vocabulary` stays what summaries read.

The first exception to "a new entity keeps the format" is voice samples (T12.77b8, the owner, 2026-11-18; docs/voice-samples.md 7.3): `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` is 2, because a format-1 build would apply the prints and hold the samples, reading a voiceprint whose voice now lives in its samples as empty. The vault rises at the first push from a 0.4.0 device, samples or not, and every older build and worker in it then gets `update_needed` until it updates.

The format rises again, to 3, when an existing entity changes shape (T12.93, the owner, 2026-11-25): a speaker row carries `ignored`, the user's choice to ignore that voice in that meeting, which before lived only in the recording computer's device-local `speaker-identities` entry and so never reached the other computers. A format-2 build would deserialise a pulled speaker row without the field and write it back whole, undoing the ignore everywhere, so the vault rises at the first push from a build with format 3 and every older build and worker gets `update_needed` until it updates. The field has a serde default: rows written at format 1 or 2 read as not ignored. A row counts as ignored only when it is also unnamed and not the user (`ignored && role != Me && person_id` empty), on every read, because a pulled row is a trust boundary. Each device copies its own legacy device-local ignores onto the rows once, after its first completed pull (or at start when it has no vault), so an edit another computer made arrives first.

It rises to 4 with T12.92 (section 7 rule 4): a final segment row carries `pass_run`, the final-pass run that wrote it. A format-3 build would write a pulled line back without it and without the edit order of rule 4a, so the vault rises at the first push from a build with format 4. The field has a serde default and is left out when absent: rows written at formats 1 to 3 read as having no run, which rule 4b never drops.

What T11.3e ships (store migration 8; a new entity, so no `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` change: an older build holds the rows as `format`):

- `earshot_store::SyncKinds { voiceprints, dictionary }` replaces the voiceprint flag in `outbox_except`, `outbox_len_except` and `ApplyRules`; `ApplyRules::after` runs inside the page's transaction, and the client uses it to rebuild `settings/vocabulary` from the live rows while Dictionary sync is on, so the list never differs from the rows it came from.
- Every Dictionary write keeps one row per word, whether or not sync is on, and reads the stored list and writes the rows and the list in one transaction, so no sync apply can fall between them. The page edits one word at a time (`vocabulary::add_entry`, `remove_entry`; transcript words through `add_word` and `remove_word`) and never sends a whole list, so it cannot remove a word another computer added after it read the list. The one whole-list path, `set_vocabulary(base, words)` (the benchmark's fresh scratch store), takes the list its caller read and refuses with `messages.stale_list` when the stored list is no longer that; its rows follow the change from `base`. A removal removes every live row of the word (matched by the join's own normalisation, so a row from another build goes too), each a tombstone with its word cleared.
- While sync is on and Dictionary sync is not off (`vocabulary::dictionary_syncs`, read inside each transaction), the stored list is the joined list (`vocabulary::rebuild_tx`): live rows in creation order, the same word (trimmed, end quotes dropped, read by `Vocabulary::new` and lower-cased) once with the earliest spelling, a word `Vocabulary::new` refuses on its own not in use, and words taken until the next would pass 100 words or 1000 characters (a prefix: everything after the first word that does not fit is not in use). Off, the list is the device's own, as written. While any word is not in use an add is refused (`messages.add_not_in_use`), because the new word would be stored and synced but not in use.
- A stored list this version refuses is not replaced by a pulled page or by turning the switch on: it stays and is reported by its reason; the user's own add, removal or whole-list save replaces it with the joined list.
- Words stored before rows existed get rows before every tick pulls, inside every write and when the switch is turned on (`vocabulary::reconcile_tx`: a list word with no live row), so a rebuild never loses them. Turning the switch on also resets the pull cursor and rebuilds the list.
- Live summary windows are reused only with exactly the words they were mapped with: a tick that changes no row leaves the list byte for byte, but a changed word, or turning Dictionary sync on (the list goes from the user's order to creation order), maps every window again at the summary (cost only).
- The Dictionary page shows `messages.not_in_use` from `fixtures/ui/summary_settings_cases.json`; the Sync page has "Sync Dictionary words" (`set_sync_dictionary`) before the voiceprint switch. Removing this computer leaves the Dictionary switch as it is.

## 11. Phase 3 interface

`earshot-worker` is a device with `kind: "server"` in its presence blob, `offers_summaries: true`, and the target half of 10.1. Its key comes from a file named by `EARSHOT_WORKER_KEY_FILE`. Nothing in phases 1 or 2 may assume a device has a window, a microphone or Credential Manager: the sync client takes its key and token through `SyncVault` and its store through `Services`, both of which a worker can provide.

What T11.6 built (docs/sync.md section 10, "Running the worker"): the crate `earshot-worker` (`join`, `run`, `health`; configured by `EARSHOT_WORKER_*` variables, none of them a secret), its `SyncVault` over two files (`EARSHOT_WORKER_KEY_FILE`, `EARSHOT_WORKER_TOKEN_FILE`, default under `/data/vault`), presence through `SyncClient::for_address(..).as_summary_worker(writer)` (`kind: "server"`, `writer_kind` located at every send: `local` for the configured model service resolving inside the model network's fixed subnet (`EARSHOT_WORKER_MODEL_SUBNET`), else `openai_compatible`), and `SummaryService::own_model_endpoint`, the one remote service that may write a kept-local meeting, called only by the worker.

## 12. Failure behaviour

| What happens | Result |
|---|---|
| Server unreachable | status line, back-off; nothing else changes |
| Relay restored from an older copy, or its data replaced | each device notices at its next tick (a new epoch, or fewer rows than it has seen), queues every row again and reads the vault from the start; nothing is shown unless it happens more than 3 times in a day (`server`) |
| Token revoked | loop stops; page says this computer was removed |
| Wrong pairing code | fixed copy; nothing stored |
| A row cannot be opened | counted, skipped; the page shows the count |
| A row here cannot be sealed (too large, or audio) | left queued, counted; the page shows a note with the count; everything else is sent |
| Vault format newer than this build | loop stops; page says to update Earshot |
| Row for a meeting in use here | held, applied when the meeting is free |
| Device clock far ahead | its rows are not sent (it finds them, or the relay refuses the push with `clock_ahead`); the page says so; rows that reached a relay from before T14.44 are held on other devices and reported |
| Device clock far behind | rows from the others are held `clock` and tried again hourly; they apply once its clock is right |
| Sync thread panics | caught; one status event; the loop restarts on the next app start |
| Store busy | the tick ends and is retried |

## 13. Tests and acceptance

- **T11.2a** `cargo test -p earshot-sync`: seal and open round trip; a changed byte, key, stamp or id refused; audio signatures and oversize refused; pairing code round trip, case and look-alike letters; `Debug` shows no key bytes.
- **T11.1a** store and runtime `kept_local` tests: migration from the committed fixture; the two decision points with the column alone.
- **T11.1b** `cargo test -p earshot-store sync`: one table-driven case per step of 5.3 and per side effect of 5.4; triggers pinned to `SYNCED_TABLES`; an apply leaves no outbox entry of its own; on-disk erasure after a remote deletion.
- **T11.2** `cargo test -p earshot-sync-server`: every call; limits; slot expiry and single use; token hashing; supersede; paging; format refusal; nothing secret in the log.
- **T11.3** `cargo test -p earshot-runtime sync`: two simulated devices and an in-process relay converge after concurrent edits; a recording meeting is not pushed; back-off is clock-free; every audit entry is the relay.
- **T11.3b** the three-device ordering test of section 7.
- **T11.3c** the online rule; presence round trip.
- **T11.3a** desktop IPC tests (no secret in any response) and UI tests.
- **T11.4** `accept m11`: `sync.convergence` and `sync.no_audio` as PLAN 4.2 words them. Reviewer on harness honesty.
- **GATE-09** by hand: the container on the owner's server, the desktop and the Surface paired, a meeting recorded on each appears on the other, a deletion on one removes it and its audio on the other, the relay's database holds no readable text.

Local test servers end connections through the lingering close. No test uses the real Credential Manager except one round trip with a per-run target.

## 14. Decisions for the owner

1. Reorder: T11.4 and GATE-09 after phase 1, before T11.3d and T11.3e (section 0).
2. Device names default to the computer name and travel only inside the encrypted presence blob.
3. The outbox fills even while sync is off (5.2), so turning sync on later needs no scan.
4. A remote deletion deletes this device's audio for that meeting without asking (5.4). It is the same person's deletion, made on another device.
5. Judgements carried from the design: online at 150 s, claim within 90 s, stale target at 5 minutes, a stamp more than 24 h ahead is held.

## 15. Replaced transcript text is erased (T14.47)

Status: proposed, not built, after six spec reviews (15.13 lists what each revision changed; the fourth rebuilt the rule around the one device that runs a meeting's final pass, the fifth made that device the only one that clears what a pass replaced and stated what 0.9.0 computers do beside it, and the seventh drops every clearing of another device's drafts and the merge and finality rules that answered a write that cannot happen). It destroys words for good on every device, so it is built only after the owner has read it (15.11). 0.9.0 computers are not locked out: they write envelope format 4, the format this rule keeps, and sync beside updated ones (15.4, 15.10), as they do for section 7A (7A.7).

The decision is the owner's (2026-11-26): once transcript text is replaced it holds no words, on every device. Until this rule a final pass left the lines it replaced as tombstones with their text and words, and a meeting's draft (its `pass = Live` rows) stayed live with its text after the final transcript was stored; both synced. Summary history (tombstoned summary bodies, which Undo restores) is out of scope and unchanged. No row changes shape: `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` stays 4 and there is no migration.

### 15.1 The cleared form

One SQL statement in earshot-store writes it, by SQLite rowid, beside `delete.rs`'s `ERASE_BY_ROWID`: `deleted = 1`, `text = ''`, `words = '[]'`, `confidence = 1.0`, `updated_*` this device's stamp (one HLC stamp per row), and `speaker_id = NULL`. Kept: id, meeting, track, `pass`, start, end, `text_edited_at`, `pass_run`, `created_at`, origin device. No typed read is involved, so a row whose values do not convert is cleared like any other; one that still does not read afterwards is made a canonical tombstone as a deletion does (`for_deletion::canonicalise`), so it syncs.

- A row **holds text** when its text or its words are not empty: `SegmentRow::holds_text`, and `text <> '' OR words <> '[]'` in SQL, pinned to each other by a test. A cleared row and a deletion's erasure do not hold text.
- **Why 1.0.** A meeting deletion's erasure (section 7 rule 4a, `SegmentRow::is_erasure`) has confidence 0 and no speaker and is ordered by plain `updated_at`, so that it beats an edit. A replaced line must do the opposite: lose to an edit that did not see the replacement. A line's own confidence can be 0 with no speaker (T14.23 stores a recogniser fault as 0), so keeping its own values would sometimes read as an erasure. The fixed 1.0 makes `is_erasure` false for every cleared row, whatever its speaker. `is_erasure` and `delete_meeting` do not change (only `is_erasure`'s doc comment, which says a final pass's tombstone keeps its text); a deletion still rewrites cleared rows into erasures. 0.9.0 has the same `is_erasure`, so it orders cleared rows the same way.
- **Why the speaker is cleared on every row.** A draft row never carries a speaker: `store_live` (`crates/earshot-runtime/src/live/mod.rs`) is its only writer and stores the live worker's segment as it is, and a recognised segment is built with `speaker_id: None` (`Segment::from_words`, `crates/earshot-core/src/asr/types.rs`); live speaker work keeps its windows in device-local records and never writes a segment row. So clearing a draft's speaker changes nothing, and live summary reuse, which labels draft rows by `MeetingView::speaker_name` (15.3), reads the track labels it read before. A replaced final line's speaker is cleared because the pass may tombstone that speaker row (`replaced_speakers`), and a cleared final row needs nothing from it: rule 4a and 4b read only `pass`, `text_edited_at`, `pass_run`, times and `updated_at`, and rule 4c revives only speakers of live edited lines. The invariant that draft rows carry no speaker is pinned (15.2, "Writers of draft rows").
- It is a marker in an existing field. Pinned by: a core test that a row cleared from confidence 0, -0 or NaN is never an erasure, with and without a speaker; 1.0 and 0.0 read back exactly through SQLite and through a sealed envelope; a mutation control (15.9). A field of its own is the cleaner form: it is a later task, taken at the next rise of `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` that changes the segment row's shape for another reason (15.12), and not part of this one.
- Everything rule 4a compares (`pass`, `text_edited_at`, `updated_at`) is as it was for the tombstone that kept its text, so rule 4 gives the answers it gave (15.5).

### 15.2 The predicate and the one function

**One device runs a meeting's passes.** Only the device that holds a meeting's recordings runs its final pass or a re-transcription:

- Audio file rows are NeverSync (`AudioFile`, `crates/earshot-core/src/model/entities.rs`: "audio never leaves the device"; `AudioFileRepo` rows are hard-deleted and never pushed). `host::final_pass` reads its tracks through `load_post_input` from this device's audio rows and files, and returns `HostPost::NothingToProcess` when there are none.
- A re-transcription (`edit::retranscribe_with`) runs the same `host::final_pass` under `lease_meeting_alone(Processing)`, and the app offers it only while `MeetingDetail.audio_kept` (a row whose file exists here).
- The worker builds the runtime without `engines` and runs no pass.

**The owner of a meeting** is the device named by the meeting row's `SyncMeta.origin_device_id` (`crates/earshot-core/src/identity/sync.rs`, set from the stamping device when the row is created and kept by every later write and by sync). The live session creates the meeting row on the device that records it, in the same session that creates the FLAC writers, so the owner is the device whose audio rows the pass reads. The owner is chosen over "a device holding audio rows" because audio rows are deleted by retention (`retention::run`) while the meeting row's origin never changes: an owner whose audio is gone must still be able to clear its draft in the sweep (15.6), and a computer that never held the audio must never clear it. A store's own device id is fixed for its life (`device_id` in local state), so the owner stays the owner across restarts and updates; a new store joining the vault gets a new id and owns nothing it pulls.

Only the owner decides that a draft is replaced (P2), and only the owner clears what its pass replaced: in its pass and in its sweep. Every other device applies the owner's cleared rows and clears a tombstone that still holds text when it pulls one (15.4, 15.6). Any device may stamp again a cleared row it already holds, with its content unchanged, under "Lines go out before what they replace" below: re-queueing after an epoch reset or on `queue_everything` queues cleared rows a non-owner pulled, and the check must be free to move them above its lines. A re-stamp decides nothing: it never turns a live row into a cleared one. A device never decides from what it has pulled that another device's draft is replaced, also when the owner has left the vault (the owner, 2026-11-27, 15.11 e).

**Why only the owner.** A 0.9.0 owner can still re-transcribe beside updated computers (15.10). If any of them cleared its draft and its new run then found nothing for every track (a one-track meeting, or a run that heard nothing anywhere), 0.9.0's `run_reporting` would tombstone every unedited final line, and the meeting would show nothing on every computer for good. With the owner rule a 0.9.0 owner's draft is never cleared, so such a meeting shows its draft everywhere, as it would on 0.9.0 alone. A run that empties one track while another keeps final lines leaves that track showing nothing whatever this rule does, because the view is meeting-wide (live final lines, else the live draft, `shown_lines_of` in `crates/earshot-core/src/model/repo.rs`); that is 0.9.0's own behaviour, and this rule does not make it worse.

**Trust.** A pulled meeting row's origin device is taken as it arrives; nothing checks it against the device that pushed it. A vault peer that sent a wrong origin naming this device could make this device's sweep clear the draft of a meeting a 0.9.0 computer recorded. That is accepted under the vault's trust model (section 2: every device holding the vault key can already write any row, a meeting deletion included).

The other writers of segment rows are a user's edit, move or split (on any device: text changes in place and no line is tombstoned), rule 4b (on any device: it tombstones a line that lost all its words under an edited line of the same track) and a meeting deletion's erasures. 0.9.0 computers are the remaining source of copies that hold replaced text: their passes leave replaced lines with their text, and they push them as long as they sync.

In a meeting that does not read deleted here, a segment row is **replaced** when:

- **P1** it is a tombstone that holds text (either pass): a line a final pass or rule 4b took out; or
- **P2** this device is the meeting's owner, and the row is a `pass = Live` row that is not a tombstone, whose track is a live Track row of the same meeting, and that track has at least one live `pass = Final` row of the same meeting here: the draft of a track whose final lines are shown. In SQL the owner test compares the meeting row's origin device with the store's device id, the final row is matched on meeting and track together, and the track through the `track` table on its id and meeting, so a row that names another meeting's track never makes a draft replaced.

P2 tests for a live final row, never a tombstone, because that is what the view tests: a meeting shows its live final lines, else its live draft. A track whose final lines are all tombstones keeps its draft, which the meeting shows when no track has a live final line.

`Store::clear_replaced_text(meeting, limit)` is the only code that applies the predicate: it selects up to `limit` rowids by it in SQL and writes each in the cleared form; it returns the count and whether more remain. In a meeting that reads deleted here it writes the erasure form instead, to every row that holds text. It is idempotent: a second call writes nothing. On a device that is not the meeting's owner it can match only P1.

**Writers of draft rows.** The invariant, on every device of this release and of 0.9.0: a live `pass = Live` row is written only by the live session while it records, and never carries a speaker. `store_live` is the only shipped code that writes a `pass = Live` segment row, and its segments come from the live worker with `speaker_id: None` (15.1). Every other shipped writer of segment rows leaves drafts alone: `edit_segment`, `move_line`, `split_line`, `use_word` and `put_back_word` take Final rows only (`final_line`); rule 4a orders, and 4b rewrites, Final rows only (`edits_win`, `crates/earshot-store/src/sync.rs`); and `edit::merge_speakers` rewrites only rows that name the merged speaker (`speaker_id == Some(merge)`), which no draft row does. So after a meeting's recording no device writes a live draft row with a new stamp, and a cleared draft, stamped by the owner after its final lines and so after every draft stamp, wins over every live copy by plain `updated_at`, at the relay and on every device, with no rule of its own. Re-queueing (`queue_every_row`, `queue_everything`) pushes a live draft with its stored stamp, which loses. Other code writes existing `pass = Live` rows without making a draft or giving one a speaker, and is named as such: `apply_remote` writes pulled rows as they arrive (the owner's own drafts and cleared rows); `clear_replaced_text` and the clearing of a pulled tombstone write the cleared form (no speaker); a meeting deletion's erasures (`delete_meeting`, core `model/repo.rs`, `speaker_id = None`) write the erasure form; `merge_speakers`' `put_segment` writes only rows behind its `speaker_id != Some(merge)` filter. Pinned by a scan test, `edit_draft_rows_are_written_only_by_the_live_session`, over shipped sources (the files section 7's scan reads), which fails on (a) any construction of a `pass = Live` segment row outside `store_live`: `SegmentRow::new` with `TranscriptPass::Live` as its pass, a `pass: TranscriptPass::Live` field in a struct literal, or an assignment `.pass = TranscriptPass::Live` (reads, comparisons and filter arguments such as `live_segments(.., TranscriptPass::Live)` are not constructions); and (b) any assignment to a segment's `speaker_id` with a value other than `None` outside the named sites: the final pass's labelling of its own Final rows (`post`, `diar::assign`), `move_line` (after `final_line`), and `merge_speakers`, where the scan also requires the `speaker_id != Some(merge)` guard in the same loop. Its mutation controls: the scan run over a copy of the sources with one added `SegmentRow::new(.., TranscriptPass::Live, ..)` outside `store_live` fails, and so does one with `merge_speakers`' guard removed. Also pinned by a live session test with speaker work on (`live_draft_rows_carry_no_speaker`: every `pass = Live` row a session stores has no speaker). A change that gave draft rows a speaker, or a second writer, would let `merge_speakers` write a live draft after the clearing and bring its text back everywhere; it needs this rule revisited first. `Repo::replace_segments` (core `model/repo.rs`, no shipped caller) would write tombstones back as live rows; the scan test of section 7 already keeps it out of shipped code, and this rule relies on that.

- **Monotone.** A cleared row matches neither part again (no text; a tombstone), and nothing writes a draft row after its recording. Text returns to a final row only as a live line (rule 4a, or a later write to a live copy elsewhere, as before this rule), which P1 does not match. On the owner, a track that has a live final line keeps one until the meeting is deleted, with one exception: an edit, move or split changes a line in place and removes none, rule 4b never drops an edited line and drops a line only under one, and a pass leaves a track alone when its recogniser heard nothing there (below). The exception is a pass whose recogniser heard words on the mic that were all echo: it replaces the mic's lines with none (15.11, provisional answer 1). P2 then stops holding for that track, but its draft was cleared when it first had final lines, so nothing is left to clear. A 0.9.0 owner is not bound by any of this, which is why only the owner clears drafts.
- **Copies converge without stamping each other.** The function writes a row only while the predicate holds for the copy here. A cleared copy pulled from another device is applied by its stamp like any row (15.4) and does not match, so it is never written back. Two devices that clear the same tombstone before hearing of each other (P1 only: P2 is the owner's alone) write the same content, because the cleared form keeps only values both copies share; the later stamp wins at the relay and on both. So a device writes a given row at most once per text-bearing copy it meets, also beside a peer that never clears.

It runs in two places and nowhere else:

1. **The final pass**, on the owner, last in its write transaction, for its own meeting, to completion. Every stamp it writes is later than the pass's final lines. A pass that fails or is cancelled writes nothing, so nothing is cleared. In the final pass a store error fails the pass like any other.
2. **The one-off sweep** (15.6), for rows stored in the old form: P1 in every meeting, P2 in the meetings this device owns.

No clearing runs after a pull, and no record lists meetings waiting to be cleared: a receiver gets the owner's cleared rows in the owner's own push, after the lines that replace them (below).

Two further writers, both of a single row through the same statement and inside the transaction that writes the row: rule 4b's tombstone of a line that lost all its words (section 7, any device, in the page's transaction), and a pulled tombstone that holds text (15.4).

**Lines go out before what they replace.** The outbox pushes rows by their current `updated_at`, oldest first (`Store::outbox_page`, `crates/earshot-store/src/sync.rs`), and the relay files rows in the order they are pushed. Any later write of a line stamps it again (`edit_segment`, `move_line`, `split_line`, `use_word`, `put_back_word`, rule 4a's re-stamp, rule 4b's rewrite), and re-queueing (`queue_every_row` after an epoch reset, `queue_everything` on joining) queues every row with its stored stamp. So a line written after the clearing could go out after the cleared rows, and a receiver that completed a pull between those push pages would release its hold (15.4) and show nothing. The invariant, per track, for every device of this release, promised for the cleared rows that replace lines of this release's passes: those this device's pass or sweep P2 wrote, and an owner's cleared rows this device holds and queues again (a re-queue or a re-stamp). It is not promised for a P1 clearing of a tombstone this device pulled or swept that a 0.9.0 pass wrote: the 0.9.0 computer pushed that tombstone without the invariant, and clearing it changes no shown line here or on a receiver, which already holds the tombstone or holds it by H2. The check below cannot tell the kinds apart (both are cleared tombstones) and treats every queued cleared row of the track alike; for a P1 row that costs at most one re-stamp with its content unchanged and promises nothing. **This device pushes such a cleared tombstone of a track only after the relay holds a copy of a live final line of that track that this device holds live: one this device pulled or already pushed, or one sent earlier in the same push.** It says nothing of lines another device takes out later (a later pass on the owner, rule 4b), which reach a receiver as tombstones of their own and are covered by H2 (15.4).
- **The check.** For a meeting and track with cleared tombstones queued here (`deleted = 1`, holding no text, confidence 1.0), the invariant holds when some live `pass = Final` row of that track here is either not queued (it came from the relay or has been pushed to it) or queued, sendable, and stamped below the lowest stamp of the track's queued cleared rows. A queued line is sendable when its stored values convert (`repo::synced_by_id_or_damaged` reads it) and it seals: its plaintext passes `check_plaintext` (`crates/earshot-sync/src/guard.rs`: not over 192 KiB, no audio signature), the check the push makes before sealing. A line that does not convert stays queued and is never pushed (`Store::outbox_page` leaves it out and reports it as unsendable); a line the guard refuses is skipped by the push while the rest of the page goes (`SealError::Refused`, `continue`, in the push loop of `crates/earshot-runtime/src/sync/engine.rs`). Neither counts, however it is stamped. The store does not depend on earshot-sync, so the check is given the push's own guard as a function (the same seam for every caller of the check), never a copy of its rule; a test pins that the check and the push judge the same rows refused. A track whose only live final lines here are unsendable cannot meet the invariant, and stamping again does not help: its cleared rows go out, and a receiver shows no line for that track (listed in 15.10). When it does not hold, the track's queued cleared rows are stamped again, above every row the transaction wrote. Rows already pushed are never touched.
- **When it runs.** At the end of every transaction, before it commits, for each meeting and track of which the transaction wrote a live `pass = Final` row that the outbox queues. Both write paths note the track: `StoreTx::put_segment` (the edits, the final pass) and `repo::put_synced` (rule 4a's re-stamp and rule 4b's rewrite in `apply_remote`, which also write through the outbox when they stamp a row of their own). Neither is exempt: a 4a re-stamp can re-queue a local edit that was never pushed, so it is the same case. A pulled row that `apply_remote` writes without queuing it is not noted. Re-queueing runs the check for every meeting and track with queued cleared rows, in the transaction that re-queues.
- **Cost.** The check is two indexed lookups per noted track. The re-stamp happens only when every live line of the track still queued here was written after its queued cleared rows and none has reached the relay: in practice once, when a user rewrites every line of a track after its pass while offline, or after a re-queue. After a re-stamp the cleared rows are above every line, so later writes of those lines need no re-stamp until all of them are written again. At most one re-stamp of a track's queued cleared rows per full rewrite of its lines (a track of one meeting hour holds about 500 draft rows), never one per `use_word` click.
- A row stamped again keeps its content. In rule 4a's order a later stamp can only make a cleared final tombstone win over a concurrent unedited copy of the same line (which it replaced), never over an edit (`text_edited_at` is compared first); a cleared draft stamped again only moves further above the live draft copies it already beats (above, "Writers of draft rows").

**A side the pass found nothing on.** Judged per track, on this run's recogniser lines only, never on the lines stored here:

- A track found nothing when this run's segments for it (`Transcription::into_segments`), after the words under that track's edited final lines are dropped by `earshot_core::asr::drop_words_under_edits` (the function `label_tracks` uses, `crates/earshot-runtime/src/post/mod.rs`) and before echo cleaning, hold no text. Words that lie only under an edited line therefore never count as found: without that, a run whose only new words fell under an edited line would replace the track's other lines.
- Both tracks are judged the same way, before echo cleaning. A mic whose recogniser heard words that echo cleaning then removes in full has found something: the run is positive evidence that the side held only echo, and it replaces the mic's lines as normal, possibly with none (15.11, provisional answer 1).
- Edited lines play no part in the judgement. They are read from the store and kept either way: the write transaction of `PostProcessor::run_reporting` splits a processed track's earlier final rows into `edited`, kept, and `replaced`, tombstoned.
- A track that found nothing and has at least one live `pass = Final` row here, edited or not, is **kept**: the pass treats it as a track it did not process. It leaves `processed`, so all its final rows fall into `kept` and their speakers into `kept_speakers`; its diarisation is replaced by the empty one in the policy (`two_track(mic, &none)` or `two_track(&none, lb)`, as for a recorded track that is not processed); and `full` is computed over the tracks the pass replaces, not over `input.tracks`, so it is false whenever a recorded track is kept and no speaker of the kept track is tombstoned. Its live records (`live-stretches/*`, `live-speakers/*`) are deleted as for every track of the input. It reports `PostWarning::EarlierLinesKept { track }` (banner `post-kept-lines`, fixed copy), which a re-transcription carries on its terminal `JobEvent::Retranscribed`.
- A track that found nothing and has no live final row here has nothing to keep and is processed as today: it gets no line, and its draft stays, because P2 does not match it (15.11 a and b).
- **When every track of the input is kept**, the meeting changes no line and its write transaction writes nothing but this: it deletes the live records of every track and runs `clear_replaced_text` (which finds nothing in a meeting already in the new form and changes no shown line). It writes no segment or speaker row and leaves `speaker-identities/<meeting>`, `voiceprint-me/<meeting>` (today deleted when no Me was named, `post/mod.rs` near the `voiceprint_me_key` match), `unknown-lines/<meeting>`, `word-choices/<meeting>`, the pass backend record, the echo locks and the retention clock as they were, and enrols no voice. The other tracks of a pass that keeps one are replaced as usual. A recogniser can return nothing for a bad run, and replacing the lines would now be for good (15.11 c).
- **The decision is made once.** The kept tracks are decided from what the `early` read of T14.48 finds before the transaction, which this rule widens: for each processed track, its edited final lines (for the words dropped under them) and whether it has any live `pass = Final` row at all, unedited included (for "has lines to keep"). The transaction reads both again. If either changed for any track (the edited lines, or a track gaining or losing its last live final row) and the decision for that track would differ, the pass fails with `RuntimeError::Conflict` and writes nothing, because the echo cleaning below already used the earlier decision. In this process that cannot happen: pulled rows of a leased meeting are held `busy`, and line edits are refused `MeetingBusy` under the lease.

**Echo against a kept reference (15.11 d).** The reference is judged right after transcription, before `clean_echo` runs. When it is kept, `clean_echo` gives `clean_mic_echo` (`MicEcho::reference`) the words of the reference track's live final rows here, in time order, in place of this run's reference words (there are none). They are read in the same short store lock as the edited lines of the `early` read. `MicEcho::ref_diar` stays this run's reference diarisation, whose turns come from the audio, so the text rule, the speaker check and `drop_echo_clusters` all run as they do today. A kept line whose text was edited has no words (`edit_segment` clears them) and adds nothing; a moved or split line keeps its words. When the mic is kept and the reference is replaced, nothing is cleaned against the kept mic lines: echo runs from the far end into the mic, and the kept mic lines were cleaned in their own run. A change to the kept reference rows between that read and the write transaction is covered by the conflict rule above when it changes an edited line, and otherwise accepted (15.10). Such a run has `full` false, as above.

### 15.3 Readers

- Cleared rows are tombstones, so readers of live rows need no change: `MeetingView::load` and `shown_lines` (search, export) read live final rows, else live draft rows. `has_live_segment`, the meeting's activity, the retention clock, recovery, `summary_out_of_date`, `store_summary`'s segment check, offload's `segment_count` and re-transcribe (offered by `audio_kept`) read the lines the meeting shows, final rows or device-local records; none reads the text of a replaced row. A re-transcription reads the stored audio, never the draft, and is not offered once the audio is gone.
- One reader changes: live summary reuse (`summary::live_record`) needs each draft row's id, track, times and speaker to move a window's citations and owners onto the final lines. It reads the meeting's `pass = Live` rows whatever `deleted` says; the cleared form of a draft row keeps all it uses, and its speaker is the none it always had (15.1).
- Readers inside a pulled page's transaction (`word_choices::drop_stale_tx`, rule 4b's `edits_win`) read segments through `StoreTx::segments_that_read`, leaving damaged rows out. A word choice on a row that does not read is dropped for good: a later readable copy of that row does not get it back. That is accepted, because a row that does not read is never shown (built in T14.47).
- As built (T14.47): the speaker scan of 15.2 allows a copy of a segment's own `speaker_id` onto a rebuilt segment (`x.speaker_id = y.speaker_id`) only in named functions, and checks struct literals too; the shown transcript is meeting-wide, so the relay tests assert that a meeting never shows nothing and that no track loses lines it already showed; every writer stamps through `Db::parts()` and calls `StoreTx::send_lines_first`, and a commit that leaves a track needing a re-stamp is a debug assertion.
- `word-choices/<meeting>` holds single words of lines. The final pass already drops stale entries in its transaction (`drop_stale_tx`); the sync client does the same in the page's transaction (`ApplyRules::after`) for every meeting the page carries a segment row of. This replaces what section 7's "What does not follow" says of word choices (left until the next local write); that text is changed when this is built. `live-stretches/*` and `live-speakers/*` are already deleted by the pass. `unknown-lines`, `summary-names`, `speaker-identities` and summary citations hold ids, names or vectors and are unchanged.

### 15.4 `apply_remote`

Text-bearing copies keep arriving while any 0.9.0 computer syncs. The relay refuses only a build whose envelope format is below the vault's (`crates/earshot-sync-server/src/db.rs`), so 0.4.2 and older (format 2) are refused and 0.9.0 (format 4, `crates/earshot-sync/src/seal.rs` at v0.9.0) is not; nothing in this rule raises the format. Rows that 0.4.2 pushed before it was refused also remain on the relay. The per-row steps of 5.3 are as they were, with these additions:

- **A pulled tombstone that holds text** (step 6, either pass, when it is the copy to write): the incoming stamp is observed first, as rule 4a does, then the row is written and cleared through the statement of 15.1, in the page's transaction. Its new stamp is therefore later than the pulled one whatever this device's clock reads, so it goes out and replaces the relay's copy. Any device does this, owner or not: the row is already a tombstone, so clearing it changes nothing shown, and its 0.9.0 writer may never clear it.
- **In a meeting that reads deleted here,** a pulled segment tombstone that holds text and is the copy to write is written in the erasure form, after the incoming stamp is observed, so it goes out once and replaces the relay's copy. A pulled tombstone that holds no text (a cleared row or an erasure) is applied as it arrives, with its own stamp, never stamped again here: it holds no words, and its speaker is already none (15.1). Only a text-bearing copy is ever rewritten, and the rewrite holds no text, so two devices that both hold the deletion never send a row back and forth. Live rows of a deleted meeting are refused as before (5.3).
- **Two holds keep a receiver from showing nothing where it showed lines.** Both hold the row as `parent`. No new hold word is added, because 0.9.0 refuses a `sync_pending` row whose word it does not know (`HoldReason::of_word`), and a store can be opened by 0.9.0 after a downgrade.
  - **H1, a cleared draft.** A `pass = Live` tombstone that holds no text and is not an erasure, arriving over a live draft row here whose track has no live final row of its meeting here.
  - **H2, a final line taken out.** A `pass = Final` tombstone that is not an erasure and is the copy to write by rule 4a, arriving over a live final row here that is the last live final row of its track here.
  - Both match the track as P2 does (meeting and track together, the track a Track row of that meeting) and apply only in a meeting live here; a row naming a track of another meeting is not held and cannot hide anything. A local segment row that does not read is treated as absent, both as the row the incoming copy would replace and when counting a track's other live final rows: an incoming copy over a row that does not read is applied without a hold (that row is not shown), and H2 may hold a tombstone over the last line that reads though rows that do not read remain. Why both holds are needed: the relay files a row at the end each time it changes, so a later re-transcription's cleared rows can come before its new lines. Re-transcription 1 replaces F0 with F1 and re-transcription 2 replaces F1 with F2; the relay then serves cleared F0, F2 and cleared F1, in that order, and a receiver that still held F0 live would show nothing between the first two pages without H2. H1 is the same for the draft after a first pass.
- **The retry.** A track's first live final row here counts in `Applied::parents_written`, so the end-of-tick `parent` retry (section 6.2) applies held H1 and H2 rows in the tick that brings the lines.
- **The release (the bound on both holds).** At the end of a tick that ends `pull_complete`, after the retry, a row held by H1 or H2 is applied whatever its track then shows, unless a row that could still give its track a live final row is held too: a `pass = Final` live row of that meeting and track in `sync_pending`, held `busy`, `parent` or `clock`. Why that is enough: by "lines go out before what they replace" (15.2), a device on this release pushes a cleared tombstone of a track only after the relay holds a copy of a live final line of that track that the device held live, and that holds after edits and after re-queueing. A pull that completes reads the relay to its end, so once a pull has completed after a held row arrived, every line the relay held when that row was pushed is here or held, unless a later tombstone of its own replaced it (which H2 judges in turn) (a row held `format` cannot be a line of format 4 or below). A row is held this way at most until the end of the next tick that completes its pull, unless its replacing lines are themselves held. Those lines are already counted (`Store::held_count`), so these holds add no hold that outlasts every other: the ignore copy's condition (`copy_after_tick` in `crates/earshot-runtime/src/sync/thread.rs`: held 0 and no `busy` or `parent` row waiting) is met as soon as it would have been without them. What the release lets through is a 0.9.0 owner's re-transcription that found nothing for a track, a 0.9.0 push cut between its tombstones and its lines (0.9.0 does not stamp cleared rows again, 15.10), or lines lost on the way; the meeting then shows what the rows say. For a 0.9.0 owner's meeting, whose draft nobody cleared, that is its draft when no track has a live final line left, and otherwise the remaining tracks' final lines with nothing for the emptied track, as on 0.9.0 alone (15.2, "Why only the owner"). At the release a `parent` row counts as an H1 or H2 row when it decodes as a segment tombstone whose meeting, track and (when named) speaker rows are present here; any other `parent` row waits for its parent as before.
- `Applied::text_emptied` counts the segment rows whose copy here held text before the page and holds none after it (written in the cleared form, applied as a cleared copy over a copy that held text, or erased), never a row whose text only changed (a pulled edit). Counts of rows, never content.

### 15.5 Rule 4 gives the same answers

Computer A edits line L. Computer B, the owner (only it can re-transcribe, 15.2), which has not seen the edit, re-transcribes: L becomes a cleared tombstone on B (no `text_edited_at`), with new lines of another run over its stretch.

- On A, B's tombstone arrives. Both copies are final and neither is an erasure, so rule 4a compares `text_edited_at` first: A's edit stays, stamped again when B's stamp is later. B's new lines apply and 4b drops their words under the edit.
- On B, A's edit arrives. By the same comparison it replaces the cleared tombstone whatever the stamps (also after being held): L is live again with the user's text, 4b drops B's words under it (a line that loses all its words is cleared) and 4c revives its speaker.
- Either push order, and a third device that receives both, ends with the same rows: the edit live, B's remaining lines live, every dropped line cleared. Had the cleared tombstone been an erasure, B would have kept it whenever its stamp was later and the edit would be lost. That is why 15.1 keeps the two apart. Stamping B's queued cleared rows again (15.2) changes none of this: rule 4a compares `text_edited_at` before `updated_at`.
- A split and a move set `text_edited_at` and are kept the same way. A meeting deletion still wins everywhere: its erasures are ordered by `updated_at`, and the pulled meeting tombstone deletes the meeting here.

### 15.6 Meetings stored before this rule

Every finished meeting holds its whole draft with text, and each re-transcription left its replaced lines. `earshot_runtime::replaced_text::sweep` (a module the worker's build has too) clears them once per store, on its own thread, in the app and in the worker's `run`: P1 in every meeting, and P2 only in the meetings this device owns (15.2).

- **Why once is enough.** Every text-bearing tombstone pulled after the sweep is cleared as it arrives (15.4). A draft can be cleared only by its owner, and an owner's drafts are in its own store from the moment they are recorded: pulling rows never brings a device a draft it owns that it did not already hold. A draft that an updated device's old outbox pushes after updating was recorded by that device, so its own sweep, which runs at its first start of this release, clears it. Joining or setting up a vault, or turning sync on, brings only rows of meetings this device does not own, whose text-bearing tombstones are cleared as they arrive. So nothing ever needs to ask for a second sweep. An owner that leaves the vault changes nothing here: its meetings' drafts are never cleared by another device (15.2, 15.11 e).
- **The record.** Device record `replaced-text-swept` (`DeviceRecord::ReplacedTextSwept`, written only through `local::set_tx`, framed with `encode_versioned` at version 1, payload JSON `{"done": true}`), pinned by a `*_bytes_are_pinned` test. Absent means the sweep is still to run. An unreadable value (another version, damaged JSON) means the same, is reported as a count (never its bytes) and is replaced when the sweep ends. The sweep is its only writer and nothing removes it, so no other thread can race it; it is written in its own `Store::immediate` transaction at the end of a pass with nothing left, nothing skipped and nothing failed.
- **Start.** With no vault, or sync off: at once. With sync running: after the first tick that ends `pull_complete`, so tombstones another device already cleared arrive first and need no write here; or 10 minutes after start (a judgement), whichever is first, so local erasure never waits on a relay that cannot be reached.
- **Work.** Not a migration, which would hold the write lock for its whole run. Meetings in an order of this device's own (sorted by a hash of the device id and the meeting id), so two devices that update together seldom work on the same meeting's tombstones at once; `clear_replaced_text` with at most 500 rows a transaction, then `flush_erasures`, then a pause (2 s, a judgement, through an injected pause). It writes nothing while any Recording lease is held. A meeting leased here is skipped and tried again every 60 s after the pass over the rest. It takes no lease and no `WorkPass`: no job reads the rows it writes for their text. Each transaction writes cleared rows of one meeting, so "lines go out before what they replace" (15.2) needs nothing here: an owner's draft is cleared only on a track whose final lines it already holds, and those lines were queued or pushed when they were written.
- **Giving way to sync.** Both waits apply only while the sync thread is running and its last tick succeeded; with sync off, stopped or failing the sweep goes on. It waits while more than 5,000 rows are waiting to send, counted as the push counts them (`outbox_len_except` with the push's own exclusions): the push goes oldest stamp first, so a long queue of cleared rows would hold up a new edit. And it waits while the last tick had `text_emptied > 0` (15.4): another device is clearing tombstones, its rows are arriving, and each one already cleared needs no write here.
- **End.** A meeting that fails with a store error is counted, left as it was, and the sweep goes on with the others. A pass with a failure does not write the record, so the next start sweeps again, which costs one probe per meeting when nothing is left. Stopping anywhere is safe.
- **Traffic.** The cleared rows are pushed like any change: that is what replaces the sealed text at the relay. A draft row is cleared once, by its owner. A tombstone that holds text can be cleared by every device that holds it before a cleared copy reaches it: with D devices and N such tombstones, the usual case is N rows up once and N down on each other device; at worst D times N up and D(D-1) times N down, the worker included. What bounds it: the per-device order and the second wait above, so a tombstone is sent twice only when two devices cleared its meeting within one tick of each other. N is not measured: about 500 draft rows per meeting hour and 1 KB per sealed cleared row would make 200 meeting hours about 100,000 rows and 100 MB, at up to 10,000 rows a tick. The sweep reports its rows and failed meetings as counts.

### 15.7 The relay

A push with a later stamp replaces the row's sealed body (`ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE`), so once a cleared row is pushed the relay serves no copy of the old text. The relay's database does not zero freed pages, so the old sealed bytes can stay in its file and write-ahead log until that space is reused. They open only with the vault key, which the relay never holds.

### 15.8 Erasing from the file

`secure_delete` zeroes the old text in the database pages when a row is rewritten. The write-ahead log keeps the old page until a truncating checkpoint (`Store::flush_erasures`, best effort: a `Busy` leaves it to the next one or to the closing checkpoint).

- The final pass already runs it right after its write transaction, before `Stored` is reported: no new wait.
- The sync client runs it once at the end of a tick in which a page had `text_emptied > 0` (which counts the pulled tombstones it cleared and the rule 4b tombstones it wrote), however the tick ends (a failed request, a stop, a tick that does not end `pull_complete`).
- The sweep runs it after each transaction.
- A user's text edit (`edit_segment`, `use_word`, `put_back_word`) asks for one; a single debounced flush runs 5 s after the last request (a judgement), never inside the edit and never once per click.

### 15.9 Tests

- core `model`: `holds_text`; a cleared row is not an erasure (from confidence 0, -0 and NaN, with and without a speaker) and an erasure is; `edit_order` of a cleared tombstone against an edit, a live line, another tombstone and an erasure, both ways.
- scan: `edit_draft_rows_are_written_only_by_the_live_session` (15.2, "Writers of draft rows": no construction of a `pass = Live` segment row outside `store_live`, and no non-`None` `speaker_id` assignment outside the named sites, `merge_speakers` with its guard), with its two mutation controls (an added construction, the guard removed); `replace_segments` stays out of shipped code (section 7's scan).
- runtime `live`: `live_draft_rows_carry_no_speaker` (a two-track session with speaker work on stores only `pass = Live` rows with no speaker); `merge_speakers` of a speaker with final lines writes no draft row (it moves only rows that name the speaker).
- store: the predicate case by case (P1; P2 per track; P2 on a device that is not the meeting's owner never matches, also with audio rows copied in by hand; a track without final rows; a track whose final rows are all tombstones keeps its draft; a deleted meeting; a live final row whose track is a Track row of another meeting, and one naming no Track row, never make a draft replaced); the SQL and `holds_text` agree; no cleared row has a speaker, also when the row had one; a damaged row is cleared, reads and syncs afterwards; a second call writes nothing; the limit; 1.0 and 0.0 exact through SQLite and through a sealed envelope; "lines go out before what they replace": after a pass's transaction, rewriting every line of a track by an edit, a move, a split, `use_word`, `put_back_word`, a rule 4a re-stamp (through `put_synced`) and a rule 4b rewrite (through `put_synced`) each leaves the invariant holding at commit; rewriting all but one line stamps nothing again (the cost bound), and a hundred `use_word` writes on one line stamp the track's cleared rows again at most once; `queue_every_row` and `queue_everything` after such edits leave it holding; a pulled line written by `apply_remote` without queuing stamps nothing again; a queued line that does not convert never counts as going first (beside one sendable line stamped above the cleared rows, the cleared rows are stamped again above it; its control, the same line readable, stamps nothing); the same for a queued line the push's guard refuses (over 192 KiB, and one carrying an audio signature), and, through `LocalRelay`, a track whose only queued live line is refused while an earlier copy of another line of the track is on the relay counts that other line and leaves the receiver showing lines, while one with no other line is the limit of 15.10 (pinned as such); the check and the push judge the same rows refused; a re-stamp that runs while a push page holding the track's cleared rows is in flight (sealed, not yet acknowledged): the page's acknowledgement does not dequeue the re-stamped rows (their stamp changed), they go again above the lines, and a receiver pulling between the two pushes shows the draft or the lines, never nothing; rows already pushed are never touched; and only the track whose lines were written is checked.
- runtime `post`: a first pass and a re-transcription leave no tombstone that holds text and no draft row on a track with live final lines; every clearing stamp is later than every final line's; a track without final lines keeps its draft; a pass with no final line leaves the draft shown; a re-transcription that finds nothing for one track keeps that track's lines and speakers, replaces the other's and gives its warning on the terminal event; one that finds nothing at all changes no line and no speaker row and leaves `speaker-identities`, `voiceprint-me` (an in-room meeting with a voiceprint Me, re-run finding nothing), `unknown-lines`, `word-choices`, the pass backend record and the retention clock byte for byte as they were, deleting only the live records; a re-transcription that finds nothing on a track holding one edited line and two unedited lines keeps all three lines and their speakers and gives the warning (its control, the same track with one recogniser line outside the edited line, replaces the two unedited lines and keeps the edited one);
