a re-transcription whose only new words on a track lie under that track's edited line keeps the track (its control, one more word outside the edited line, replaces it); a mic whose recogniser heard words that echo cleaning removed in full replaces the mic's earlier lines with none, and a mic whose recogniser heard nothing keeps them; with the reference kept, new mic words that repeat the kept reference lines' words at the lock delay are removed and the same words not repeated there are kept, and a negative control with the kept lines' words withheld leaves them in; with a track kept, `full` is false and no speaker row of the kept track is tombstoned; with the mic kept and the reference replaced, the kept mic lines are unchanged; an edited line changed between the early read and the transaction so that a kept decision flips fails the pass with a conflict and writes nothing, and so does a track that loses its last unedited live final row in between (its control, a change that flips no decision, passes); an edited line is kept; a failed and a cancelled pass change nothing; live summary partials are still reused after the draft is cleared, with the same citations and owners as before the clearing; word choices of replaced lines are gone.
- store `sync` (straight pages) and runtime `sync` (through `LocalRelay`): the rule 4 tests of section 7 unchanged; edit against re-transcription in both push orders and with a third device, a held edit, a split and a move, each also asserting that no replaced row holds text on any device; the mutation control (the cleared form written with confidence 0 must fail the edit test); what a receiver shows after each prefix of the owner's push and between the page with the final lines and the page with the cleared draft (never nothing where it showed lines); a cleared draft held (H1) until its final lines apply, applied by the retry in the tick that brings them, after which the receiver has queued no row of that meeting; the owner edits every line of a track after its pass and before its push, and the push is cut at every page while a receiver completes a pull between pages (the receiver shows the draft or the lines at every step, never nothing); the same with a second re-transcription whose lines are edited before its push (H2); the same after an epoch reset re-queues everything, and after a join through `queue_everything`; a live draft copy re-queued with its stored stamp (an epoch reset, a join, a 0.9.0 peer's old outbox) loses to the owner's cleared copy at the relay and on every device; a non-owner and a 0.9.0 peer each merge a speaker of the meeting between the owner's lines and its cleared drafts arriving, and no draft row is written by either merge; in a meeting deleted here a pulled text-bearing tombstone is written as an erasure and goes out once, a pulled cleared row and a pulled erasure are applied with their own stamps and nothing is queued for them, and two devices that both hold the deletion and exchange each of these send no row back after the first exchange; the relay order cleared F0, F2, cleared F1 of two re-transcriptions, delivered to a receiver that held F0 live, the pull cut at every page (F0 or F2 shown at each step, never nothing; H2 holds cleared F0 until F2 applies), and to a receiver that held F1 live and one that held only the draft; the release: a track's last final line taken out by rows that bring no replacement is held only until the end of the next tick that ends `pull_complete`, after which `held_count` is 0 and the T12.93 ignore copy runs; a held row whose replacing final line is itself held `parent`, `busy` or `clock` is not released until that line applies; a tombstone over a local row that does not read is applied without a hold; a pulled tombstone that holds text and is the last final line of its track is held first and cleared when applied; a receiver never turns a live draft into a cleared one, in a tick or in its sweep (it only applies the owner's; a re-stamp of a cleared draft it holds, after an epoch reset or `queue_everything`, keeps the content and is allowed), and an owner whose push is cut after its final lines leaves the receiver showing the final lines with the hidden draft kept until the owner's next push; a pulled text-bearing tombstone stamped ahead of this device's clock still goes out cleared and is accepted by the relay; `text_emptied` counts a pulled cleared copy over a copy that held text and a cleared pulled tombstone, and does not count a pulled edit; a mixed pair (one device's rows written as 0.4.2 leaves them) converging to cleared tombstones, after which a device joining through the relay pulls no tombstone that holds replaced text; a peer that never clears (each row written once here, no loop); a damaged pulled tombstone that holds text (cleared, then canonicalised, and the page goes on); a meeting deletion before, after and across the pages of a clearing; a second apply of the same pages writes nothing; three devices in every order of two pages end with identical rows (on straight pages compared without stamps, since each device stamps its own clearing; through the relay with them).
- **A format-4 peer with no clearing rule** (through `LocalRelay`): a peer whose rows are written as 0.9.0 writes them (its pass tombstones every unedited line of a processed track with its text and leaves its draft live; it has no holds, no clearing and no re-stamp) records a meeting and syncs beside two updated devices. The updated devices clear the text of its tombstones and never its draft, also in their sweeps; it then re-transcribes a two-track meeting and finds nothing for one track, tombstoning that track's lines; after the release every device, the peer included, shows the other track's final lines and nothing for that track, as the peer alone would, and stays so after further ticks; it re-transcribes a one-track meeting and finds nothing, and after the release every device shows that meeting's draft, never nothing; its push cut between its tombstones and its lines leaves the updated devices showing the draft or the lines, never nothing, once the release has run; it applies the updated devices' cleared rows and pushes nothing back for them.
- The sweep: a store in the old form is cleared, drafts only in meetings this device owns; an old-form meeting owned by another device keeps its draft and has its text-bearing tombstones cleared; an old-form meeting whose final lines are all tombstones keeps its draft and still shows it; an owner whose audio retention already deleted clears its draft; stopped part way and run again; the start gate with and without a completed pull and at the cap; the record written only after a clean pass, its bytes pinned, an unreadable record sweeping again and replaced at the end; nothing written during a recording; a leased meeting done later; both waits, counted with the push's exclusions, and their release when a tick fails or sync is off; two devices sweeping together (tombstones sent twice stay within the bound of 15.6); a damaged row cleared; a failing meeting counted, the rest done and the record not written; a draft pushed from an updated device's old outbox is cleared by that device's own sweep; a second run writes nothing; a meeting whose owner was revoked from the vault keeps its draft on every other device, in their sweeps and ticks (the limit, pinned). Clock and pause injected.
- On disk: a needle in a replaced final line, in a draft row and in a word an edit removed is absent from the database file and its write-ahead log after the pass, the tick, the sweep and the debounced flush, with a second connection held open (the method of the existing erasure tests).
- `cargo xtask accept m11` unchanged: no threshold or harness edit.

### 15.10 Limits

What a 0.9.0 computer (format 4, without this rule) does beside updated ones, part of the accepted limit, as 7A.7 does for removals:

- It clears nothing. Its own passes leave the lines they replace as tombstones with their text, and its drafts stay live with their text. Updated computers clear the text of its tombstones as they pull them (15.4) and push the cleared copies, so the relay stops serving that text; they never clear its drafts (15.2), so the draft words of every meeting it recorded stay on every computer and on the relay until it updates and sweeps.
- It applies cleared rows from updated computers like any row: its `is_erasure` reads them as tombstones that are not erasures, and its rule 4a orders them as 15.5 does. It has no holds, so on a pull cut between pages it can show nothing for a track of an updated owner's meeting until the next page applies.
- As an owner it can still re-transcribe. A run that finds nothing for a track tombstones every unedited line of that track (0.9.0's `run_reporting`). Once H2 releases, every computer shows what that computer shows: the meeting's draft, which nobody cleared, when no track has a live final line left, and otherwise the other tracks' final lines with nothing for the emptied track, because the view is meeting-wide (15.2, "Why only the owner"). The second case is 0.9.0's own behaviour and this rule does not make it worse.
- It does not stamp its queued tombstones again when a line is written, so an edit after its pass can send its tombstones before its lines; an updated receiver holds the last line (H2) until a pull completes and may then show that track's draft, or nothing for that track when another track has final lines, until the lines arrive.
- A tombstone it holds may arrive cleared from an updated computer and be applied; it never clears one itself.
- It writes no draft row after a recording either: its live session is the only writer of draft rows, which carry no speaker, so its speaker merges and line edits never touch them (15.2, "Writers of draft rows"). An old draft copy it re-pushes carries its recording-time stamp and loses to the owner's cleared copy.

Release note line, word for word: "Update every computer that syncs. Until a 0.9.0 computer is updated, the words its own meetings replaced stay on every synced computer and on the sync server."

Other limits:

- A computer that never updates, or is offline, keeps what it has. So does a held row (`sync_pending` keeps its sealed body until a later copy applies).
- Backups and snapshots of a data folder or of the relay's volume taken before the clearing, and the relay's freed pages (15.7).
- Notes already exported to the vault keep the text they were written with until exported again.
- Summaries, their history and the live summary record (`live-summaries/<meeting>`, deleted when a summary is stored) are summary text, outside this rule. Speaker tombstones keep their names (rule 4c revives them).
- A draft row whose track has no live final row keeps its words though the meeting may show another track's final lines (15.11 b). A damaged draft row whose track does not read is never matched by P2.
- When a re-transcription's new lines are held on a receiver (a speaker row that cannot be opened) while the tombstones of the old lines apply, that receiver shows fewer lines of that track until they apply; H2 keeps the last one, so never none.
- A local final row that does not read is treated as absent by H1 and H2 (15.4). A track whose only other live final rows do not read can therefore show nothing after H2 applies the tombstone of its last line that reads; those rows were not shown before either.
- An owner whose push is cut after its final lines and before its cleared rows leaves the other devices showing the final lines with the replaced text kept hidden on them, and the relay holding its sealed copy, until it syncs again; its outbox keeps the cleared rows. If it never syncs again, they keep it (15.11, provisional 2).
- An owner that goes back to 0.9.0 and returns does not sweep again (its record says done): meetings it recorded or re-transcribed meanwhile keep their old-form drafts and lines until each is re-transcribed. Its text-bearing tombstones are still cleared by every device that pulls them.
- **Going back to 0.9.0 can blank a meeting.** An updated owner clears a meeting's draft, goes back to 0.9.0, re-transcribes and finds nothing for every track that has lines: 0.9.0's `run_reporting` tombstones every unedited final line, the draft was already cleared, so after H2's release the meeting shows nothing on every computer (a run that empties only one track leaves it showing nothing whatever the draft, as on 0.9.0 alone). It can be recovered only by transcribing again while the recordings are kept (on this release, or on 0.9.0 with a run that finds something). Going back is not supported; it is stated because nothing prevents it.
- **Owners that never clear (the accepted cost of 15.11 e).** A meeting whose owner is removed from the vault, reinstalled with a new device id, retired, or left on 0.9.0 for good, before it cleared that meeting, keeps its hidden replaced draft words on every computer and on the relay for good. Its shown lines are unaffected, and its text-bearing tombstones are still cleared by every device that pulls them. Deleting the meeting removes the words; transcribing it again cannot, because only the owner holds its recordings.
- A vault peer that sends a meeting row with a wrong origin naming another device can make that device's sweep clear the meeting's draft (accepted under the vault's trust model, 15.2 "Trust").
- A track whose only live final lines on the owner are queued and unsendable (they do not convert, or the push's guard refuses them: over 192 KiB, or an audio signature) cannot meet "lines go out before what they replace" (15.2): its cleared rows go out, and a receiver then shows no line for that track. For lines that do not convert the owner shows none either; a refused line the owner still shows, and no receiver ever gets it, with or without this rule.
- The rule assumes one owner per meeting (15.2). Restoring a copy of a data folder onto a second computer gives both the same device id; it is not a supported move. Both would then clear as owner, and the order argument behind the release (15.4) no longer holds for rows of the two.
- Echo against a kept reference uses the words of its live final lines: a line whose text was edited has none and removes nothing from the mic. A change to an unedited kept reference line made from another process on the same store between the pass's read and its write is not seen by the cleaning.
- Truncating the write-ahead log and zeroing pages does not wipe the disk's own freed blocks.

### 15.11 Decided by the owner (2026-11-26 and 2026-11-27)

The owner chose the safer variant of a, b and c, because the clearing cannot be undone, added d, and decided e on 2026-11-27. A meeting has a draft (the words shown while it records) and, after the clean-up that follows it, a final transcript. A call has two sides, recorded apart: your microphone and the other people.

- **a. The clean-up finds no speech at all.** The meeting then has no final transcript and goes on showing its draft. The rule: the draft is kept, words and all. The alternative: erase it, and the meeting shows no transcript.
- **b. The clean-up finds speech on one side only.** This is the usual case of a side with no final lines: you never spoke, or the only words the draft heard on that side were echo of the other side, which the clean-up removes. Less often that side's audio was lost. The meeting shows the final transcript of the side that has one; the other side's draft words are shown nowhere. The rule: those draft words are kept, on this computer and every synced one, though you cannot see, edit or remove them short of deleting the meeting. That keeps the only record of a side whose audio was lost, and it leaves in place exactly the kind of leftover words the decision set out to remove. The alternative: erase every side's draft once any side has a final transcript. Nothing shown changes and no hidden words remain, but a side whose audio was lost is then gone for good.
- **c. Transcribe again finds nothing on a side that had lines.** A recogniser can return nothing for a bad run. The rule, judged per side: that side keeps the lines it had, the other side gets its new lines, and the job ends as done with the notice "post-kept-lines" (`JobEvent::Retranscribed` carrying `PostWarning::EarlierLinesKept`). The alternatives: judge it over the whole meeting (keep everything only when no side finds anything), which erases for good a side that found nothing while the other found lines; or always replace, which can leave a meeting with no transcript at all. A side's own lines that you edited are kept whatever the run finds, and new words that fall only under them do not count as finding something (15.2).
- **d. A side is kept and the other side gets new lines.** The new lines are cleaned of echo against the kept side's lines (15.2, "Echo against a kept reference"). In practice this is the other people's side kept and your microphone's new lines cleaned of the other people's words that leaked into it.
- **e. A removed computer's meetings (decided 2026-11-27).** Only the computer that recorded a meeting ever erases its replaced draft words. A meeting recorded on a computer that is later removed from the vault, reinstalled or retired before it erased them keeps those hidden words on every synced computer and on the sync server for good (15.10, "Owners that never clear"); deleting the meeting removes them. Chosen over letting the other computers erase them once the recording computer is removed (the sixth revision's provisional answer 4), because that was the only place one computer erased another's data, and it could blank a meeting everywhere: a computer behind on its sync could erase a draft just before the recording computer's last transcription, which found nothing, arrived; a removed 0.9.0 computer paired again could do the same; and it trusted the sync server's device list.

Provisional, for the owner to confirm (each is written above as the recommendation):

1. **Your microphone heard only echo.** The rule judges each side on what the recogniser heard before echo is removed. A re-transcription that heard words on your microphone that all turn out to be echo of the other people replaces your side's earlier lines as normal, possibly with none; only a run that heard no words at all on your side keeps its earlier lines. Recommended, because hearing words that are all echo is evidence, not a failed run: if such a run kept the earlier lines, a meeting whose microphone side was filled with echo by an older echo canceller could never be cleaned by transcribing again. The cost: a run whose echo removal wrongly takes away all of your real words replaces your side with nothing, where judging after echo removal would have kept your earlier lines. It can be recovered by transcribing again while the recordings are kept (an echo-only result comes from the audio, so a later run with a better echo canceller can find your words again); once the recordings are deleted it cannot. The fourth revision recommended the opposite.
2. **The recording computer stops part way through sending.** Only the computer that recorded a meeting clears its replaced words. If it sends the new transcript and stops before sending the clearing, the other computers keep the hidden replaced words (and the relay their encrypted copy) until it syncs again. Recommended: accept, because the clearing waits in its queue and goes at its next sync, and the only loss is a computer that never syncs again. The alternative is the third revision's second clearing rule on every computer, which the review found harder to get right and which the fifth revision's 0.9.0 case shows to be unsafe.
3. **0.9.0 computers keep their replaced words.** While a computer stays on 0.9.0, the draft words of the meetings it recorded stay on every computer and on the relay (15.10), and nothing forces an update. Recommended: accept, with the release note line of 15.10, as the owner did for section 7A; the alternative, raising the envelope format so 0.9.0 is refused, forces every computer, the relay and the worker to update again, which the owner declined for 7A (7A.12).

### 15.12 Considered and not chosen

- **Cleared replaced lines as erasures** (confidence 0): rule 4a would order them by `updated_at` and lose an edit made elsewhere (15.5).
- **Marking them with `pass_run` or a new field now.** `pass_run` is absent on rows from before T12.92 and kept by deletions; a new field is a shape change and a format rise, which the owner keeps out of this task. Later task (to be added to PLAN.md, not part of T14.47): at the next `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` rise that changes the segment row's shape for another reason, the cleared form gets a field of its own and confidence 1.0 stops being its marker; the same commit keeps rows cleared before it reading as cleared.
- **Clearing draft rows but leaving them live**: every reader of the draft would have to skip empty lines.
- **Keeping a cleared draft row's speaker** (the fifth and sixth revisions): a draft row never has one (15.1), so there is nothing to keep; the cleared form clears the speaker on every row.
- **Stamping the cleared draft ahead of the final lines**: a device that pushed only the first requests and was then lost would leave the others with no transcript.
- **Ordering the outbox per meeting** (lines before tombstones in every push) instead of stamping queued cleared rows again: it changes the push order of every row kind and the relay's single order by stamp that every other rule relies on, where the re-stamp touches only a meeting's unsent cleared rows.
- **Clearing inside a page**, or by typed reads: the first makes receivers re-push whole drafts on a page split; the second stops at a row that does not read.
- **No stamp on the clearing writes** (each store cleaned silently): the relay would keep the sealed text and hand it to the next device that joins.
- **Sweeping in a migration, or with no cap on the wait for a pull**: the first blocks the store for its whole run; the second never runs on a computer whose sync is on and cannot reach the relay.
- **Deleting the rows**: sync needs the tombstone, or a device that was away would bring the line back.
- **P2 and the hold counting a tombstoned final row**: a meeting whose final lines are all tombstones shows its draft, and clearing it would leave the meeting with nothing on every device.
- **Receivers clearing drafts** (the third revision's clearing after a pull, and the fourth revision's sweep of every meeting's drafts): a device that does not own a meeting can clear the draft of a 0.9.0 owner, whose empty re-transcription then leaves the meeting with nothing on every computer for good (15.2).
- **Clearing the drafts of a meeting whose owner is gone** (the sixth revision, for an owner revoked from the vault; earlier, by silence): the only place one device cleared another's data, and every remaining risk sat there. A receiver behind on its pull could clear a draft just before the owner's last pushed run, which found nothing, arrived, blanking the meeting everywhere; a revoked 0.9.0 owner paired again could do the same; it trusted the relay's device list, where "not listed" is not the same as "revoked"; and a computer only switched off for a while would have lost its meetings' words under the silence variant. Dropped by the owner (15.11 e).
- **Owning a meeting by its audio rows**: retention deletes them, and an owner must still clear its draft in the sweep after that (15.2).
- **A sweep that can be asked for again** (the third revision's removable marker, the fourth revision's pair of counts asked for on joining, setting up and turning sync on): with drafts cleared only by their owner and pulled tombstones cleared as they arrive, nothing needs a second sweep (15.6), so the record has one writer and nothing to race.
- **Judging "found nothing" with kept edited lines counted, or before the words under edited lines are dropped**: a side with one edited line counted as processed, or a run whose only words fell under an edited line replaced the other lines.
- **A cleared draft final in `apply_remote`, and `merge_speakers` skipping drafts** (the sixth revision): both answered a speaker merge re-stamping draft rows after the clearing, which cannot happen on this release or on 0.9.0, because draft rows carry no speaker and every other writer takes Final rows only (15.2, "Writers of draft rows"). Draft rows are ordered by stamp alone, as before this rule; the invariant they rely on is pinned instead, and a finality rule would have needed its own damaged-row arm.
- **Re-stamping a meeting's queued cleared rows on every line write** (the fifth revision): up to every cleared row of the meeting per `use_word` click while offline, each rewritten with zeroed pages. The per-track check (15.2) does it only when no line of the track would otherwise go first.
- **Checking only writes through `StoreTx::put_segment`**: rule 4a's re-stamp and 4b's rewrite go through `repo::put_synced` and can re-queue a local edit that was never pushed.
- **A hold word of its own** (`replaced`) for H1 and H2: 0.9.0 cannot read a `sync_pending` row with a word it does not know, so a downgrade would fail to read held rows.
- **Holding until the lines come, with no release**: a 0.9.0 owner's empty re-transcription leaves a track whose lines never come, the row would stay held for ever and the T12.93 ignore copy would never run.

### 15.13 Revisions

Third revision, after the second spec review:

- P2 and the hold of 15.4 need a live final row of the track here, never a tombstone (15.2, 15.4).
- A track's first live final row counts as a parent written; the end-of-tick order is retry, then clearing (15.4).
- After a pull, a meeting is cleared only in a completed tick that wrote none of its lines; the meetings waiting are a device record written in the page's transaction, and removing `replaced-text-swept` starts the sweep in the running process (15.2, 15.6).
- A pulled text-bearing tombstone is cleared after the incoming stamp is observed (15.4).
- A pass that finds nothing is judged per track and names what the user sees (15.2, 15.11 c); the owner's three confirmations are rewritten in plain words (15.11).
- Removed contradictions: the invariant no longer claims a meeting never shows nothing (15.4, 15.10); the word-choice drop is marked as replacing section 7's text (15.3); the flush runs however the tick ends (15.8).
- The sweep counts the outbox as the push does, gives way only while sync runs and succeeds, sweeps in a per-device order and waits while another device's cleared rows arrive (15.6).

Fourth revision, after the third spec review:

- Rebuilt around one writer of passes: only the device holding a meeting's recordings runs its final pass or a re-transcription, so it clears what its pass replaced in the pass's own transaction and other devices apply its rows (15.2). The clearing after a pull, the pusher's-race guard and `replaced-text-pending` are gone; a recorder cut off mid-push is a stated limit (15.10, 15.11 open question 2).
- A side found nothing is judged on this run's recogniser lines only; edited lines are kept either way and never count as found (15.2).
- Echo against a kept reference: `clean_mic_echo` is given the kept reference lines' words; `full` is false for such a run (15.2, 15.11 d).
- H2 holds the last final line's tombstone of a track as H1 holds a cleared draft; both are released at the end of the next tick that completes its pull unless replacing lines are held (15.4).
- `Applied::text_emptied` counts rows emptied only (15.4). P2 and the holds match on meeting and track together with the track checked against its meeting (15.2, 15.4).
- The confidence 1.0 marker gets a field of its own at a later format rise, listed as a later task (15.1, 15.12).

Fifth revision, after the fourth spec review:

- 0.9.0 is not locked out (it writes format 4, and the relay refuses only a format below the vault's). The status, 15.2, 15.4 and 15.10 no longer claim it is; 15.10 states what a 0.9.0 computer does beside updated ones, with a release note line, and 15.9 adds a test with a format-4 peer that has no clearing rule.
- Ownership by origin: only the device named by the meeting row's `origin_device_id` clears drafts (P2), in its pass and in its sweep; other devices clear only text-bearing tombstones they pull or hold (15.2, 15.6). Chosen over audio rows because retention deletes those.
- "Lines go out before what they replace": a transaction that writes a queued live final line of a meeting stamps that meeting's queued cleared rows again before it commits, so an edit after the pass cannot send the cleared rows first; the release argument rests on it (15.2, 15.4). Tests cut the push at every page after edits.
- A cleared draft row keeps its speaker, so live summary reuse keeps action item owners (15.1, 15.3); test with a rename.
- "Found nothing" is judged after the words under edited lines are dropped and before echo cleaning; a mic whose words were all echo replaces as normal, provisional for the owner (15.2, 15.11 provisional 1).
- A run that keeps every track deletes only the live records and writes no other meeting record (15.2).
- The release also waits for a replacing line held `clock`; a local row that does not read is treated as absent by both holds, listed as a limit (15.4, 15.10).
- The sweep runs once per store with a single writer of its record, so joining, setting up and turning sync on no longer ask for one and no transaction kind or Busy path is needed for them (15.6).

Sixth revision, after the fifth spec review:

- A cleared draft is final in `apply_remote` in both directions, stamped again when it was the lower copy, as meeting, voiceprint and voice sample tombstones are; `merge_speakers` skips live draft rows of a track with live final lines and moves the speaker of cleared draft rows; the invariant that nothing writes such a draft row after the final transcript is stored is stated and pinned by a scan test (15.2, 15.4). Tests in both directions and with a 0.9.0 peer that merges.
- "Lines go out before what they replace" is now a per-track invariant, checked at the end of every transaction that writes a queued live final line through either `StoreTx::put_segment` or `repo::put_synced`, and after re-queueing; it re-stamps a track's queued cleared rows only when no line of the track would otherwise go first, at most once per full rewrite of the track's lines (15.2).
- In a meeting deleted here every pulled segment tombstone is written as an erasure (15.4).
- Meetings whose owner is revoked have their drafts cleared by the other devices, provisional (15.6, 15.11 provisional 4); the remaining owners that never clear, a revoked owner that returns on 0.9.0, a downgrade that blanks a side and the trust in the relay's device list and in a pulled origin are listed (15.10). The release note no longer reads as "on that computer only".
- The kept-track decision and its conflict rule also cover a track gaining or losing its last unedited final line (15.2). An echo-only mic is recoverable while the recordings are kept (15.11 provisional 1). `replace_segments` stays out of shipped code (15.2).

Seventh revision, after the sixth spec review and the owner's decisions of 2026-11-27:

- Only the owner ever clears a meeting's drafts: the step for meetings whose owner is gone, `clear_orphaned_drafts`, the vault device-list read and hand-off, and device record `replaced-text-orphans` are gone (15.2, 15.6, 15.9). The accepted cost is stated: a meeting recorded on a computer later removed from the vault, reinstalled or retired keeps its hidden replaced draft words (15.10, 15.11 e). `replaced-text-swept` keeps its single writer, the sweep, which is still needed once per store (15.6).
- Draft rows never carry a speaker (`store_live` is their only writer; `Segment::from_words` sets none; every other segment writer takes Final rows or rows naming a speaker). The cleared form clears the speaker on every row; the cleared-draft finality rule in `apply_remote`, the `merge_speakers` skip and speaker move, and the limit on two cleared copies with different speakers are gone. The invariant is pinned by the scan test, renamed `edit_draft_rows_are_written_only_by_the_live_session`, and a live session test `live_draft_rows_carry_no_speaker` (15.1, 15.2, 15.4, 15.9, 15.10, 15.12).
- In a meeting deleted here only a pulled tombstone that holds text is rewritten as an erasure (stamped once, after the incoming stamp); a pulled cleared row or erasure is applied with its own stamp, so two devices never send a row back and forth (15.4).
- The re-stamp check counts a queued line only when it is sendable; a track whose only lines are unsendable is listed as a limit (15.2, 15.10). The invariant is worded as what the check gives (this device pushes a cleared tombstone only after the relay holds a copy of a live final line of the track), and the release argument rests on a pull that reads the relay to its end (15.2, 15.4).
- The view is meeting-wide, so a 0.9.0 owner's run that empties one track leaves that track showing nothing whatever the draft, as on 0.9.0 alone; the draft is shown only when no track has a live final line. 15.2, 15.4, 15.9 and 15.10 no longer claim every computer falls back to the draft, and the downgrade limit is stated for a meeting, not a side (A8).

After the seventh spec review (passed, five advisories):

- A sendable line both converts and passes the push's guard (`check_plaintext`); the check takes the push's guard as a function, a test pins that both judge the same rows, and the limit covers refused lines (15.2, 15.9, 15.10).
- Only the owner decides a draft is replaced; any device may stamp again a cleared row it holds, needed after an epoch reset or `queue_everything`; the receiver test allows such re-stamps (15.2, 15.9).
- The scan test states what it matches (constructions of `pass = Live` rows outside `store_live`, and non-`None` `speaker_id` assignments outside named sites, with `merge_speakers`' guard required), names the writers of existing Live rows that are allowed, and has two mutation controls (15.2, 15.9).
- The per-track invariant is promised for cleared rows replacing lines of this release's passes, not for P1 clearings of 0.9.0 tombstones; the check treats all alike (15.2).
- Stale references fixed (15.10: provisional 2). New test: a re-stamp racing an in-flight push page (15.9).

## 16. One damaged row never stops a sync page, a removal or the start-up repair (T14.57)

Status: reviewed and passed (spec review 5, 2026-11-27, with seven advisories, fixed), not built. The owner's decisions are in 16.12; the revisions in 16.13. It is built before section 7A (T14.49), which relies on it (16.10). No row changes shape: `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` stays 4, there is no migration, no relay change and no wire field.

### 16.1 The problem

A row is *damaged* when its stored values do not convert for this build: text that is not UTF-8, an enum word this build does not know (a `role` of `'bogus'`), a blob of the wrong size, an integer out of range. `repo::is_unreadable` (`crates/earshot-store/src/repo.rs:1575-1577`) is the one test. Damage is local to one store (a disk fault, a hand edit, a build that wrote a value this one refuses); the relay holds sealed copies, so another device's copy of the same row is normally fine.

T14.26 made the store's own deletion, outbox and `apply_remote` step 6 survive such a row: `split_rows` (`repo.rs:445-468`) returns the rows that read plus the rowids of those that do not; `synced_by_id_or_damaged` (`repo.rs:1508-1518`) and `row_reads_deleted` (`repo.rs:1523-1535`) judge a local row by the columns that still read; the outbox counts a queued damaged row as unsendable and skips it (`sync.rs:974-986`); `Store::held_page` reads held rows in SQL; `delete_meeting` erases a damaged row by rowid (`ERASE_BY_ROWID`, `delete.rs:285-330`). A pulled copy replaces a damaged local row as an absent row is replaced, except where a tombstone here stays final (`sync.rs:1261-1308`), so on a syncing device most damage heals at the next pull of that row.

But many readers on the same paths still decode whole rows through `query` (`repo.rs:196-207`), which fails the whole read on one damaged row. Inside `apply_remote` that error rolls the page back, and since the same page is pulled again every tick, sync stops for good on that device. In `Store::remove_person` it refuses the removal for good. In a one-off start-up repair it holds the repair back at every start. Three spec reviews of section 7A in a row found such readers (7A.13, 7A.2 D1); this section fixes them once, for every path that has to keep going.

### 16.2 The rule

**R1. Paths.** The rule covers code that runs:

- inside `Store::apply_remote_with` (`crates/earshot-store/src/sync.rs:1206-1565`) and everything it calls: the page loop, `converge` (rules 1, 2, 3, 3a), `edits_win` (rules 4b, 4c), and the host's `ApplyRules::after` closure (`crates/earshot-runtime/src/sync/engine.rs:1256-1262`: `names_changed_tx` and the Dictionary rebuild);
- in the tick around a page, because a path that must not stop on a damaged row includes every reader that runs before it in the same loop (16.13, review 2): the Dictionary `vocabulary::reconcile` that runs before push and pull on every tick with Dictionary sync on (`engine.rs:855-859`; `reconcile_tx`, `vocabulary.rs:307-330`, which decodes `dictionary_words` twice, at 311-312 and 321), `names_before` before the page (`engine.rs:1243-1253`), `meetings_deleted_elsewhere` (`engine.rs:1310-1327`) and the push's `outbox_sent` (`sync.rs:1069-1087`); `reconcile_tx`'s other callers come with it: `add_entry`, `remove_entry`, `set_vocabulary` (`vocabulary.rs:474`, `573`, `622`) and `syncing_turned_on_tx` (`vocabulary.rs:339-346`, called at `sync/mod.rs:511` and `550` and `sync/setup.rs:75`), so turning Dictionary syncing on, setting up and joining do not stop on one either;
- in every user-started biometric erasure, each of which must work with a damaged row anywhere in the store: Remove (`edit::remove_person`, `crates/earshot-runtime/src/edit.rs:2817-2862`, and `Store::remove_person`, `crates/earshot-store/src/delete.rs:160-217`), Forget voice (`edit::forget_person_voices`, `edit.rs:2800-2808`, and `Store::forget_voiceprints_of`, `db.rs:513-528`) and Forget my voice (`my_voice::forget_my_voice`, `crates/earshot-runtime/src/my_voice.rs:375-417`), all through the shared `identities::forget_identity_biometrics` and `forget_identity` (`crates/earshot-store/src/identities.rs:186-261`); and the People page's list that Remove and Forget voice start from (`edit::list_persons_in`, `edit.rs:2121-2150`);
- in a one-off start-up repair: today the T12.93 ignore copy (`ignore_copy::copy_once`, `crates/earshot-runtime/src/ignore_copy.rs:82-124`) and the summary-seen start (`summary_seen::mark_existing_once`, `crates/earshot-runtime/src/summary_seen.rs:120-140`); the first page a client applies with every repair (`apply_remote_every_repair`) is covered by the first item; 7A's removal repair and identity sweep join when built.

**R2. Reads, enforced by a type.** Every root on those paths is a free function taking a `GuardedTx` (inside a transaction) or a `GuardedRead` (a read under the store lock), new types in earshot-store (16.3). They expose only readers built on `split_rows`, lenient `Col` readers, named lenient aggregates, the named guarded methods the page loop needs, and the named writers of 16.4. They implement neither `MeetingRepo` nor `VoiceprintRepo` and give no access to the connection. Inside a root, `Store` and `StoreTx` are reached only through `guarded_*` constructors (16.3); non-root code in the same modules (user edits not on these paths, other readers) keeps the ordinary repository traits. So a decoding reader (`query` over a whole row: `tx.speakers`, `all_persons`, `synced_by_id`, `dictionary_words`, core `identity_of`, and the rest of 16.5) cannot be called on a guarded path: the compiler enforces R2. The scan test (16.8) proves only what the compiler cannot: that each named root takes a guarded type, that no root touches a connection or SQL directly, and that each SQL constant is used only by the function that owns it. 16.5 is the migration checklist for the change, not the guarantee.

**R3. A row that does not read is skipped and counted.** It takes no part in the decision it would have fed, the page or transaction goes on, and the skip is counted (16.7). Only `is_unreadable` errors are skips: any other store error (a missing table, I/O, `Busy`) still fails the page or transaction as today.

**R4. Nothing is erased or rewritten on a guess.** A damaged row is written only by rowid, by a named writer, under the written-columns rule of 16.4: the columns that decide the write must read; a column whose old value did not read may be set only when the readable path sets that column to the same value whatever its old value (`ignored = 0`, `person_id = NULL`, `deleted = 1`, a tombstone's `display_name = ''`), plus the owner's one decided case, the zeroing of a removed person's voice vector (16.4); a value derived from other data (a live row's "Speaker N", a merge target) is never written over a value that did not read. A row whose remaining damage is untouched stays unreadable, so unsendable (the outbox skips it, T12.77b7), and never overwrites another device's good copy. When a column that decides a write does not read, the row is not written at all.

**R5. Counts never content.** Every count is a number; no id, name or value of a damaged row is logged, shown or stored.

### 16.3 Reading without decoding

- **The guarded types and their layering** (review 2, B2). `GuardedTx<'a>` wraps a `StoreTx` and `GuardedRead<'a>` wraps a `&Store`, both in a new module `crates/earshot-store/src/guarded.rs`, which owns every lenient reader, every by-rowid writer and every SQL statement they run, each statement a named `const` in that module (`CHAIN_STEP_SQL`, `SPEAKER_LINKS_SQL`, `ERASE_PRINT_BY_ROWID_SQL`, and so on), and nothing else.
  - *Roots are free functions taking a guarded type.* The page's body moves out of `StoreTx::apply_remote_with` into `guarded::apply_page(g: &mut GuardedTx<'_>, ..)`, and `converge`, `dead_person_repair`, `edits_win`, `forget_identity_biometrics`, `forget_identity`, `remove_person` (the store half), `forget_voiceprints_of` (its transaction body), `outbox_sent`'s body and the runtime roots are free functions of the same shape. `Store::apply_remote`, `Store::remove_person` and `Store::forget_voiceprints_of` keep their signatures and only open the transaction, call `guarded_transaction` and pass the result on. `Store::remove_person(person, ..)` computes `identity_tx` itself inside that transaction; `edit::remove_person` computes it too, for its refusal and its names, under the same `guarded_write` lock guard, so both see the same identity (review 3, A1). `AfterApply` (`sync.rs:294`, today `&dyn Fn(&mut StoreTx<'_>)`) becomes `&dyn Fn(&mut GuardedTx<'_>) -> Result<(), StoreError>`.
  - *Constructors only.* `Store::guarded_read`, `Store::guarded_transaction`, `Store::guarded_immediate` and `StoreTx::guarded` are the only way a guarded type is made. `guarded_immediate` opens the transaction with `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, so the write lock is held before the first read, as `migrate` (`db.rs:296`) and the export lease (`export.rs:507`, `527`) do today; there is no general `Store::immediate`. A guarded root that must hold the write lock before its first read (7A's one-off sweep inside `Store::open`) is opened by it (7A review 11). These are the only constructors, and a root (in `sync.rs`, `identities.rs`, `delete.rs`, and in the runtime `summary_names.rs`, `vocabulary.rs`, `my_voice.rs`, `ignore_copy.rs`, `summary_seen.rs`, `edit.rs` and `engine.rs`) reaches the store through nothing else. Store-side roots are made by `Store::guarded_*`; runtime roots by `Services::guarded_*` below.
  - *The runtime half* (review 3, B1; review 4, B1 and B2). `Services::store()` gives a guard that derefs to `Store` (every decoding reader) and `parts()` gives `(&mut Store, &mut Hlc)`, so a runtime root never holds `&Services`. Runtime roots are free functions whose store parameter is a guarded type:
    - `&ServicesRead`, from `services.guarded_read()`: holds the store lock guard and offers only a `GuardedRead`;
    - `&mut ServicesWrite`, from `services.guarded_write(|w| ..)`: holds the lock guard and the HLC for the whole closure and offers guarded reads, `w.transaction(|g, stamp| ..)` (one `GuardedTx` transaction; several may run in turn under the one lock), and the store's guarded erasures `w.remove_person(..)`, `w.forget_voiceprints_of(..)`, which commit their transaction and then checkpoint, still under the lock, as `Store::remove_person` and `Store::forget_voiceprints_of` do today;
    - `GuardedTx` / `GuardedRead`, for roots called from the above.
    Each public entry point that takes `services` (`edit::remove_person`, `forget_person_voices`, `list_persons`, `my_voice::forget_my_voice`, `ignore_copy::copy_once`, `summary_seen::mark_existing_once`, `vocabulary::reconcile` and the Dictionary edits) becomes a one-line wrapper: `services.guarded_write(|w| remove_person_in(w, person))` or `guarded_read`. The compiler enforces R2, because no root can name a `Services`.
    - *How the multi-step ones fit.* `edit::remove_person_in(w, person)`: guarded reads for the refusal, the identity and `names_before`; then `w.remove_person(person)` (its own transaction, commit, checkpoint); then `w.transaction(|g, _| names_changed_tx(g, &before))`, the second transaction of today's `edit.rs:2857-2860`, all under the one lock guard, so nothing changes between them. `my_voice::forget_my_voice_in(w)`: guarded reads for the starting persons and the live count, then `w.forget_voiceprints_of(&mine)`, whose checkpoint runs under the same guard; a `Busy` after the commit is still answered with the live count, as today (`my_voice.rs:412-415`).
    - *`engine.rs`* (review 4, A3; review 5, A1). The tick's glue does network work, so it is not a root: `tick_into`, `try_tick`, `push`, `pull`, `apply`, `retry_held`, `retry_parents`, `meetings_deleted_elsewhere` and `observe_vault`. Its store access (and only that: `self.services.clock`, `leased_meetings()` and `recording_meetings()` stay as they are) is one `self.services.guarded_read()` or `guarded_write(..)` call per step, and `ServicesWrite` gives every store call the glue makes: `apply_remote(page, rules, every_repair) -> Applied`, which holds `names_before`, the page's apply (with the `after` closure) and the `flush_erasures` that follows under one guard, so the glue never needs `parts()`; `flush_erasures`; `outbox_page` (T14.26's lenient outbox read) and `outbox_sent_in`; `held_page` and `held_count`; `outbox_len_except`; the `sync-cursor` compare-and-set; `device()`; and the `sync_settings` read (device-local records through `local_value`). `observe_vault` (`judge_vault`'s cursor and re-queue, 6.2) takes a `&mut ServicesWrite`. `apply`'s one other store call, `Services::delete_meeting` for a meeting deleted elsewhere, is T14.26's deletion path and stays as it is, a named exception in 16.8.
    - `named_after_me_in` (`edit.rs:1833-1841`, review 4, A3) takes a `&GuardedRead`: it calls `list_persons_in` and reads the user's name key through `person_links` and `split_rows` (today's `user_name_key`), so it is a guarded reader too; its callers pass the guarded read they hold.
  - *What the page loop needs, as named guarded methods*: `synced_by_id_or_damaged`, `row_reads_deleted`, `request_reads_withdrawn_by`, `outbox_forget(table, id)` (the `DELETE FROM sync_outbox` of `sync.rs:1393-1396` and `1488-1491`), `hold` and `unhold` (`sync_pending`), `put_synced`, and `write_or_hold(row) -> Written | MissingParent`, a guarded savepoint (`sync.rs:1483-1502`) that writes the row and removes its outbox entry, or rolls back to the savepoint and reports `MissingParent` so the loop holds the row `parent`, without exposing the connection.
  - *Records*: `local_value`, `set_local_value`, `delete_local_value`, and `identity_record_keys()` (the `local_state` key read of `forget_identity`, `identities.rs:211-223`); the runtime's record writers get guarded variants, `local::set_guarded` beside `local::set_tx` and a `record_tx` (summary names, `summary_names.rs:118-140`) taking a `GuardedTx`.
  - *Writers of readable rows* (`put_person`, `put_speaker`, `put_summary`, `put_dictionary_word`) are re-exposed unchanged; writing a whole readable row is not reading.
  - The Dictionary edits (`add_entry`, `remove_entry`, `set_vocabulary`) are guarded too: each is a one-line `services.guarded_write(..)` wrapper over its root (`add_entry_in` and so on), which calls `reconcile_tx` and `rebuild_tx` on the `GuardedTx` it is given (review 5, A5). `StoreTx::guarded` exists only for store code that already holds a transaction.
- **Lenient column reads.** A direct read must itself survive a value that does not convert. Today's direct queries read typed columns with `?` (`r.get::<_, [u8; 16]>(1)?` at `sync.rs:571` and `584`, `r.get(0)?` at `744-748`), so a person id blob of the wrong length fails `collect` and with it the page. Every direct read on these paths reads each column into a small three-way value, `Col<T>` (`Read(T)` or `Unreadable`), through `Row::get_ref` and a conversion that cannot error, and always selects `rowid`, so a row can be written by rowid even when its `id` does not read. A row whose deciding column is `Unreadable` is a skip for that decision (R3).
- **Three-way `deleted`.** `row_reads_deleted` answers `false` both for live and for an unreadable column. Decisions here use `Deleted::{Yes, No, Unreadable}` from the same column read.
- **New store readers** (earshot-store, on `Store` and `StoreTx`, all through `split_rows` or lenient reads, all counting what they skip):
  - `speaker_links(filter)`: per speaker row `rowid`, `id`, `meeting_id`, `person_id`, `deleted`, `display_name`, each a `Col`; the filter is a fixed SQL condition (a meeting, or `person_id` in a temporary id table, as 7A.2 C1's `temp.push_only`).
  - `person_links()`: per person row `rowid`, `id`, `deleted`, `is_me`, `merged_into`, each a `Col`.
  - `speakers_or_damaged(meeting)` (today's `for_deletion::speakers`, `repo.rs:667-680`, made available beside the plain reader) and `summaries_or_damaged(meeting)`.
  - `current_summary_or_damaged(meeting) -> Option<Result<Summary, ()>>`: the row `CURRENT_SUMMARY_ROWID_SQL` chooses (`repo.rs:954-957`), read by `split_rows`, as `shown_summary` does (`repo.rs:962-`); a current row that does not read is `Err(())`, never an older summary.
  - `person_or_damaged(id)` and `meeting_or_damaged(id)` (the latter exists, `delete.rs:234-239`); `meetings_lenient()`: `rowid`, `id`, `deleted` of every meeting row.
  - `current_summary_id_lenient(meeting)`: the id `CURRENT_SUMMARY_SQL` chooses (`repo.rs:935-938`) read as a `Col` (today's `current_summary_id`, `repo.rs:942-951`, fails on an id blob of the wrong length through `get_id` at 946-948).
- **Named lenient aggregates** (each a fixed query whose every column is read as a `Col`, a row with an `Unreadable` key column counted and left out): `crowded_meetings()` (rule 2's `meeting_id ... HAVING count(*) > 1`, `sync.rs:707-714`, whose `r.get(0)` at 711 fails on a bad blob); `person_meetings_lenient()` (the People page's (person, meeting) pairs, today `repo::person_meetings`, `repo.rs:838-847`, `get_id` on both columns); `voice_rows_of(identity)` (the prints and samples whose `person_id` or `voiceprint_id` is in an id set, with `rowid` and `deleted`); and `has_voice(identity)` (review 2, B3; review 3, B3), today's meaning of `live_prints_of(..).any(|(_, e)| e.count() > 0)` (`edit.rs:2140-2143`) with today's fold: the members' prints and their samples are read through `split_rows` (`voiceprints_or_damaged`, `samples_or_damaged`), and core's public `effective(print, samples)` (`crates/earshot-core/src/voiceprint/sample.rs:460-463`, which is `fold(print, samples, &[])`, the fold `live_prints` uses, with no samples excluded; `has_voice` excludes none, so it is the same answer and `fold` stays private; review 4, A1) runs on the rows that read, so a sample whose model, length, person or values do not match its print is left out exactly as today. A live print with effective count above 0 is a voice; a print or sample that does not read is counted as damaged and decides nothing.
- **One chain walk.** `chain_end(g, p) -> Removed | Live | Unknown` follows `merged_into` reading each step's `deleted` and `merged_into` only, exactly today's `ends_deleted` answer (`sync.rs:535-554`; `Some(true)` at 543-547) on readable rows (review 2, B6): `Removed` at a step whose `deleted` reads `Yes`, whatever its `is_me` (rule 3a erases there today, and still does); `Live` at a step with `deleted` `No` and `merged_into` reading `None`; `Unknown` for a loop, a person not known here, or a step whose `deleted` or `merged_into` is `Unreadable`. It replaces `ends_deleted`, which reads through `synced_by_id`. Rule 3a erases only on `Removed`, so a damaged step never causes an erasure by guess. As built by T14.57 the walk read no `is_me`. Since 7A (T14.49a) it reads `is_me` at a tombstoned step and has a fourth outcome, `MeTombstone` (7A.2 P4 and D1): `is_me` reading `true` there is `MeTombstone`, never a removal, so rule 3a no longer erases at a tombstoned "Me"; `false` is `Removed`; unreadable is `Unknown`. An `Unknown` caused by a step that does not read is counted (`Applied::chains_unknown`, the step's rowid in `damaged_skipped`); a loop or a person not known here counts nothing.
- **One identity walk.** `identity_tx(g, person) -> Identity { members, skipped }`: `person` plus every person whose `merged_into` chain, read through `person_links`' `id` and `merged_into`, reaches it (the rule of core `identity_of`, `crates/earshot-core/src/voiceprint/mod.rs:574-585`, and `chain_reaches`, `mod.rs:546-565`). Membership depends only on `id` and `merged_into`, never on `is_me` (review 2, B4): a person whose `id` or `merged_into` does not read cannot be placed and is counted in `skipped`. `is_me` decides only whether Remove is refused (16.5 item 18). Core `identity_of` stays for core and in-memory callers; no guarded path can call it.

### 16.4 Writing by rowid

A damaged row is written only by one of the named writers in `guarded.rs` (`erase_print_by_rowid`, `erase_sample_by_id` moved there from `sync.rs`, `erase_sample_by_rowid`, `unlink_speaker_by_rowid`, `tombstone_person_by_rowid`; there is no summary writer, since rule 2 leaves damaged summaries alone), and only under the **written-columns rule**:

- every column that decides the write reads (for a speaker unlink `person_id` and `meeting_id`; for a person tombstone `id` and `merged_into`; for a print `person_id`; for a sample `person_id` or `voiceprint_id`);
- `erase_sample_by_rowid` (review 2, B5) erases a sample whose `id` does not read, found by rowid through `voice_rows_of`, when its `person_id` reads as a member or its `voiceprint_id` as one of the identity's prints. It sets every column of the erased form, all constants (`deleted = 1`, `voiceprint_id`, `person_id`, `model`, `speech_ms`, `meeting_id`, `speaker_id` NULL, `vector = x''`) and the stamp, and leaves `id` as stored, so the row stays unreadable and unsendable but holds no voice. `erase_sample_by_id` stays the writer for a sample whose `id` reads;
- a column may be set when its old value read, to what the readable path writes;
- a column whose old value did not read may be set only when the readable path sets it to the same value whatever its old value: `person_id = NULL`, `ignored = 0`, `deleted = 1`, a tombstone's `display_name = ''` (a speaker row whose `deleted` reads `Yes`, or a person being tombstoned), and the stamp;
- a value derived from other data (a live speaker row's first free "Speaker N", a merge target, anything computed from the row's other columns) is never written over a value that did not read: the name of a live speaker row whose `display_name` does not read is left as stored, so the row stays unreadable and unsendable (7A.2 D1, review 9 B2);
- **the owner's one decided case** (16.12 a): a removed person's voiceprint `sum` is zeroed whatever it reads as, `sum = zeroblob(length(CAST(sum AS BLOB)))`, when the print's `person_id` reads as a member of the identity being erased, or `chain_end` of it says `Removed`. A `sum` that does not read is still that person's biometric data (PLAN 3.9). Its length comes from the stored value, so the result is not always the readable path's tombstone: a readable `sum` of whole floats gives exactly the zeroed vector `forget_voiceprints` writes (`mod.rs:637-641`); a damaged one gives zero bytes of the damaged value's byte length, which reads as a zeroed vector only when that length is a whole number of floats (otherwise the row stays unreadable and unsendable), and the print's other columns stay as stored. When the row reads afterwards it is a tombstone with no voice in it and syncs as removed. A voice sample is erased by `erase_sample_by_id` (`sync.rs:616-636`, rule 3 and 3a's writer today), whose every column it sets is a constant of the erased form.

No writer of a speaker, person or print row sets `role`, `model`, `count`, `speech_ms`, `meeting_id`, `is_me`, `merged_into` or any other column outside these lists (review 3, A1: a sample's erased form does clear its `model`, `speech_ms` and `meeting_id`, all to constants). Erasures write in a fixed order, so test 12's literal outputs hold (review 3, A4): today's order of `forget_identity_biometrics` and `Store::remove_person` (`identities.rs:195-197`, `delete.rs:171-212`): identity entries by record key, then prints in identity order (members in the order core `identity_of` lists them, the person first and then `all_persons`' id order, each member's prints by id), then samples in id order (core's `BTreeMap`), then speaker rows in meeting order and creation order within a meeting, then person rows in identity order. Stamps are taken in that order. A meeting deletion's erasure (`delete_meeting`, T14.26) is the one existing exception: the user deleted the whole meeting, so it writes canonical tombstone values whatever read; it is unchanged.

### 16.5 The decoding readers on these paths (the migration checklist)

This list is what the change has to move onto the guarded types; it is not the guarantee (R2 is, through the compiler and 16.8). Review 1 found readers the first draft missed (16.13), which is why. Each entry: where, what the code needs, and how it reads after this change. "Skipped" means counted under R3. File paths are under `crates/` unless named.

**Inside `apply_remote`**

1. `ends_deleted` (`earshot-store/src/sync.rs:535-554`, `synced_by_id(person)` at 543). Needs each step's `deleted` and `merged_into`. Becomes `chain_end` (16.3).
2. Rule 3a's sample and print queries (`sync.rs:566-586`). Already direct, but typed reads with `?` fail on a bad blob. Become lenient reads of `rowid`, `person_id`, `meeting_id` (samples) and `rowid`, `person_id` (prints); a row whose `person_id` does not read is skipped.
3. Rule 3a's print read (`sync.rs:591-592`, `synced_by_id(voiceprint)`, then a whole-row rewrite). Needs nothing but the rowid once `chain_end` says `Removed`. Becomes an erasure by rowid, `erase_print_by_rowid` (16.4: `deleted = 1`, `sum` zeroed, stamp). A readable print gets exactly today's row.
4. Rule 3's sample query (`sync.rs:742-758`). Lenient read of `rowid` and `meeting_id`; erased by rowid.
5. Rule 1's `all_persons` (`sync.rs:676`, `repo.rs:1311-1314`). Needs `is_me`, `deleted`, `merged_into`, `created_at`, `id`, `display_name`, and whole rows to rewrite. Becomes `split_rows` over `person`: damaged persons take no part (neither kept nor merged).
6. Rule 2's `summaries_of` (`sync.rs:717`, `repo.rs:923-930`). Needs `deleted`, `created_at`, `id` and whole rows to tombstone. Becomes `summaries_or_damaged`: damaged summaries take no part. The "crowded" query (`sync.rs:707-714`) reads `meeting_id` with a typed `r.get` (711) and fails on a bad blob; it becomes the named aggregate `crowded_meetings()` (16.3).
7. Rule 4's meeting check (`sync.rs:791-792`, `synced_by_id(meeting)`). Needs `deleted`. Becomes `meeting_or_damaged` plus the three-way `deleted`: `No` runs the rule, `Yes` or `Unreadable` skips the meeting (counted).
8. Rule 4b's `segments_of` (`sync.rs:796`, `repo.rs:416-424`). Becomes `segments_or_damaged` (`repo.rs:428-440`): damaged segments neither protect words under them nor lose words.
9. Rule 4c's `speakers_of` (`sync.rs:841`, `repo.rs:827-834`). Needs `deleted`, `role`, `id` and whole rows to revive. Becomes `speakers_or_damaged`: a damaged tombstoned speaker named by an edited line is not revived; a damaged row counts towards `me_live` when its `role` reads `Me` or does not read and its `deleted` reads `No` or does not read (lenient reads of `role` and `deleted` beside the split; review 3, A3), so a revival never makes a second "Me" beside a damaged row that may be one; a revived "Me" then comes back as `Room`.
10. Rule 4a's meeting check (`sync.rs:1429-1430`, `synced_by_id(meeting)`). Becomes `meeting_or_damaged` plus three-way `deleted`; `Unreadable` falls back to plain `updated_at` order for that row (as for a meeting deleted here).
11. A pulled person tombstone's side effect (`sync.rs:1545-1556`) -> `forget_identity_biometrics` (`identities.rs:186-198`) -> core `identity_of` (`identities.rs:195`; `earshot-core/src/voiceprint/mod.rs:574-585`, `repo.persons()` at 579, which is `all_persons`, `repo.rs:1772-1774`; `chain_reaches` with `repo.person` at 561, `get_person`, `repo.rs:1306-1309`), `forget_identity` (16.5 item 20) and core `forget_voiceprints` (`identities.rs:197`; `mod.rs:620-648`: `samples_of_person` at 630, `voiceprints_of` at 633, `samples_of` at 634, `erase_samples` at 646 -> `repo.sample(id)` at `voiceprint/sample.rs:652`; all `query` readers, `repo.rs:1330-1337`, `1405-1416`, `1477-1489`, and a damaged sample is an error there by design, `repo.rs:1372-1373`). Needs member ids; for prints `person_id`; for samples `person_id` and `voiceprint_id`. Becomes a store-side erasure with no core call: `identity_tx` (membership by `id` and `merged_into` only, 16.3); prints of the identity (any `deleted`, as core reads them) found through `voice_rows_of`; live ones erased by `erase_print_by_rowid` (16.4, including a `sum` that does not read); samples whose `person_id` is a member or whose `voiceprint_id` is one of those prints, `deleted = 0`, erased by `erase_sample_by_id`, or by `erase_sample_by_rowid` when their `id` does not read. A sample whose `person_id` and `voiceprint_id` both do not read is skipped. Returns `Forgotten` with two new counts (16.7): `entries` (identity entries `forget_identity` dropped, which today's `forget_identity_biometrics` throws away, 7A.4) and `zeroed` (prints whose `sum` did not read and was zeroed), plus `skipped`.
12. The host's `names_changed_tx` in `ApplyRules::after` (`engine.rs:1257`; `earshot-runtime/src/summary_names.rs:207-230`: `tx.summaries` at 213, `tx.speakers` at 219). Needs the meeting's current summary (id and body, for `uses_name`) and the readable speaker rows' `id`, `deleted`, `display_name`. Becomes `current_summary_or_damaged` (a current summary that does not read skips that meeting's marks) and `speakers_or_damaged` (a damaged row's mark is skipped).
13. The Dictionary rebuild in `ApplyRules::after` (`engine.rs:1258-1260` -> `vocabulary::rebuild_tx`, `earshot-runtime/src/vocabulary.rs:262-278`, `tx.dictionary_words()` at 275). Needs every live word row. A rebuild without a word would drop it from `settings/vocabulary` on a guess. `rebuild_tx` itself changes: it reads through `split_rows` over `dictionary_word` on a `GuardedTx`, and when any word row does not read it writes nothing and returns a new outcome, `Rebuild::Unread { skipped }`, leaving the stored list as it is (as `OverRefused::Keep` keeps a refused list). On a page that is counted and the next page with every row readable rebuilds. Every caller of `rebuild_tx` handles `Unread` (review 2, A2): `syncing_turned_on_tx` (`vocabulary.rs:344`) keeps the stored list and turns syncing on (the next page rebuilds); `set_vocabulary` (`vocabulary.rs:490`), `add_entry` (`578`) and `remove_entry` (`624`), the user's own edits, which decide on the rebuilt list, keep their refusal: `Unread` refuses the edit as `NotInUse` does today and rolls it back, so a page edit never stores a list that leaves a word out.
13a. The Dictionary reconcile (review 2, B1): `vocabulary::reconcile` / `reconcile_tx` (`vocabulary.rs:307-330`), run before push and pull on every tick with Dictionary sync on (`engine.rs:855-859`), and inside `add_entry`, `remove_entry`, `set_vocabulary` (`vocabulary.rs:474`, `573`, `622`) and `syncing_turned_on_tx` (`vocabulary.rs:343`, reached from `sync/mod.rs:511`, `550` and `sync/setup.rs:75`). It decodes `dictionary_words` twice (311-312, 321), so today one damaged word row fails every tick before anything is pushed or pulled, and refuses turning syncing on, setting up and joining. Needs each row's `word`, `deleted` and the stored list. Becomes `split_rows` over `dictionary_word` on a `GuardedTx`: the first pass tombstones the readable quote-only rows as today; the second pass, which makes a row for each listed word that has no live row, is skipped while any word row does not read (the damaged row may be that word's live row, and a second row would be a guess), and returns `Reconciled::Unread { skipped }`, counted. The tick goes on to push and pull; the user's edits refuse as for `Rebuild::Unread`; `syncing_turned_on_tx` goes on with the list kept.
13b. The Dictionary edits' own readers (review 3, A2): `set_vocabulary`'s `tx.dictionary_words()` at `vocabulary.rs:482` and its `remove_rows_tx` at 480; `add_entry`'s at 567 (the `NotInUse` check) and 574; `remove_entry`'s `remove_rows_tx` at 623; and `remove_rows_tx` itself (`vocabulary.rs:371-372`). They become `split_rows` over `dictionary_word` on the edit's `GuardedTx`; when any word row does not read, the edit is refused with the same refusal as `Rebuild::Unread` (it rolls back, nothing is written). With Dictionary sync off nothing pulls a copy over the damaged row, so it is never healed: every Dictionary edit is refused until the row is repaired (by turning Dictionary sync on, when another device holds a good copy, or by restoring a backup). The Dictionary page tells the user so with fixed copy `dictionary_damaged` in `fixtures/ui/summary_settings_cases.json` (draft: "A word in your Dictionary on this computer is damaged, so the list can't be changed. Turning on Dictionary sync can restore it from another computer."), shown in place of the refusal; the summary path keeps its stored list (`settings/vocabulary` is read as stored, so summaries still get the words).

**Around a page and the push**

14. `names_before` (`engine.rs:1243-1253`; `summary_names.rs:174-198`: `repo.summaries` at 180, `repo.speakers` at 183-184). As item 12, with the same readers. 7A.9 widens its set; the per-meeting skip of 7A.2 D1 is replaced by these per-row skips.
15. `meetings_deleted_elsewhere` (`engine.rs:1310-1327`, `store.meeting(m.id)` at 1319, `get_meeting`, `repo.rs:280-283`). Needs the local row's `deleted`. Becomes `meeting_or_damaged`: a readable live row as today; a damaged row whose `deleted` reads `No` or `Unreadable` is deleted through `Services::delete_meeting`, which handles a damaged meeting (T14.26); the pulled tombstone decides, which is not a guess about the row. `Yes` deletes nothing.
16. `outbox_sent` (`sync.rs:1069-1087`, `synced_by_id(table, id)` at 1077, push path). Needs `updated_at` only. Becomes a direct lenient read of the three `updated_*` columns; a stamp that does not read leaves the entry queued (the row is unsendable anyway). Today one row damaged between its send and this mark fails the tick.

**Removal**

17. `edit::remove_person`'s target (`edit.rs:2823-2827`, `db.person`). Becomes `person_or_damaged`: a damaged target is `PersonNotFound` (it cannot be on the People page, item 23, so this answers only a stale request).
18. `edit::remove_person`'s user check (`edit.rs:2832-2840`, `identity_of` and `store.person` per member). Needs each member's `is_me`. Becomes `identity_tx` and `person_links`. `is_me` decides only refusals here (review 2, B4): a member whose `is_me` reads `true` refuses as today (`PersonIsTheUser`); a member whose `is_me` does not read refuses too, with a new `EditError::PersonUnreadable` and fixed copy (16.7), because removing the user on a guess breaks the owner's 7A.12. Nothing is left out of a removal that goes ahead: every member is removed. The refused person's voices can still be erased with Forget voice, which never reads `is_me`.
19. `edit::remove_person`'s row list (`edit.rs:2843-2855`: `identity_of`, `store.meetings()` at 2846, `all_meetings`, `repo.rs:285-292`; `store.speakers` at 2850) and its `names_before` / `names_changed_tx` (2856, 2860). Needs the live rows whose `person_id` is a member, by meeting. Becomes `speaker_links` with `person_id` in the identity and `deleted` `No`, joined to `meetings_lenient()` so a row of a meeting whose `deleted` reads `Yes` is left out, as today's `m.meta.deleted` filter does (`edit.rs:2847`; a meeting whose `deleted` does not read is left out too, counted); the names through items 12 and 14.
20. `forget_identity` (`identities.rs:207-261`, `tx.speakers(meeting)` at 238). Needs each row's `id` and `person_id`. Becomes `speaker_links(meeting)`. An entry is dropped when `given_person` is a member or its row's `person_id` reads as one. It is also dropped, counted, when it cannot be placed and could be the identity's (it errs towards erasing, as 7A's P5 does for an unreadable `from`; the owner, 2026-11-27, 16.12 b): its row's `person_id` does not read, or it matches no readable row while the meeting holds a row whose `id` does not read (it may be that row's). In a user-started erasure (Remove, Forget voice, Forget my voice) such an entry is dropped whatever it might be, because whether a row is the user's never narrows an erasure the user started (review 2). Only when the erasure is a pulled person tombstone's side effect (not started by this device's user) is such an entry kept, counted, when it could be the user's (16.12 b): its `given_person` names a person whose `is_me` reads `true` or does not read, or the row it may belong to has a `role` reading `Me` or not reading, or a `person_id` naming such a person. Only the device-local entry goes; no row is written. An entry whose row is missing (no row with its id, and no row of the meeting whose `id` does not read) or whose row's `person_id` reads as someone else is kept, as today.
21. `Store::remove_person` (`delete.rs:160-217`: `identity_of` at 171, `forget_identity_biometrics` at 173, `tx.meetings()` at 176, `tx.speakers` at 177, `tx.person` at 201). Becomes: `identity_tx` computed inside `Store::remove_person`'s own transaction (16.3; under the same `ServicesWrite` guard as item 18's, so the same set), used for biometrics, rows and persons alike; item 11's erasure; a shared helper `unlink_speakers_tx(tx, identity, hlc, clock)` over `speaker_links` (readable rows rewritten exactly as today through `speakers_or_damaged`; a damaged row whose `person_id` reads as a member rewritten by `unlink_speaker_by_rowid` under the written-columns rule (16.4: `person_id = NULL`, `ignored = 0`, the stamp, "Speaker N" only when `display_name` and `deleted` both read and `deleted` is `No`, `''` when `deleted` reads `Yes`); a damaged row whose `person_id` or `meeting_id` does not read left untouched and counted; names taken for "Speaker N" are the readable names of the meeting's other live rows, an unreadable name counting as taken by no row); persons tombstoned as today when readable, by `tombstone_person_by_rowid` otherwise (`deleted = 1`, `display_name = ''` whatever it read as, since the readable path clears it whatever it was, and the stamp). 7A's P3 reuses `unlink_speakers_tx`.
22. Forget voice: `edit::forget_person_voices` (`edit.rs:2800-2808`: `db.person(person)` at 2803, a decoding read of the target) and `forget_identity_biometrics` behind `Store::forget_voiceprints_of` (`db.rs:519-525`). The target is read with `person_or_damaged` (damaged: `PersonNotFound`, as item 17); the erasure is item 11's.
23. The People page list (`edit::list_persons_in`, `edit.rs:2121-2150`: `repo.person_meetings()` at 2124, `repo.rs:838-847`, which reads both ids with `get_id` and fails on one speaker row whose `person_id` is 15 bytes; `repo.persons()` at 2127; `identity_of` at 2131; `live_prints_of` at 2140). Remove and Forget voice start here; today one damaged person row, or one such speaker row, leaves the page empty with an error, so nothing can be removed or forgotten. Becomes `person_meetings_lenient()` (a pair whose `person_id` or `meeting_id` does not read is left out and counted), `split_rows` over `person` (damaged persons left off the list, counted), `identity_tx`, and for "has a voice" `has_voice(identity)` instead of `live_prints_of` (whose sample reads error on a damaged sample by design), with today's meaning (16.3). It also feeds `edit::list_persons` (`edit.rs:2113`, through `services.guarded_read()`), the desktop's `detail::persons` (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/detail.rs:1249`, behind the `list_persons` command), the meeting detail (`detail.rs:942`) and `named_after_me_in` (`edit.rs:1833-1841`, its call at 1837), which take `.persons` and carry `damaged` to the page where they show a list.
23a. Forget my voice (`my_voice::forget_my_voice`, `my_voice.rs:375-417`: `store.persons()` at 380-386 to find the `is_me` persons, `identity_of` at 389, `voiceprints_of` at 390, `samples_of` at 394, `samples_of_person` at 398-400 to count what is live in case the checkpoint is `Busy`). Becomes `person_links` (the persons whose `is_me` reads `true` are where it starts; a person whose `is_me` does not read is not a starting point, counted, but is forgotten when it is a member of one of them, since membership never reads `is_me`, 16.12 f), `identity_tx` for each, `voice_rows_of` for the live count, and the erasure of item 11 through `Store::forget_voiceprints_of`. A damaged person row anywhere, the user's own included when its `is_me` reads, no longer stops it.
  - *When any person row's `is_me` or `id` does not read* (the owner, 2026-11-27, 16.12 g, with review 4's check; `id` added after review 5, A2, since a person whose `id` does not read cannot be judged either), the user may be that person, so Forget my voice also uses the evidence the store records of the user's own voice. Only the user's own voice sample is stored with origin `SampleOrigin::Own` (`voiceprint/sample.rs:36-46`: "Your voice", written by `enroll_me` through `my_voice::enrol_my_voice`; a meeting voice always records its meeting and speaker, and the user's speaker row is never enrolled, AGENTS T12.19), read as `meeting_id` and `speaker_id` both NULL on a live sample (`sample.rs:277`, `372-379`); a sample whose `meeting_id` or `speaker_id` does not read is not evidence. The persons such samples name (`person_id`) become further starting points, each only when its own `is_me` does not read `false` (a person whose `is_me` reads `false` is someone else, whatever a sample says). From them the erasure is the ordinary one (item 11): `identity_tx` of each, so merged-in persons, base-only prints (a print made before voice samples, T12.77b, its voice in its base alone) and identity entries are covered. A print is taken only through its own `person_id` being a member, never through a sample's `voiceprint_id` naming it: biometric erasure is chosen by the person a row names, never by a link from another row unless both rows name the same person (review 4). So an `Own` sample whose `voiceprint_id` names another person's print erases the sample (its own `person_id` is a member) and leaves that print.
  - *`incomplete`* is defined by the same trigger: the result carries `incomplete: true` when any person row's `is_me` or `id` does not read and that person is not reached as a starting point or a member, since it may be the user and its voice is then left (16.6). The page shows fixed copy `forget_my_voice_incomplete` in `fixtures/ui/people_copy_cases.json` (draft: "Some of your saved voice data on this computer could not be read, so not all of it may have been forgotten."), in place of `forget_damaged_note`.

**Start-up repairs**

24. The ignore copy (`ignore_copy.rs:82-124`: `tx.meetings()` at 91, `tx.speakers` at 106). Needs live meetings' ids and each row's `id`, `deleted`, `role`, `person_id`, `ignored`. Becomes a lenient read of meeting `rowid`, `id`, `deleted` and `speakers_or_damaged`: a damaged row is skipped and counted. The copy runs once and writes `ignore-copied` in the same transaction as today, even when it skipped a damaged row (the owner, 2026-11-27, 16.12 c, reversing the earlier answer after review 4): a run again later could overwrite an un-ignore made on another computer and already pulled here, because the copy cannot tell a value it set from one a pull set since. So there is no rerun and no per-process flag; `copy_after_tick` (`sync/thread.rs:456-465`) still stops at the record, as today. Cost, stated: the legacy ignore flag of a row skipped as damaged is lost for good on this device once the record is written (`legacy_of` reads nothing after the copy), so when that row reads again (a pulled copy) its voice reads as not ignored here unless another device's copy carries the ignore; the skip count goes into a fixed log word. Two more costs (review 5, A4): a meeting whose own `id` or `deleted` does not read is skipped whole, so every row of it loses its legacy ignore; and a row that heals later, whose legacy flag had made it a user edit (`SpeakerIdentity::edited` reads the legacy flag before the copy), no longer reads as edited, so the next re-transcription of that meeting may not carry the name it had been given.
25. The summary-seen start (`summary_seen.rs:127`, `tx.meetings()`; 132, `current_summary_id`, `repo.rs:942-951`, whose `get_id` at 946-948 fails on a bad id blob). It needs only the current summary's id, never its body. Becomes `meetings_lenient()` and `current_summary_id_lenient(meeting)`; a meeting whose current summary's id does not read is not marked.
26. 7A's removal repair and identity sweep, when built: their own reads already follow 7A.2 D1; the existing readers they call are items 12, 14, 20 and 21.

**Found and left out of scope** (not on these paths): `name_changed_tx` (`summary_names.rs:148-158`, inside a user's naming edit: a refusal there is a refused edit, not a stuck path); the meeting detail, search and Speakers panel readers (T14.24 covers what they show); the offload step's request reads; core `forget_voiceprints`, `identity_of` and `resolve_person` themselves, which stay for core and in-memory callers and for every path not in R1.

### 16.6 What a skip costs

- **Rule 1.** A damaged live "Me" is neither kept nor merged here; a second readable "Me" still converges. On devices where the row reads, rule 1 runs over it, and their copy then replaces the damaged one here at the next pull (16.1).
