- **Rule 2.** A damaged live summary is not tombstoned here; the readable ones converge among themselves, and every device writes the same tombstones for the rows that read everywhere. The meeting may show the damaged summary as unreadable (T14.24) until a pulled copy replaces it.
- **Rule 3, 3a, a removal's erasure.** A voice row whose deciding ids do not read keeps its vector here; it cannot be matched (the readers that name voices error on it or skip it) and is unsendable. A print or sample whose `person_id` reads as a member, or whose chain reads `Removed`, is always erased.
- **Rule 4.** A damaged edited line does not protect the words under it, a damaged unedited line keeps its words, a damaged tombstoned speaker is not revived. Each converges when the pulled copy replaces the row.
- **Names marks.** A damaged speaker row, or a meeting whose current summary does not read, loses only its `summary-names` mark: that summary is not marked out of date for that name.
- **Dictionary.** `settings/vocabulary` keeps its last list while a word row does not read.
- **Removal, Forget voice, Forget my voice.** A damaged speaker row whose `person_id` does not read loses its identity entry (16.5 item 20: Save answers `NoStoredVoice` and a re-transcription does not carry it, also when the row was never the removed person's) but keeps whatever link it holds; it is not shown (unreadable rows are left out of the detail) and is unsendable, so other devices unlink their copy as usual. A damaged member's person tombstone has its name cleared; it becomes sendable only when nothing else in it was damaged. Remove is refused, with fixed copy, while a member's `is_me` does not read (16.5 item 18): that person stays until the row reads again (a pulled copy), and Forget voice still erases its voices. `PersonUnreadable` (a member's `is_me` does not read) and `PersonNotFound` (the target's own row does not read, 16.5 item 17) are the only ways a damaged row refuses Remove (review 3, A1); every other damaged row is skipped and counted.
- **Kept, honestly** (review 1, B3 and B4; review 2, A1, so this list and 16.3 say the same). These are left on this device, counted, with nothing erased:
  - *A chain with an unreadable `deleted` or `merged_into` step*: `chain_end` is `Unknown` for everyone whose chain passes through it, so rule 3a keeps their prints and samples; `identity_tx` cannot place them, so no Remove, Forget voice or Forget my voice includes them; until the step reads (a pulled copy). `identity_tx` never reads `is_me`; since 7A (T14.49a) `chain_end` reads it at a tombstoned step (`MeTombstone`, 7A.2 D1), where an unreadable `is_me` is `Unknown` and so keeps that chain's voices too. The store's tables are STRICT, so an unreadable `is_me` cannot be stored today (16.10a); otherwise it only refuses Remove.
  - *A voice row whose `person_id` and `voiceprint_id` do not read*: kept with its vector.
  - *Forget my voice when a person row's `is_me` does not read* (16.12 g): a person with an unreadable `is_me` that no `Own` sample names and that is not merged into a starting point keeps its row, prints, samples and identity entries, and the page says not all may have been forgotten; an `Own` sample's `voiceprint_id` link to another person's print never erases that print.
  - *The user's identity entries* (review 4, A2): kept when the user's person is not reached in that case (the entries of every member that is reached are dropped, as in item 20); kept under a pulled person tombstone's side effect when an unplaceable entry could be the user's (next bullet). A user-started erasure of anyone else never keeps one for being possibly the user's.
  - *An identity entry that could be the user's*, only under a pulled person tombstone's side effect (16.5 item 20); a user-started erasure drops it.
  - *A speaker row whose `person_id` or `meeting_id` does not read*: keeps its link; its entry is dropped as above.
  - *A person whose own row does not read* (review 5, A6): left off the People page (item 23), so it cannot be the target of Remove or Forget voice; its prints, samples and entries are kept until the row reads again (a pulled copy). Forget my voice takes it only as g allows (its `id` or `is_me` unreadable triggers g and `incomplete`).
  - *An `Own` sample whose `person_id` reads as a person whose `is_me` reads `false`* (review 5, A6): kept, with that person's print; it names someone else, so it is neither a starting point nor evidence of anything left, and `incomplete` stays `false` for it.
- **Start-up.** The ignore copy runs once and is marked done; a row it skipped as damaged loses its legacy ignore for good on this device (16.12 c), so when the row reads again its voice reads as not ignored here unless another device's copy of the row carries `ignored`. The summary-seen start leaves a meeting with an unreadable current summary unmarked (it may show "new summary" once).
- **Never.** No skip sends a guessed value or stops the page, the tick, the removal, a Forget or the start. The only erasures a damaged value leads to are a removed person's voice vector (16.4, the decided case), a damaged member's name on its tombstone (a constant), a sample whose `id` does not read erased in place by `erase_sample_by_rowid` (16.4, every column it sets a constant), and device-local identity entries (16.5 item 20), all towards erasing what the user asked to erase. Remove refuses only as stated above; the Dictionary edits refuse while a word row does not read (16.5 item 13b).

### 16.7 Counts and what is shown

- **Store.** `Applied::damaged_skipped`: rows the page and its rules skipped, each `(table, rowid)` once per page (a set, so a row skipped by two rules counts once). `PersonRemoval::damaged_skipped` and `Forgotten`'s new `skipped` for removals. Counts only.
- **Erasures are counted where they decide a checkpoint** (review 1, A1). `Forgotten` gains `entries` (identity entries dropped) and `zeroed` (prints whose `sum` did not read and was zeroed; each also counts in `voiceprints`). A new `Forgotten::erased()` is `voiceprints + samples + entries` and decides every checkpoint: `apply_remote`'s side effect adds `erased()` to `Applied::biometrics_forgotten`, so the client's `if applied.biometrics_forgotten > 0` (`engine.rs:1281-1284`) flushes a removal that dropped only centroids; `Store::forget_voiceprints_of` and `remove_person` already checkpoint always. `total()` keeps its meaning (`voiceprints + samples`, the voices shown as forgotten), so no shown count changes; AGENTS' "every flush or shown count uses `total()`" becomes "every flush uses `erased()`, every shown count `total()`" when this is built.
- **IPC and bindings** (review 1, A2). `edit::remove_person` returns `PersonRemoval` with `damaged_skipped`; the desktop's `remove_person` command (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/ipc.rs:1116-1120`, today `Result<(), IpcError>`) returns a new `PersonRemovedView { damaged: bool }`. `edit::list_persons_in` returns `PersonList { persons, damaged_skipped }`; the `list_persons` command (`ipc.rs:1044`) returns `PersonListView { persons: Vec<PersonItem>, damaged: bool }`, and the meeting detail (`detail.rs:942`) takes `.persons`. `forget_person_voices` (`ipc.rs:1130`, `u32`) and `forget_my_voice` (`speaker_settings.rs:587`) gain a `damaged: bool` beside their count. Runtime return types (review 2, A2): `edit::forget_person_voices` and `my_voice::forget_my_voice` return `Forgot { voices: usize, damaged: usize }` instead of `usize`; `edit::list_persons_in`'s other caller `named_after_me_in` (`edit.rs:1837`) takes `.persons`; `Remove` refused for an unreadable `is_me` is `EditError::PersonUnreadable`, mapped by `detail::edit_error` to fixed copy `remove_unreadable` in `fixtures/ui/people_copy_cases.json` ("This person can't be removed because some of their details on this computer are damaged."). Core `Forgotten`'s `AddAssign` adds every field and its derived `PartialEq` covers the new ones, so the core tests that compare a `Forgotten` (`voiceprint/tests.rs`, `sample_tests.rs`) are updated with the new fields at 0. `forget_identity` still deletes a record that does not decode whole, as today (`identities.rs:229-235`), counting it as one in `entries`. `packages/ui/src/ipc/bindings.ts` is regenerated with `EARSHOT_UPDATE_BINDINGS=1 cargo test -p earshot-desktop bindings` in the same commit, with the mock (`createMockIpc`) and the `Ipc` methods updated; tests assert on the serialised JSON that no name or id of a damaged row is in any result.
- **Client.** `TickReport.damaged_skipped`: the size of the union of the tick's page sets. `SyncStatus.damaged_skipped` (last completed tick; `serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_zero")`, so a status without it reads as before and an older build reads a newer record).
- **Sync page.** One note for damaged rows, never two: when `damaged_skipped` or `unsendable` is above 0 the page shows `damaged_note` in place of `unsendable_note` (`fixtures/ui/sync_copy_cases.json:47`). Proposed copy, fixed, no count (the two counts are of different sets): "Some items on this computer are damaged, so sync left them as they are. Everything else syncs as usual." (Draft wording, kept until the build is shown to the owner, 16.12 d.) Both suites read it from the table.
- **People page.** After Remove, Forget voice or Forget my voice with `damaged` true: `remove_damaged_note`, "Removed. Some damaged items on this computer could not be read, so they were left as they are." (Forget uses `forget_damaged_note`, the same sentence with "Forgotten.") When the list skipped persons: `list_damaged_note`, "Some people on this computer are damaged and are not shown." Both in a new table `fixtures/ui/people_copy_cases.json` read by both suites.
- **Start-up repairs.** The count goes into the repair's own device record where it has one (7A's `removal-repair`) and a fixed log word with the count; nothing is shown, since no window is open yet and the Sync page note covers sync.
- **Never content** (R5): a test serialises `Applied`, `TickReport`, `SyncStatus` and `PersonRemoval` after a run over damaged rows holding invented names and asserts none appears.

### 16.8 The scan test

The compiler gives R2 (16.2): a guarded type has no decoding reader to call. The scan test proves only what the compiler cannot. It is `sync_damaged_rows_never_stop_the_guarded_paths` in earshot-runtime (so `cargo test -p earshot-runtime sync` runs it), reading source files of earshot-store and earshot-runtime by path from the workspace root. It strips comments only; string literals are kept and read, because the SQL it judges lives in them (review 2, B2: the first draft stripped strings, so its `zeroblob(` check could never fire).

- **Each root takes a guarded type; each wrapper is one line** (review 4, B1 and B2). The compiler enforces R2 once no root can name a `Services` or a `Store`; the scan checks only signatures and wrappers, for the store and the runtime alike. Store roots (file, function): `guarded.rs` `apply_page`, `chain_end`, `identity_tx` (and, since 7A, `sweep_identities`, which the scan also requires to be opened only through `Store::guarded_immediate`); `sync.rs` `converge`, `dead_person_repair`, `edits_win`, `outbox_sent_in`; `identities.rs` `forget_identity_biometrics`, `forget_identity`; `delete.rs` `remove_person_in`, `unlink_speakers_tx`; `db.rs` `forget_voiceprints_in`. Runtime roots: `summary_names.rs` `names_before`, `names_changed_tx`, `record_tx`; `engine.rs` `meetings_deleted_elsewhere_in` and the `after` closure's function; `vocabulary.rs` `reconcile_in`, `reconcile_tx`, `rebuild_tx`, `remove_rows_tx`, `add_entry_in`, `remove_entry_in`, `set_vocabulary_in`; `edit.rs` `remove_person_in`, `forget_person_voices_in`, `list_persons_in`, `named_after_me_in`; `my_voice.rs` `forget_my_voice_in`; `ignore_copy.rs` `copy_once_in`; `summary_seen.rs` `mark_existing_once_in`. Each root's store parameter is `GuardedTx`, `GuardedRead`, `&ServicesRead` or `&mut ServicesWrite`, and no root has a parameter of type `Services`, `Store` or `StoreTx` (or a reference to one). Each public wrapper named in 16.3 (`edit::remove_person`, `forget_person_voices`, `list_persons`, `my_voice::forget_my_voice`, `ignore_copy::copy_once`, `summary_seen::mark_existing_once`, `vocabulary::reconcile`, `add_entry`, `remove_entry`, `set_vocabulary`) has a body that is exactly one `services.guarded_read()` or `services.guarded_write(..)` call whose closure calls its root. In `engine.rs` the glue functions named in 16.3 (`tick_into`, `try_tick`, `push`, `pull`, `apply`, `retry_held`, `retry_parents`, `meetings_deleted_elsewhere`, `observe_vault`) reach the store only through `self.services.guarded_read()` / `guarded_write(..)`: the scan judges store access only (`.store()`, `.parts()`, a `Store` or `StoreTx` value), never other uses of `Services` (`clock`, `leased_meetings()`, `recording_meetings()`); the one named exception is `apply`'s `self.services.delete_meeting(..)` (T14.26's deletion path). 7A added its roots (7A.12a, 7A.12b).
- **No connection, guard or SQL in a root.** A root's body holds no `.conn`, `execute(`, `query(`, `query_map(`, `query_row(`, `prepare(` or `prepare_cached(`; no `.store()`, `.parts()`, `edit::write(` or `services` (the compiler already refuses these without a `Services` in scope; the scan names them so a later signature change is caught); and names no `Store`, `StoreTx` or `&**db` value. Non-root code may still call `.store()`.
- **SQL constants are used only by their owners.** Every SQL statement a guarded path runs is a `const` in `guarded.rs` (the scan finds each `const NAME: &str = "..."` and reads its text). `SQL_OWNERS` maps each constant to the one function allowed to use it; the scan fails when a constant is used anywhere else in the scanned files, when a constant has no owner, or when an owner entry matches no constant. In `guarded.rs` no `query`, `execute` or `prepare` call takes anything but one of these constants (no inline or formatted SQL). In an owner, a column is read only through the `Col` conversion: a typed `r.get::<`, `r.get(..)?`, `get_id(`, `get_opt_id(` or a row decoder (`speaker_row`, `person_row`, and the rest) outside a `split_rows` call fails.
- **By-rowid writers are named.** A constant whose text holds an `UPDATE` keyed on `rowid` is owned only by a named writer of 16.4 (`erase_print_by_rowid`, `erase_sample_by_rowid`, `unlink_speaker_by_rowid`, `tombstone_person_by_rowid`), and a constant holding `zeroblob(` only by `erase_print_by_rowid`. `ERASE_BY_ROWID` (`delete.rs`, T14.26) is the one named exception outside `guarded.rs`, checked the same way.
- **Fails closed** when a root, an owner or a file is not found, when a `SQL_OWNERS` entry matches nothing, or when a root's signature cannot be parsed.
- **Mutation controls**, each run in the same test on copies of the real files: (1) `query(c, SOME_SQL, &[], |r| get_id(r, 0))` inserted into a copy of an owner fails (strict column read); (2) `self.conn.prepare("SELECT 1")` inserted into a copy of `converge` fails (connection and SQL in a root); (3) a copy of `forget_identity` whose parameter is `&mut StoreTx<'_>` fails (root not guarded); (4) a root renamed in a copy of its file fails closed; (5) `zeroblob(` added to the text of the constant `unlink_speaker_by_rowid` owns fails, and so does `ERASE_PRINT_BY_ROWID_SQL` used from a copy of `unlink_speaker_by_rowid`; (6) `services.store().person(id)` inserted into a copy of the wrapper `edit::remove_person` fails (a wrapper of more than one call), and so does `let (store, hlc) = self.services.store().parts();` inserted into a copy of `engine.rs` `apply` (review 3, B1), while a copy of `apply` that keeps its `self.services.leased_meetings()` call passes (review 5, A1: store access only); (7) a copy of `ignore_copy::copy_once` rewritten to the old route, `edit::write(services, |tx, stamp| ..)` with its body inline, fails (review 4); (8) a copy of `remove_person_in` that calls a new helper `fn names_of(services: &Services, ..)` fails, because the root then needs a `services` parameter (root signature) and the helper is a function taking `&Services` called from a root (review 4). The ignore copy's single run is pinned by test 10, not by the scan.

### 16.9 Tests

Damage is made by SQL in the test (`UPDATE speaker SET role = 'bogus'`, a `display_name` set to a non-UTF-8 blob, a `person_id` blob of 15 bytes), as `crates/earshot-store/tests/delete.rs:338` does. Names are invented. Store tests in `crates/earshot-store/tests/sync_damaged.rs` (names start `store_sync_damaged_`), runtime tests named `sync_damaged_*` and `edit_remove_person_damaged_*`.

Every test states its control (review 2, A3): the same case with the damage removed, or with the fix taken out, gives the other answer, so the test can fail.

1. **A pulled person tombstone with a damaged person row elsewhere**: the page commits; the removed identity's prints, samples and centroids are gone; the damaged row is byte for byte unchanged; rule 1 ran; `damaged_skipped` is 1. Same with the damaged row merged into the removed person (its `merged_into` reads, its `display_name` is a non-UTF-8 blob): its prints and samples are erased by rowid, its row is tombstoned with the name cleared and becomes a readable, sendable tombstone; with a second damaged column (`created_wall` as text) it stays unsendable. Control: on today's code the same page fails and is rolled back (`all_persons` in rule 1).
2. **A damaged speaker row in a meeting with a summary**: a page renames another speaker of that meeting; it commits, the readable row's `summary-names` mark is recorded, the damaged row is skipped. Variant: the current summary is the damaged row; the page commits and that meeting records no mark. Control: with the speaker row repaired, its own rename is marked too.
3. **A damaged row under `forget_identity`**: a damaged speaker row (`role` bogus) linked to the removed person loses its entry; one whose `person_id` does not read loses its entry too and is counted, and its row is byte for byte unchanged; a damaged row whose `person_id` reads as someone else keeps its entry. Control: with the unreadable-`person_id` arm removed, the second case keeps its entry and the test fails.
4. **The People page's Remove with a damaged row**: (a) a damaged speaker row linked to the person is unlinked by rowid (`person_id` NULL, `ignored` 0, "Speaker N" when its name reads, `role` still bogus) and stays unsendable; (b) a damaged speaker row elsewhere is untouched; (c) a damaged person row elsewhere: the list shows everyone else with `list_damaged_note`, and Remove succeeds; (d) a merged-in member whose `is_me` does not read refuses Remove with `PersonUnreadable` and nothing is written; (e) a member whose `is_me` reads true still refuses with `PersonIsTheUser`; (f) a speaker row elsewhere whose `person_id` is a 15-byte blob: the People page lists everyone (today `person_meetings` fails), `list_damaged_note` shows, and Remove and Forget voice both succeed; (g) a live linked row whose `display_name` does not read keeps its name bytes and stays unsendable (the derived "Speaker N" is never written over it). Controls: (a) to (c) and (f) on today's code fail with a store error; (d) with the member's `is_me` readable as `false` removes it; (g) with the name readable writes "Speaker N".
5. **Rules 3 and 3a over damaged voice rows**: a print and a sample of a removed person with damaged `model` are erased by rowid; a sample whose `id` is 15 bytes is erased by `erase_sample_by_rowid` (no voice left, still unsendable); one whose `person_id` and `voiceprint_id` do not read is untouched; a chain with an unreadable `merged_into` step gives `Unknown` and erases nothing. Control: with the step readable and ending at a tombstone, the same page erases them.
5a. **The decided case, a voice vector that does not read** (16.4, 16.12 a): a removed person's print whose `sum` is the damaged column is zeroed with `zeroblob(length(CAST(sum AS BLOB)))` during Remove, Forget voice, Forget my voice and rule 3a. A TEXT `sum` of 8 bytes becomes a readable tombstone with a zeroed two-float vector that is sent and applies on another device as removed (through `LocalRelay`); a blob of 7 bytes is zeroed and stays unreadable and unsendable. Controls: a print of a live person with the same damage is untouched; test 4 (g) shows no derived value is written over an unreadable one.
6. **A pulled meeting tombstone over a damaged local meeting row**: the meeting is deleted through `Services::delete_meeting`. Control: with its `deleted` reading `Yes`, nothing runs.
7. **Rule 4 with damaged rows**: a damaged segment and a damaged speaker in a meeting with an edit clash; the page commits and the readable lines follow rule 4 as today. Control: the same meeting without the damaged rows gives the same readable rows.
8. **The Dictionary rebuild with a damaged word row**: `settings/vocabulary` is unchanged and the skip counted; once the row is replaced by a pulled copy, the next page rebuilds. Control: with no damaged row, a page that brings a new word changes the list.
8a. **The Dictionary reconcile and a tick** (review 2, B1): with Dictionary sync on and one damaged `dictionary_word` row, a tick runs `reconcile` and then pushes and pulls; a word listed in `settings/vocabulary` with no readable row gets no new row while the damaged row exists, and the readable quote-only rows are still tombstoned. Turning Dictionary syncing on, setting up and joining succeed with the list kept. Control: on today's code the tick fails at `reconcile` before the push; with the row repaired, the listed word gets its row.
9. **`outbox_sent` with a row damaged after its send**: the tick ends normally and the entry stays queued. Control: an undamaged sent row leaves the outbox.
10. **Start-up**: the summary-seen start finishes with a damaged speaker row and a damaged meeting row present and writes its record. The ignore copy with a damaged speaker row holding a legacy ignore (16.12 c): it flags the readable rows and writes `ignore-copied` in the same transaction; a second start and two completed ticks (through the production `copy_after_tick`) run nothing; the damaged row's legacy flag no longer counts once the record exists. A pulled un-ignore of a readable row made after the copy is never overwritten by a later start. Controls: with the damaged row readable, its ignore is copied too; with `ignore-copied` removed by hand, the next start runs the copy once more (the record, not a flag, is what stops it).
11. **Healing through `LocalRelay`**: two devices; one damages a row it holds; the other edits that row; the damaged device's pages commit, and after the pull its copy reads again and every rule converges with the other device. Control: before the pull, the two devices differ on that row.
12. **Same answers without damage** (review 2, B3 and B6): on stores with no damaged row (generated, with merges, voices, tombstoned `is_me` persons and people with and without effective voice counts), the new store-side `forget_identity_biometrics` erases exactly the rows core `forget_voiceprints` erases; `chain_end` answers as `ends_deleted` does, a readable tombstoned `is_me` step included (`Removed`, so rule 3a erased there as before; 7A's `MeTombstone` deliberately changed that case, 7A.11); `list_persons_in` gives the same list and the same `has_voice` as `live_prints_of(..).any(|(_, e)| e.count() > 0)`; and `remove_person`, rules 1 to 4 and the names marks write the same rows as before. All pinned against outputs captured from the commit before the change (AGENTS: a default-equals-old test pins literal outputs, never the new code against itself). The generated stores include samples `fold` leaves out (another model, another length, another person, a non-finite value) on a print whose base count is 0, so `has_voice` must say no voice for it, as today (review 3, B3). Control: one generated store with a person whose only print has effective count 0 shows no voice in both, and the same store with one matching sample added shows a voice in both. Forget my voice is pinned too (review 5, A7): on the undamaged stores its count (`Forgot.voices`, the `total()` of today's `forget_voiceprints_of`) and the live count it answers with when the checkpoint is `Busy` (today's `my_voice.rs:378-406` set of live print and sample ids) equal the captured outputs, and `damaged` and `incomplete` are 0 and `false`; the count does not change, because the new trigger (g) never fires on an undamaged store. *Changed by 7A* (T14.49a): the store-side digest (`store_sync_damaged_same_answers_without_damage_as_before`) covers whole stores, so it moved with every 7A rule its generated pages reach, not only `MeTombstone`. Its per-step store dumps before 7A and with it were compared table by table (T14.49f; seeds 1 and 4 also by undoing only `MeTombstone`, which moves both first changes a page later, and the extra tick below by moving only that observe): in every seed they are equal up to the first page where a 7A rule acts. Seed 0: P4, a pulled live copy written over the user's tombstone here. Seed 1: `MeTombstone`, after the test's own `remove_person` of an `is_me` person; of two samples the page pulls, the one of that "Me" is no longer erased by rule 3a but by rule 3 (its print is tombstoned) right after it, so the two erasures swap their logical stamps and nothing else changes. Seeds 2 and 5: P3 unlinking speaker rows; in the same page the prints and samples rule 3a erases are stamped one logical tick later, because P1's decision (`tombstone_final`) observes a pulled person row's stamp once more before writing it. Seed 3: P1 writing a pulled person tombstone over a later live copy here, stamped again. Seed 4: `MeTombstone` again, a pulled sample of that "Me" left live. The digest was re-captured then, and each of those rules is pinned with literal expectations in `crates/earshot-store/tests/sync_removed.rs`.
13. **Other errors still fail**: with the `person` table renamed away, the page fails and nothing is written. Control: with the table back, the same page commits.
14. **Counts never content** (16.7), including the IPC results' serialised JSON. Control: the invented names are found in the store by the same search, so a miss is not a search that cannot find them.
15. **Forget my voice with a damaged person row**: an unrelated person row with a non-UTF-8 name, and separately the user's own row with a damaged `display_name` (its `is_me` reads): both runs succeed and erase the user's prints, samples and entries. A person merged into the user whose `is_me` does not read is a member and its voices are forgotten too (membership never reads `is_me`). Control: on today's code both runs fail with a store error.
15a. **Forget my voice when a person row's `is_me` does not read** (16.12 g): person U (the user, `is_me` not readable) has a print with two live `Own` samples, a second print made before voice samples (base count 2, no sample) and an identity entry; person V is merged into U; person W (`is_me` reads `false`) has a print P named by the `voiceprint_id` of a third `Own` sample whose `person_id` is U. Forget my voice erases both of U's prints, the `Own` samples, V's voices and U's and V's entries; it keeps W's print P byte for byte. Controls: with W's `is_me` unreadable and W named as `person_id` by an `Own` sample, W's prints are erased too; an `Own` sample whose `person_id` is W while W's `is_me` reads `false` leaves that sample and W's print (W is someone else); with a second person X whose `is_me` does not read and that no `Own` sample names, the result is `incomplete: true` and the page shows `forget_my_voice_incomplete`, and without X it is not incomplete. Case for an unreadable `id` (review 5, A2): with a person row Y whose `id` is a 15-byte blob (its `is_me` readable as `true`), g fires, Y's voices are not reachable and the result is `incomplete: true`; with Y's `id` repaired it is not.
16. **Forget voice with a damaged row** elsewhere (a person row, a speaker row with a 15-byte `person_id`, a damaged sample of another person): it succeeds, and the damaged rows are byte for byte unchanged. A member of the target whose `is_me` does not read has its voices forgotten. Control: on today's code it fails with a store error.
17. **The next page after a refused removal** (review 2, B4): Remove refused for a member whose `is_me` does not read leaves every row as it was; a following page that writes a person row (so rule 3a runs) changes nothing for that identity, since no tombstone was written. Control: with the member's `is_me` readable as `false`, Remove goes ahead and the following page finds the identity's voices already erased.
18. **An entry matching no readable row** (B4 of review 1): a meeting holds a speaker row whose `id` is a 15-byte blob and an identity entry naming an id no readable row has. Remove and Forget voice of another identity drop the entry, whatever the unreadable row's `role`. Under a pulled person tombstone's side effect the entry is dropped too, except that with the unreadable-id row's `role` reading `Me` (or not reading) it is kept. Control: with the unreadable-id row repaired and naming someone else, the entry is kept in every case.
19. **A centroid-only removal is checkpointed** (A1 of review 1): a pulled person tombstone whose identity has an identity entry and no voices sets `Applied::biometrics_forgotten` above 0 through `Forgotten::erased()`, and the client calls `flush_erasures`. Control: an identity with no entry and no voice leaves it at 0 and no flush runs.
20. **Dictionary page edits while a word row does not read**: `add_entry`, `remove_entry` and `set_vocabulary` are refused through `Rebuild::Unread`, `Reconciled::Unread` or their own split reads (16.5 item 13b) and roll back, with Dictionary sync on and off; the page shows `dictionary_damaged`; with sync off a tick heals nothing and the refusal stays; after the row is repaired they succeed. Control: on today's code the edit fails with a store error rather than the refusal.
21. **Named aggregates over bad blobs**: rule 2's `crowded_meetings()` with a summary whose `meeting_id` is 15 bytes, and the summary-seen start with a current summary whose `id` is 15 bytes: the page and the start finish, the row is counted. Control: on today's code both fail.

Accept: as T14.57's entry in PLAN.md (`cargo test -p earshot-store sync`; `cargo test -p earshot-runtime sync`; `cargo test -p earshot-runtime edit`; `cargo xtask accept m11`). The entry's `accept` already holds the commands the IPC changes of 16.7 need (`cargo test -p earshot-runtime my_voice`, `cargo test -p earshot-desktop --bin earshot`, `cmd /c pnpm --filter @earshot/ui test`, `cmd /c pnpm -r build`).

### 16.10 How section 7A uses this

7A (T14.49) is built after this section and relies on it:

- 7A.2 D1 is replaced by R2 here: every reader 7A adds runs on a `GuardedTx` or `GuardedRead`, its roots join the scan test, and the "Known, left to T14.57" list goes.
- `chain_end` is defined here with three outcomes and today's answers (`is_me` not read; `Unknown` only for an unreadable `deleted` or `merged_into` step, a loop or an unknown person); 7A adds `MeTombstone` (P4) with its `is_me` read and decides then what an unreadable `is_me` means there; P2 and rule 3a treat `Unknown` as "changes nothing".
- P3's damaged-row rewrite is `unlink_speakers_tx` (16.5 item 21) with 16.4's written-columns rule; P2's inputs read through `person_links`; P2's and P3's tombstone writes use `tombstone_person_by_rowid` and `unlink_speaker_by_rowid`.
- The widened `names_before` set uses the per-row skips of 16.5 items 12 and 14, so 7A no longer needs its per-meeting skip.
- 7A's removal repair and identity sweep are start-up repairs under R1, and their roots join the scan test.
- 7A's P5 drops an entry when a row's `person_id` does not read (`relinked`, `from` unreadable), and 16.5 item 20 does the same in `forget_identity` (16.12 b), so the two agree.

### 16.10a As built (T14.57, 2026-11-27)

- The store's tables are STRICT, so some damage this section imagined cannot be stored: an unreadable `person.is_me` (an `INTEGER NOT NULL`, every integer reads as a bool) and a TEXT print `sum`. `PersonUnreadable` keeps its code path and copy but has no test; rule g's trigger is reached through an unreadable `id`.
- Statements a guarded path runs that T14.47 placed in `replaced.rs` and `sync.rs` stay there as named constants registered in `EXTERNAL_SQL_OWNERS` (in `guarded.rs`), held by the scan to the same owner rules.
- Runtime code calls `StoreTx::guarded` only in `name_changed_tx`, `drop_stale_tx` and `syncing_turned_on_tx` (`GUARDED_FROM_STORE_TX` in `sync_damaged_scan.rs`), each with its reason.
- A sample whose `person_id` and `voiceprint_id` both do not read, and a print whose `person_id` does not read, are counted and left on this device (whose they are cannot be known); the "left as they are" notes say so.
- `dictionary_damaged` has two forms, by whether Dictionary sync is on; the copy was approved by the owner.
- Clearing a damaged row today needs a good copy from another device (test 11) or the deletion of its meeting; a way for the user to clear the rest is a later task.

### 16.11 Formats and other builds

- No row changes shape and no new row is written: `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` stays 4, no migration, no relay change, no wire field, no `sync_pending` reason.
- `SyncStatus.damaged_skipped` is an optional field left out when 0, so the stored `sync-status` record of a store without damage is byte for byte as before.
- Rows this rule writes are rows the readable path already writes (unlinked speakers, tombstoned persons, erased voices) with this device's stamp; a 0.9.0 computer reads them as it does today. A damaged row that stays damaged is never sent, so nothing new reaches another device.
- A 0.9.0 computer with a damaged row of its own still stops as today; this needs the update.

### 16.12 Decided by the owner (2026-11-27)

a. **A voice vector that does not read is zeroed during a removal** (16.4, the decided case). It is biometric data of a removed person; the zeroed row syncs as removed. It is the one deliberate exception to "never clear a value that failed to read" (R4): the written-columns rule (16.4) otherwise sets an unread column only to a constant the readable path writes whatever the old value, and the zeroed vector's length comes from the old value. Written with `zeroblob(length(CAST(sum AS BLOB)))`; pinned by test 5a and by the scan test's named by-rowid writers (16.8).
b. **An identity entry whose row's `person_id` does not read is dropped**, in `forget_identity` too (16.5 item 20), matching 7A's P5 (errs towards erasing). Test 3. Extended after review 1 (coordinator, within this answer): an entry matching no readable row while the meeting holds a row whose `id` does not read is dropped the same way; either kind is kept when it could be the user's only under a pulled person tombstone's side effect; a user-started erasure (Remove, Forget voice, Forget my voice) drops it whatever it might be (after review 2). Tests 3 and 18.
c. **The ignore copy runs once and marks itself done even when it skipped a damaged row** (the owner, 2026-11-27, after review 4, reversing the earlier answer "not done while it skipped"): a run again later could overwrite an un-ignore another computer made and this one already pulled, since the copy cannot tell its own value from a pulled one. There is no rerun and no per-process flag; the skipped row's legacy flag is lost (16.5 item 24, 16.6). Test 10.
d. **Copy**: the draft wording of 16.7 (`damaged_note`, `remove_damaged_note`, `list_damaged_note`) stays for now; it is shown to the owner when the build lands.
e. **Scope**: the wider scope stays (meeting, segment and Dictionary word rows on these paths, 16.5 items 7 to 10, 13, 15, 16, 24, 25, and the People page list, item 23). After review 1 it includes every user-started biometric erasure (Remove, Forget voice, Forget my voice; items 22, 23a).
f. **`is_me` decides only refusals of Remove** (coordinator after review 2, replacing the review-1 wording, within the owner's 7A.12: sync never removes the user's own person). Identity membership, on every path (Remove, Forget voice, Forget my voice, a pulled person tombstone), depends only on `id` and `merged_into`, never on `is_me`; `chain_end` does not read `is_me` (7A adds that with `MeTombstone`). Remove is refused when a member's `is_me` reads `true` (`PersonIsTheUser`, as today) or does not read (`PersonUnreadable`); Forget voice and Forget my voice cover every member whatever its `is_me`. Forget my voice starts from the persons whose `is_me` reads `true`, and in the case of g also from those g adds. Tests 4 (d), 15, 16, 17.
g. **Forget my voice when a person row's `is_me` does not read** (the owner, 2026-11-27, after review 4, with the reviewer's check): it also erases by the "Your voice" samples (origin `Own`). The persons those samples name by `person_id` are further starting points, each only when its `is_me` does not read `false`; from each, `identity_tx` covers merged-in persons, base-only prints and identity entries. A print is taken only by its own `person_id`, never through a sample's `voiceprint_id` naming another person's print. The trigger is any person row whose `is_me` or `id` does not read (the `id` added after review 5, A2), and `incomplete` is defined the same way: such a person neither reached as a starting point nor a member. Tests 15a (with the control that another person's print named by an `Own` sample survives).

### 16.13 Revisions

First draft (2026-11-27), not yet spec-reviewed. Written from T14.57's entry and 7A.2 D1's list; the code search added: rule 3a's strict typed column reads and print rewrite, rule 3's sample query, rule 2's `summaries_of`, rule 4's meeting checks, `segments_of` and `speakers_of`, the Dictionary rebuild, `meetings_deleted_elsewhere`, `outbox_sent`, `Store::remove_person`'s `identity_of` and `tx.person`, `edit::remove_person`'s target and user checks, `list_persons_in`, the ignore copy and the summary-seen start (16.5).

- R4 and 16.4 item 3 state the zeroing of a removed person's unreadable voice vector as R4's one deliberate exception, through one writer, `erase_print_by_rowid` (16.5 items 3 and 11); new test 5a; 16.6 "Never" names the two erasures a damaged value leads to.
- `forget_identity` drops an entry whose row's `person_id` does not read (16.5 item 20), as 7A's P5 does; 16.6, test 3 and 16.10 follow.
- The ignore copy writes `ignore-copied` only after a run that skipped nothing and runs again at each start until then (16.5 item 24); 16.6 and test 10 follow.

The scan test now proves only that each root takes a guarded type and that no SQL appears outside the named lenient readers, with a mutation control inserting `query(.., |r| get_id(r, 0))` (16.8). 16.5 is the migration checklist, not the guarantee.

Tests 4 (f), 15, 16, 21.
- (Superseded by review 2, B4 and B6: membership never reads `is_me`, `chain_end` does not read it, and an unreadable `is_me` only refuses Remove.) - A1: `Forgotten` gains `entries` and `zeroed`, and `erased()` decides checkpoints, so a centroid-only removal is flushed in the apply path (16.7); test 19. A2: the IPC result types and the `bindings.ts` regeneration are named, and `rebuild_tx` itself changes, with `add_entry` / `remove_entry` keeping their refusal (16.5 item 13, 16.7); test 20. A3: the summary-seen start reads only the current summary's id, leniently (16.5 item 25).
- Lessons from the review (the reviewer's): (1) on a path that must not stop on a damaged row, every SQL read goes through `split_rows` or a lenient `Col` reader, and any other `query`, `query_row` or `query_map` there is refused by an allowlist scan, never a denylist; a reader list found by searching is a checklist, never the guarantee; (2) every erasure of biometric data the user can start (Remove, Forget voice, Forget my voice) keeps working with a damaged row anywhere in the store.

After spec review 2 (2026-11-27: blocked on six points and three advisories; decisions by the coordinator within the owner's answers):

- B1: the Dictionary `reconcile` / `reconcile_tx`, which runs before push and pull on every tick and inside the Dictionary edits and turning syncing on, is a guarded path (R1, 16.5 item 13a); tests 8a and 20.
- B2: the reviewer's layering. Every guarded root is a free function taking a guarded type; `Store` and `StoreTx` give those modules only `guarded_*` constructors; the page loop's needs are named guarded methods (`synced_by_id_or_damaged`, `row_reads_deleted`, `request_reads_withdrawn_by`, `outbox_forget`, `hold`, `unhold`, `write_or_hold` as a guarded savepoint, `identity_record_keys`). - A1: 16.3 and 16.6 now say the same about chains through an unreadable step. A2: the interface changes are named (guarded `record_tx` and `local::set_guarded`, every `Rebuild::Unread` caller at `vocabulary.rs:344`, `490`, `578`, `624`, core `Forgotten`'s `AddAssign` and equality, `Forgot` as the return of `forget_person_voices` and `forget_my_voice`, `named_after_me_in`, `forget_identity` still deleting an undecodable record whole) (16.5, 16.7). A3: the ignore copy's cost while undone is stated (16.5 item 24), and every test states its control (16.9).

  - "A path that must not stop on a damaged row includes every reader that runs before it in the same loop, not only the ones inside the transaction."
  - "Whether a person is the user decides only refusals of removal; it never narrows which rows an erasure the user started covers."

After spec review 3 (2026-11-27: blocked on four points, narrowing, and four advisories; decisions by the coordinator within the owner's answers):

- B1: the runtime half of the boundary. `Services` gets `guarded_read()` and `guarded_write(..)`, holding the lock guard (and the HLC for writes); runtime roots reach the store only through them. The scan refuses `.store()` and `.parts()` in root bodies as well as the type names, covers `tick_into`, `pull` and `apply` in `engine.rs`, and has mutation control 6 (`services.store().person(id)`) (16.3, 16.8).

- A1: `Store::remove_person` computes the identity inside its own transaction, under the same lock guard as `edit::remove_person`'s; 16.4's column list is scoped to speaker, person and print rows; `erase_sample_by_rowid` is in 16.6 "Never"; `PersonUnreadable` and `PersonNotFound` are stated once as the only damaged-row refusals of Remove; the review-1 B3 bullet is marked superseded.
- A2: the Dictionary edits' own readers are item 13b; with Dictionary sync off a damaged word row refuses every Dictionary edit until it is repaired, and the page says so with `dictionary_damaged`; test 20.

- A4: `edit::list_persons`, `detail::persons` and `AfterApply` are named; erasures write in today's order so test 12's literal outputs hold (16.4); item 19 keeps the deleted-meeting filter.
- Lessons from the review (the reviewer's):
  - "`copy_after_tick` runs `ignore_copy::copy_once` after every completed tick, and only the `ignore-copied` record stops it. A rule that leaves the record unwritten needs its own per-process limit."
  - "A scan of roots that take `&Services` refuses `.store()` and `.parts()` too, not only the type names: a method call through the lock guard's Deref reads a whole row."

After spec review 4 (2026-11-27: blocked on five points and three advisories; the owner decided two, the rest by the reviser):

- The owner reversed 16.12 c: the ignore copy runs once and writes `ignore-copied` even when it skipped a damaged row, because a rerun could overwrite an un-ignore already pulled from another computer. `RAN_THIS_START` and the rerun are gone; the lost legacy flag is stated (16.5 item 24, 16.6); test 10 rewritten. This supersedes review 3's B2 entry above.
- The owner decided 16.12 g: with any person row's `is_me` unreadable, Forget my voice also starts from the persons the `Own` samples name, each only when its `is_me` does not read `false`, through `identity_tx`; prints only by their own `person_id`, never through a sample's `voiceprint_id`; `incomplete` by the same trigger (16.5 item 23a, 16.6); test 15a has the control that another person's print named by an `Own` sample survives. This supersedes review 3's B4 entry above.
- B1, B2: runtime roots take guarded parameters (`&ServicesRead`, `&mut ServicesWrite` with guarded reads, guarded transactions under one lock and the store's guarded erasures that commit then checkpoint, or `GuardedTx` / `GuardedRead`); each public `fn x(services, ..)` is a one-line wrapper over `services.guarded_*`. The compiler enforces R2; the scan checks root signatures and wrappers only, with mutation controls 7 (the `edit::write(services, ..)` route) and 8 (a helper taking `services`). `edit::remove_person`'s two transactions and `forget_voiceprints_of`'s checkpoint run under the one `ServicesWrite` guard (16.3, 16.8).

- Lessons from the review (the reviewer's):
  - "A once-per-start guard is kept per store or `Services`, never as a process static: lib test binaries run many stores on parallel threads."
  - "A repair that may run again never rewrites a row whose current value came from a pull after its last run."

After spec review 5 (2026-11-27: passed, with seven advisories, all fixed):

## 17. Damaged items can be cleared by the user (T14.62)

Status: proposed (first draft, 2026-11-27), not spec-reviewed, not built. Built after sections 16 (T14.57) and 7A (T14.49), which it relies on. No row changes shape: `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` stays 4, there is no migration, no relay change and no wire field. Open questions for the owner are in 17.12.

### 17.1 The problem

After section 16 a damaged row (16.1: its stored values do not convert for this build, `repo::is_unreadable`) is left as it is and noted: on the Sync page (`damaged_note`), on the People page (`list_damaged_note` and the result notes after Remove, Forget voice and Forget my voice), and on the Dictionary page (`dictionary_damaged`). It clears only when a pulled copy replaces it (16.1, test 11 of 16.9) or its meeting is deleted (T14.26). A row that no pull ever reaches stays for good, and with it the note, with nothing the user can do (16.10a, last point). This section gives the user two actions:

- **Repair** (17.3): this computer reads the whole vault again, so every damaged row that has a good copy at the relay is replaced. Sync must be set up and on.
- **Remove damaged items** (17.4 to 17.6): what is still damaged is deleted on this computer only, never sent, so the other computers keep their copies. Works with sync on, off or never set up.

It is a rare-case tool: both actions are started by the user, reuse what exists, and change no sync rule.

### 17.2 Where the actions are

- **One shared control.** A "Remove damaged items" button that opens one confirmation dialog (17.6), the same component on every page that shows a damaged note: beside the Sync page's `damaged_note` (`packages/ui/src/settings/SyncPage.tsx:351`), beside the People page's `list_damaged_note` and its three result notes (`remove_damaged_note`, `forget_damaged_note`, `forget_my_voice_incomplete`), and beside the Dictionary page's `dictionary_damaged`. So a computer that never set up sync (whose Sync page has no Status section) can still remove what the People or Dictionary page found.
- **Repair** is a button beside the Sync page's note only, shown when sync is set up and on (`SyncView.set_up && SyncView.on`). With sync off or not set up only Remove is offered (T14.62). A Repair in progress shows its progress line there instead of the button (17.3.4).
- When sync is set up and on, the dialog adds one line suggesting Repair first (`dialog_try_repair`, 17.7), because Remove deletes rows Repair might have replaced.

### 17.3 Repair

#### 17.3.1 The mechanism

Repair is the existing pull restart a switch uses when it is turned on (`set_sync_voiceprints` and `set_sync_dictionary`, `crates/earshot-runtime/src/sync/mod.rs:540-573`): `restart_pull_tx` (`sync/mod.rs:686-695`) sets the `sync-cursor` record's `pull_seq` to 0 and keeps `seen_seq`, `epoch` and `resets`. The next tick pulls the vault from its first row, page by page, through the ordinary `apply_remote`. Nothing else changes:

- **No re-push and no `queue_everything`.** The relay keeps every row any device ever pushed, its latest copy, and never deletes a row (section 4: "Rows are never deleted"; an accepted push replaces the older copy of that key). So a pull from 0 hears every row the relay holds, and no other device needs to push anything. `queue_everything` (`crates/earshot-store/src/sync.rs:1405-1422`) is for joining another vault and is not called; Repair queues nothing and sends nothing beyond what the tick would send anyway.
- **Not a vault reset.** Because `seen_seq` and `epoch` are kept, `judge_vault` (`sync/mod.rs:727-764`) still reads the relay's history as the same (`before` is not below `Cursor::seen()`), so Repair never counts towards `MAX_VAULT_RESETS` and never re-queues.
- **Applying a page twice changes nothing** (6.2): a pulled row that is not newer than the local copy is ignored, and side effects (5.4) follow only what was written. This is what the switch restart and the vault reset already rely on.
- **The cursor race** is the existing one: the cursor moves only by compare-and-set from the value its page was pulled after (`advance_cursor`, `sync/mod.rs:662-681`), so a Repair pressed while a page is on the way wins, as a switch does.

The Repair command (`repair_sync`, 17.8) runs `restart_pull_tx` and writes the device record `sync-repair` (17.3.4) in one store transaction, then asks the sync thread for a tick at once (as "Sync now" does). It is refused (`invalid`, never reached by the page) when sync is not set up or is off. It may run while a meeting records: a pull already runs then, and rows of a leased meeting are held `busy` as always (5.3 step 4).

#### 17.3.2 What a re-pull heals

T14.26 made `apply_remote` treat a local row that does not read as absent (`crates/earshot-store/src/guarded.rs:1683-1725`): the pulled copy is written whatever the two stamps, and its outbox entry goes with the write (`write_or_hold`). So a damaged row is replaced by any readable pulled copy of it, with these exceptions, unchanged by this section:

- **A tombstone here stays final** (`stays_final`, `guarded.rs:1698-1720`): a damaged meeting, voiceprint or folder row whose `deleted` reads `Yes` against a live pulled copy; the same for a person row (not against a pulled "Me", 7A.2 P4); an erased voice sample against a live copy; this device's withdrawal of its own summary request against an open one. The pulled copy is ignored and the damaged row stays. A pulled tombstone does replace it.
- **A kind switched off** (5.3 step 1): voiceprints (off by default) and, when turned off, Dictionary words are not applied, so their damaged rows are not healed.
- **A pulled copy that does not open or decode** is held `format` (5.3 step 2), not written.
- **No copy at the relay**: a row nobody ever pushed. That is a row damaged here before its first push (it then sits in this outbox as unsendable), a row of a kind every device keeps switched off, the rows of a meeting being recorded, and a row refused before sealing (6.2).
- **An unsent edit is lost.** A row edited here and damaged before it was pushed is replaced by the older copy the relay holds. Its newer values did not read, so nothing readable is lost.

Damaged rows that remain after a Repair are what Remove is for.

#### 17.3.3 Cost

A Repair reads what joining a new computer reads: every row body the relay holds, tombstones included (transcripts, summaries, notes, people, voices and words; never audio, which is not synced). It sends nothing extra. It is bounded by the relay's page limits (at most 500 rows and 4 MiB of bodies per page, section 3) and runs on the sync thread, one tick at a time, with the store lock held only for each page's transaction (6.2). Time and bytes grow with the vault; the build measures both on `sync_eval` with a generated vault of a stated size and writes the figures here (17.11 test 16). No figure is claimed before that.

#### 17.3.4 Progress and result

- **Record.** Device record `sync-repair` (`DeviceRecord::SyncRepair`, `version u32 LE | JSON {state}`, `state` `armed` or `reading`), written only by `repair_sync` (`armed`) and by the pull (`reading`, when it fetches a page with `after = 0` while the record says `armed`). It exists only while a Repair runs, so a restart resumes it. Setting up, joining and Remove this device delete it (another vault, or none).
- **Done.** A Repair is done at the end of the first tick whose pull ended with no further rows (`TickReport.pull_complete`) while the record says `reading`: it read from 0 to the relay's last row. That tick then deletes the record, runs the damaged-items scan (17.4.1) and reports `TickReport.repair_done: Option<u64>`, the number of damaged rows still here. Requiring `reading` first means a tick that was already past 0 when Repair was pressed (and whose cursor move then failed the compare-and-set) never ends it.
- **Progress.** While the record exists the Sync page shows `repair_running` with a percentage: `pull_seq * 100 / seen_seq` from the `sync-cursor` record, rounded down and at most 99 (sequence numbers have gaps, so this is an estimate of the vault read, never a promise). While sync is stopped by a problem (`SyncView.stopped`) the existing problem bar says why and the percentage does not move.
- **Result.** `SyncHost` keeps the last `repair_done` in memory and the page shows it once the Repair has ended: `repair_done_none` when nothing damaged is left (the note then goes, 17.7), `repair_done_left` with the count otherwise, beside the Remove button. It is cleared by Remove, by a new Repair and by a restart, and is never stored.

### 17.4 Remove: which rows

#### 17.4.1 The scan

Remove's set is every row of a synced table (`SYNCED_TABLES`, `crates/earshot-store/src/schema.rs:385`) that does not read: section 16's definition, judged by the same row decoders through `split_rows`, nothing looser. A new store reader `damaged_rows(table, after_rowid, limit) -> (Vec<rowid>, last_rowid)` walks each table in rowid order, 500 rows per read, each read under one short `guarded_read` (the store lock is released between reads, so a recording meeting's writes are never held up for the whole scan). It reads whole rows to decode them, so its cost grows with the store; the build measures it on a generated store and writes the figure here (17.11 test 16). It runs off the main thread (`ipc::blocking`) when the dialog opens, when Remove runs, and at the end of a Repair. The scan's count is as of the scan; Remove re-checks each row in its own transaction (17.5).

Not in the set: device-local tables and records (`local_state`, `sync_pending`, `sync_outbox`), and NeverSync rows (`audio_file`, `export_record`), whose damage T14.26's deletion handles.

#### 17.4.2 Why deletion

Remove deletes rows; it writes no column of any synced row. That keeps section 16's written-columns rule (R4, 16.4) trivially: nothing is set on a guess, because nothing is set. It also gives the "this computer only" property without a new sync rule:

- **Never queued.** The outbox triggers fire on `INSERT` and `UPDATE` only (5.2), so a `DELETE` queues nothing; Remove also deletes the row's existing `sync_outbox` entry, matched by `tbl` and the row's raw stored `id` bytes. No tombstone is written, so nothing reaches the relay or another computer, and their copies stay as they are.
- **Healable later.** A deleted row is absent here, so any later pull of a good copy (a Repair, another computer's edit, a switch turned on) writes it back as a new row (5.3 step 6). Remove does not prevent that, by design: the other computers still hold the row.
- **Erased from the file.** The store runs with `secure_delete` on, and Remove ends with `flush_erasures` (the truncating checkpoint), so deleted bytes leave the database pages and the WAL, as a biometric erasure does (PLAN 3.9). A `Busy` after the commit means the removal is committed (as for `forget_voiceprints`).

### 17.5 Remove: what happens to each row

One IMMEDIATE transaction with `PRAGMA defer_foreign_keys = ON` (the store enforces foreign keys: `track`, `speaker`, `segment`, `note`, `summary`, `summary_request`, `audio_file` and `export_record` reference `meeting(id)`; `segment` references `track(id)` and `speaker(id)`; `audio_file` references `track(id)`). For each row of the scan, re-read by rowid inside the transaction (a row that now reads, or is gone, is skipped):

| Damaged row | What Remove does |
|---|---|
| `segment`, `note`, `summary`, `summary_request`, `folder`, `dictionary_word` | Deleted by rowid. |
| `voiceprint_sample` | Deleted by rowid. |
| `voiceprint` | Deleted by rowid, with every sample whose stored `voiceprint_id` equals its stored `id` (raw byte equality, so it works when either does not read). |
| `person` | Deleted by rowid, with every print and sample whose stored `person_id` equals its stored `id` (raw), and, when its `id` reads, its identity entries (`forget_identity` with that one member, user-started, 16.5 item 20). Speaker rows linked to it keep their link and stored name (rewriting them would be a synced write); readable persons merged into it keep their rows, their chain ending at a person not known here, as for a person not yet pulled (`chain_end` `Unknown`). |
| `track` or `speaker` that no `segment` or `audio_file` row references (raw equality) | Deleted by rowid; for a speaker whose `meeting_id` and `id` read, its identity entry is dropped. |
| `track` or `speaker` still referenced | **Left as it is** and counted `in_use` (17.12 question 2). |
| `meeting` | **The whole meeting, on this computer only** (17.5.1). |

A damaged tombstone is deleted like a live row (17.12 question 1): a meeting, voiceprint, person or folder row whose `deleted` reads `Yes`, or an erased sample. Its content was cleared when it was written, so nothing readable is lost, but a live copy pulled later (possible only when this computer's deletion never reached the relay) then comes back here, as it is on the computers that still hold it. A row whose `deleted` does not read is deleted the same way: whether it was a tombstone cannot be known, and deleting writes no guess.

Device-local per-row records that name a deleted row (word choices and unknown-line marks by segment id, summary-seen and summary-names by summary id, summary edits) keep their entries; every reader already ignores an entry whose row is gone (as after a re-transcription replaces lines). The build checks this per record and lists any reader that does not (17.11 test 11).

#### 17.5.1 A damaged meeting row

The meeting cannot be read, so nothing of it can be shown or opened, and its children cannot be deleted alone without breaking the foreign keys. So the whole meeting goes, on this computer only, through a new store function `remove_meeting_here(rowid)` beside `delete_meeting` (`crates/earshot-store/src/delete.rs:287`), which reuses its file step:

1. Audio files first, each before its row, read from the meeting's `audio_file` rows matched by raw `meeting_id`, then, when the meeting's `id` reads, its audio folder (`remove_meeting_audio_dir`), as `delete_meeting` does.
2. In the transaction: every row of `segment`, `note`, `summary`, `summary_request`, `speaker`, `track`, `audio_file` and `export_record` whose stored `meeting_id` equals the meeting row's stored `id` (raw), readable or not, then the meeting row, each with its outbox entry. Nothing is tombstoned.
3. When the meeting's `id` reads, the device-local keys `Services::delete_meeting` erases for a meeting (the `meeting_records!` list and the export lease).
4. People, voiceprints and voice samples stay, whichever meeting they came from, as for a deletion (T9.1u).

The meeting's readable rows go too: they are counted separately (`meetings`, 17.7) and the dialog says so, with their recordings. On a computer where this is the only copy (sync never set up, or the meeting never pushed) the meeting is gone for good (17.12 question 3). The other computers keep it; a later Repair brings it back here without its recordings, as a meeting synced from another computer.

### 17.6 Remove: when it runs and how it is confirmed

- **Never while a meeting records or finishes, or a meeting job runs.** Remove takes a new pass, `WorkGate::enter_damaged_removal` (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/activity.rs`), refused `busy` (`busy` copy, 17.7) while `work > 0` (meetings, which hold their pass through finishing, and meeting jobs) or `left > 0` (an after-meeting thread that stopped answering), and while it is held `enter_meeting` and `enter_work` refuse `busy` with the same kind of copy. It is short (no model, no network), so a meeting started by the tray or the shortcut in that moment is refused once and can be started again; nothing records meanwhile. It is held from the re-scan to the end of the checkpoint. A refusal writes nothing.
- **Confirmed with a count.** The dialog runs the scan (`damaged_items`, 17.8) and shows `dialog_body` with the number of rows it would delete, plus `dialog_meetings` when whole meetings go and `dialog_in_use` when rows will be left. With nothing to remove it shows `dialog_nothing` and no Remove button. Remove sends the counts it showed; the command scans again under its pass and refuses with kind `changed` (copy `changed`) when it would delete more rows or more whole meetings than were confirmed, writing nothing; the dialog then scans again. Fewer is accepted (a row healed meanwhile).
- **Result.** `result_removed` with the count, plus `result_in_use` when rows were left; with nothing left the damaged notes go (17.7).
- **Sync running.** Remove does not stop the sync thread. Its transaction and a page's are both IMMEDIATE under the store lock, so they never interleave; a page that writes a good copy right after Remove is a heal. The push never reads a damaged row (it is unsendable), so nothing Remove deletes was on its way out.
- **What updates.** The command's result says whether meetings, people or voices changed; the desktop then sends the existing `SyncEvent` (`apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/sync_settings.rs:206`) with the ids of removed meetings whose `id` read and `people: true` when a person, print, sample or speaker row went, so the meeting list, an open meeting and the People page reload as they do after a sync. The Dictionary page needs nothing: `settings/vocabulary` is not touched (17.5 and 17.12 question 4).

### 17.7 Copy and the notes

- **Copy table.** A new `fixtures/ui/damaged_copy_cases.json`, read by both suites (the desktop's tests through a `damaged_copy` module and the UI through `ipc/damagedCopy.ts`, as `people_copy_cases.json` is). Shipped with the app: public copy only, counts as `{count}` with `one` and `many` forms as `refused_note`. Draft copy, to be approved by the owner (17.12 question 5):
  - `remove_button`: "Remove damaged items"
  - `repair_button`: "Repair"
  - `repair_intro`: "Repair reads everything from the sync server again, so damaged items can be replaced by good copies from your other computers. Nothing extra is sent. It can take as long as setting up a new computer."
  - `repair_running`: "Repairing: {percent}% read from the sync server."
  - `repair_done_none`: "Repair finished. Nothing damaged is left on this computer."
  - `repair_done_left`: one "Repair finished. 1 damaged item has no good copy on your other computers. You can remove it.", many "Repair finished. {count} damaged items have no good copy on your other computers. You can remove them."
  - `dialog_title`: "Remove damaged items?"
  - `dialog_checking`: "Checking this computer..."
  - `dialog_body`: one "1 damaged item on this computer will be removed.", many "{count} damaged items on this computer will be removed." Followed always by "They are removed from this computer only. Your other computers keep their copies."
  - `dialog_meetings`: one "This includes 1 meeting that can't be opened, with its recordings.", many "This includes {count} meetings that can't be opened, with their recordings."
  - `dialog_in_use`: one "1 damaged item is still part of a meeting's transcript and will be left.", many "{count} damaged items are still part of a meeting's transcript and will be left."
  - `dialog_try_repair`: "Repair on the Sync page may replace them with good copies from your other computers first."
  - `dialog_nothing`: "Nothing damaged is left on this computer."
  - `confirm`: "Remove", `cancel`: "Cancel"
  - `result_removed`: one "Removed 1 damaged item.", many "Removed {count} damaged items."
  - `result_in_use`: one "1 damaged item was left because a meeting's transcript still uses it.", many "{count} damaged items were left because a meeting's transcript still uses them."
  - `busy`: "Damaged items can't be removed while a meeting is recording or finishing, or a meeting job is running. Try again when it has finished."
  - `changed`: "The damaged items on this computer changed. Check them again."
- `dialog_try_repair` names the Sync page, so it gets a source-pinning case in `page_names_tests.rs` (AGENTS: a note naming a page). No copy names a row, value or meeting (R5).
- **The notes go when nothing damaged is left.** The People page's list note and the Dictionary refusal are computed when read, so they go by themselves. The Sync page's `damaged_note` follows the last tick's `unsendable` and `damaged_skipped` (`sync_settings.rs:745-746`), which with sync off would never be refreshed, and misses damaged rows no tick touches. So a new device record `damaged-left` (`DeviceRecord::DamagedLeft`, `version u32 LE | JSON {count}`) holds the last scan's count, written by every scan (the dialog, Remove's end, a Repair's end); when a scan finds 0 it also sets the stored `sync-status`'s `unsendable` and `damaged_skipped` to 0 (they describe rows that are gone; a later tick that finds new damage sets them again). The note shows when `unsendable`, `damaged_skipped` or `damaged-left` is above 0. A tick that wrote its status from counts taken before Remove can bring the note back for one tick (at most 30 s while sync runs), accepted.

### 17.8 IPC, records and interfaces

- **Commands** (desktop, all `async` plus `ipc::blocking`, since they reach the store or a pass): `damaged_items() -> DamagedItemsView { count: u32, meetings: u32, in_use: u32, repair_offered: bool }`; `remove_damaged_items(confirmed: DamagedConfirm { count: u32, meetings: u32 }) -> DamagedRemovedView { removed: u32, meetings: u32, in_use: u32 }`, errors `busy`, `changed` (new kind, retryable) and `internal`; `repair_sync() -> ()`. `SyncView` gains `repair_percent: Option<u8>` (while `sync-repair` exists) and `repair_left: Option<u64>` (the in-memory result of 17.3.4). Counts and flags only; tests assert on the serialised JSON that no id or name is in any result. `bindings.ts` regenerated with `EARSHOT_UPDATE_BINDINGS=1 cargo test -p earshot-desktop bindings`, with the `Ipc` methods and `createMockIpc` (mock data option `damaged: { count, meetings, in_use }`).
- **Runtime**: `earshot_runtime::damaged` with `scan(services) -> DamagedScan` and `remove(services, confirmed) -> Result<DamagedRemoval, DamagedError>`, both one-line `services.guarded_read` / `guarded_write` wrappers over their roots (16.3); `sync::repair(services)`. Store: `damaged_rows` (17.4.1), `remove_damaged_in` and `remove_meeting_here` in `guarded.rs` (17.9). The sync client: `TickReport.repair_done`, the `reading` mark on the first page from 0.
- **Records** (device-local, no migration): `sync-repair` (17.3.4) and `damaged-left` (17.7). Both counts only.

### 17.9 Guarded paths and the scan test

Remove is a user-started erasure, so it joins R1 (16.2) and the scan test of 16.8: its roots (`damaged::scan_in`, `damaged::remove_in`, store `remove_damaged_in`, `remove_meeting_here`) take guarded types; every statement it runs is a named `const` in `guarded.rs` in `SQL_OWNERS`; its `DELETE ... WHERE rowid` statements (one constant per synced table, since a table name cannot be a parameter) are owned by one named writer, `delete_damaged_by_rowid`, and its meeting statements by `remove_meeting_here`. The scan gains a rule: a constant holding `DELETE FROM` keyed on `rowid` or on a raw `meeting_id`, `voiceprint_id` or `person_id` match is owned only by those two writers (beside `ERASE_BY_ROWID`, T14.26). Mutation controls: such a constant used from a copy of `unlink_speaker_by_rowid` fails; a copy of `remove_damaged_in` that calls `put_speaker` (a synced write) fails, because the scan refuses every `put_*` and by-rowid writer of 16.4 in Remove's roots (Remove writes no synced row, 17.4.2). Repair reads no synced row and is not a guarded root.

### 17.10 Formats and other builds

- No row changes shape and no row is written by Remove: `ENVELOPE_FORMAT` stays 4, no migration, no relay change, no wire field, no `sync_pending` reason.
- Repair uses the existing pull and cursor; a 0.9.0 relay or worker sees ordinary pulls.
- `sync-repair` and `damaged-left` are new device records; an older build ignores them. `sync-status` keeps its shape (17.7 only zeroes two optional fields, left out when 0).
- What another computer holds never changes because of Remove; a Repair changes this computer only.

### 17.11 Tests

Damage is made by SQL in the test as in 16.9; names are invented. Store tests in `crates/earshot-store/tests/damaged_remove.rs` (names start `store_damaged_remove_`), runtime tests `sync_repair_*` and `damaged_*`, desktop tests `damaged_*`. Every test states its control.

1. **Repair heals** (through `LocalRelay`, two computers): A damages a speaker row (`role` bogus) and a segment row that B holds readable; Repair; after A's ticks both read equal to B's, the scan finds 0, `repair_done` is 0 and the Sync page shows `repair_done_none` and no `damaged_note`. Control: the same ticks without Repair leave both damaged.
2. **Repair sends nothing and is not a vault reset**: A's outbox length and the relay's row count are unchanged by the Repair alone; the cursor's `epoch`, `seen_seq` and `resets` are unchanged; no `queue_everything` ran. Control: a local edit made during the Repair is pushed (the push did run), and a changed epoch in the same harness gives a reset.
3. **Repair's end needs a read from 0**: a tick already past 0 when Repair is pressed (its compare-and-set fails) does not end it; the next tick, which fetches `after = 0`, marks `reading`, and the following complete pull ends it and deletes `sync-repair`. A restart in the middle resumes. Control: with the `reading` check removed, the first tick ends it and test 3 fails.
4. **What Repair cannot heal**: a damaged meeting tombstone against B's live copy (7A and T14.26's finality), a damaged voiceprint with voiceprint sync off, and a row never pushed stay damaged and are counted in `repair_done`. Control: with B's copy a tombstone, voiceprint sync on, and the row pushed before its damage, each is healed.
5. **Remove deletes on this computer only and sends nothing**: a damaged segment with no copy anywhere and a damaged note B holds are deleted by rowid; their outbox entries are gone; the next push sends nothing for them; B's rows are byte for byte unchanged; no synced row's `updated_at` changed on A. Control: with the damage removed the scan finds nothing and the rows stay.
6. **Healable after Remove**: after test 5, a Repair brings B's note back to A. Control: without the Repair it stays absent.
7. **A damaged meeting row**: its tracks, speakers, segments, notes, summaries, requests, audio rows and files, export records and device-local records are gone, persons, prints and samples stay; B keeps the meeting; after a Repair the meeting is back on A without audio. Variant: the meeting's `id` is a 15-byte blob: rows and files found by raw `meeting_id` and audio rows go, local records stay (counted). Control: a meeting whose only damaged row is a segment loses only that segment.
8. **In use is left**: a damaged speaker referenced by a segment and a damaged track referenced by an audio row are left, counted `in_use`, and the dialog and result show the in-use lines. Control: the same speaker with no referencing segment is deleted.
9. **Biometric rows are erased from the file**: a damaged person row with prints and samples, a damaged print with samples, and a damaged sample: all deleted, the person's identity entries dropped, and the invented vector bytes are not found in the database file or WAL after `flush_erasures` (a second connection held open across, as the on-disk erasure tests do). Control: another person's voices are byte for byte unchanged, and the search finds the bytes before Remove.
10. **Never while a meeting records or finishes**: Remove is refused `busy` and the store is byte for byte unchanged while a meeting pass is held (recording, then finishing), while a job pass is held and while an after-meeting thread is left; a meeting start while the removal pass is held is refused. Control: with no pass held the same Remove deletes the rows.
11. **Per-row records**: after removing a damaged segment and a damaged summary, the meeting detail, word choices, unknown-line marks, summary-seen and summary-names readers work and show no stale effect. Control: the same readers with the rows readable show their entries.
12. **Confirmation**: a Remove confirmed for fewer rows or meetings than the scan now finds is refused `changed` and writes nothing; equal or more confirmed proceeds. Control: changing only the confirmed count flips the outcome.
13. **The notes go**: with sync set up and off and a stale `sync-status` holding `unsendable` 2, Remove leaves nothing, the status's counts read 0, `damaged-left` is 0 and `SyncView.damaged_note` is `None`; the People list reports no damage; a Dictionary edit refused with `dictionary_damaged` before succeeds after. Control: a Remove refused `busy` leaves the note.
14. **Sync off offers Remove only**: `repair_offered` false and no Repair button with sync off or not set up; `repair_sync` refused. Control: with sync on and set up it is offered and runs.
15. **Counts never content**: every IPC result, `TickReport`, `sync-repair` and `damaged-left` serialised after a run over rows holding invented names contains none. Control: the names are found in the store by the same search before Remove.
16. **Cost figures**: the scan and a Repair over a generated store and vault of a stated size, timed and measured in bytes on an idle host, with the figures written into 17.3.3 and 17.4.1 (informational, gates nothing).
17. **UI** (`SyncPage.test.tsx`, `PeoplePage.test.tsx`, the Dictionary page's tests, a `DamagedItems.test.tsx`): the button sits beside each damaged note and opens one dialog; `dialog_checking`, then the counts with the one and many forms; `dialog_try_repair` only with sync on; Remove calls the command with the shown counts; the result, `busy` and `changed` copy; Repair only with sync on, its progress line and its result. All copy from the table. Control: with no damage none of it renders.

Accept: as T14.62's entry in PLAN.md.

### 17.12 Open questions for the owner

1. **Damaged tombstones are deleted** (17.5). The alternative keeps them as markers so a pulled live copy never brings back what was deleted here, but then they stay damaged and some rules (rule 1 over persons) count them on every page, so the note never goes. Deleting them lets a meeting, voice, person or folder deleted here come back only when that deletion never reached the relay and another computer still holds it live. Proposed: delete.
2. **A damaged track or speaker that a transcript still uses is left** (17.5). Alternatives: remove its whole meeting on this computer, or clear the lines' speaker on this computer (a write to readable rows that would differ from the other computers until those lines change). Proposed: leave and count; Repair is the way to heal it when sync is on.
3. **A damaged meeting row removes the whole meeting, recordings included** (17.5.1), the only copy when this computer never synced it. The alternative is to leave it, but then it cannot be opened, its rows are content the user cannot see or delete, and the meeting list fails on it today (finding below). Proposed: remove, said in the dialog.
4. **A removed Dictionary word row is made again from the list.** `settings/vocabulary` keeps the word (16.5 item 13), so the next reconcile (a tick with Dictionary sync on, or the next Dictionary edit) gives it a new row, which is sent with Dictionary sync on and read as the same word on the other computers (`row_key`). Proposed: accept, since the user never removed the word.
5. **Copy** (17.7) is a draft.

Found while writing, outside this task: the meeting list reads every meeting row whole (`Services::live_meetings_since`, `crates/earshot-runtime/src/services.rs:715`, `db.meetings()`), so one damaged meeting row today fails the whole list with an error rather than a note. Section 16 left the meeting list out of scope (16.5, last paragraph). Remove clears it, but a lenient list belongs in a task of its own.

### 17.13 Revisions

- First draft (2026-11-27), not yet spec-reviewed. Written from T14.62's entry, section 16 as built (16.10a), section 4 (the relay keeps every row), 6.2 and 6.3 (the cursor, `restart_pull_tx`, `queue_everything`), `apply_page`'s handling of a local row that does not read (`guarded.rs:1683-1725`), the store's foreign keys (which decided whole-meeting removal and the in-use rule), `WorkGate` (the removal pass) and the Sync, People and Dictionary pages' notes.
